# Miziki

A local-first music player for iPhone (installed from Safari to the home screen), served from GitHub Pages at
https://esang-mao.github.io/Miziki/miziki.html. It plays FLAC, WAV, ALAC and MP3 files the user picks from their device. Nothing is uploaded. An optional social layer (Supabase) sits on top, and the player must work fully without it.

Long-term plan: modularize the codebase, build out the Supabase social layer, then wrap the app with Capacitor for the App Store.

## Repo layout

- `miziki.html` holds the markup. The main player script used to be one big inline `<script>` block at the end of `<body>`; it now lives in `src/main.js` (step 2a), loaded via `<script type="module" src="src/main.js">` in that same position. It is still one big ~10,100-line file — splitting *that* up is the rest of step 2 and beyond. See "Refactor rules" below. Since step 01 (security hardening), its `<head>` also carries the Content Security Policy and `referrer` meta tags — see "Security" below; GitHub Pages can't set real HTTP response headers, so the CSP has to live here as a `<meta http-equiv>` tag, not a header. Since step D2, `<head>` also loads `src/theme-boot.js` (a classic `<script src>`, placed before the stylesheet `<link>` tags so it blocks parsing and sets the `data-theme` attribute before any CSS applies — see that file's own entry) and carries the Character-menu Appearance row's markup (three `[data-theme-choice]` buttons) in the Character route. Since step D3 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), the tab bar (`<nav id="tabbar">`, unchanged) is wrapped in a new `<div class="sidebar" id="sidebar">`, physically still in the exact same spot right after the mini-player, with new sibling markup inside that same wrapper (`<div class="sidebar-extra" id="sidebarExtra">`: `#sidebarPlaylists`, the playlists list `src/shell/sidebar.js` renders into, and `#sidebarSundown`'s `#sideRateWhy`/`#sideLegStart`/`#sideLegEnd`, which `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` mirrors into) — invisible scaffolding below the 1024px breakpoint (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`'s `.sidebar{display:contents}`), the desktop left sidebar above it. Since step D5, `#libNav` (the Library route's own view-mode tab row) also carries the new desktop-only crate order toggle's markup, `<div class="seg" id="crateGroupToggleTab">` (two `[data-group]` buttons, `az`/`genre`), inserted right before the existing `#hdrActions` — new ids, the same "mirror existing state, don't clone or relocate the control" shape the sidebar's own playlists list and the D4 player bar's controls already established; the existing Profile → Library tools copy, `#crateGroupToggle`, is untouched. Since step D6, the full-player overlay (`#playerOverlay`) gained a new `.player-layout` wrapper around the existing `.stage` (itself gaining a new `#stageInfo` wrapper around six pre-existing, untouched elements — `.now`/`#scrub`/`.times`/`.transport`/`.modes`/`#modeNote`) and a sibling `#playerSidePanel` (three tabs, `#playerSideTabs`, over one body, `#playerSideBody`) — desktop-only, invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint the same way D3's sidebar and D5's crate detail panel already are; no existing id moved, removed, or changed. Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), the tab bar is four buttons, not five — `data-route="character"` is gone, and `#route-sundown` now holds a Day/Sound tablist (`.sd-tabs`, `[data-sundown-view]`) over two sibling views: `#sundownDayView` (the day arc SVG, `#sunDot`/`#arcLabel*`/`#arcTime*`, the phase block `#phaseName`/`#phaseSub`, the existing "Now playing at" row, the existing Sundown/Sunrise controls now under `.sd-heading` labels, the Day-drift shortcut row `#dayDriftSummary`/`#dayDriftLink`, and the 24h "Preview the day" slider `#preview`) and `#sundownSoundView` (the old Character route's content verbatim, minus the Appearance row — see below). `#route-character` no longer exists at all; `src/shell/routes.js`'s `showRoute()` redirects any request for it to `sundown`, selecting Sound. The Appearance row (`[data-theme-choice]`) moved off this route entirely, onto a new Profile → Settings screen (`src/profile/screens.js`'s `renderAppSettingsScreen`) — see that module's own entry and the DA3 changelog entry below for the full reasoning on both moves. Since step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), the desktop sidebar's own Appearance control (`#sidebarAppearance`, D7) gained a fourth button, `<button data-theme-choice="nychthemeron" aria-label="Nychthemeron: follows sunrise and sunset">Nychthemeron</button>` — `src/wiring/character.js`'s existing boot-time `querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` wires it with zero code change there. **RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "a quieter player and library") made four markup changes, all superseding text earlier in this bullet:** `#sidebarAppearance` is gone outright (Profile → Settings is its one home now, per the DA4 sentence just above); the Profile → Library tools copy of crate grouping (`#crateGroupToggle`/`#crateGenreBtn`, mentioned in the step D5 sentence above) is gone, leaving `#crateGroupToggleTab` as the only home, shown on every platform now rather than desktop-only; `#modeNote` (the three-line Vinyl paragraph under the mode toggle, one of the six elements the step D6 sentence above lists as wrapped unchanged into `#stageInfo`) is deleted outright, not just emptied; and the `#libTools` static-markup block (S1 §5) moved from `#route-profile` into `#route-library`, now reached from that route's own new `···` overflow menu (`#hdrOverflowBtn`/`#libOverflowMenu`/`#libOverflowScrim`, holding the relocated `#selectToggle` and a new `#libOverflowToolsBtn`) instead of from the profile home screen — see `src/shell/header.js`'s own entry. The player overlay's `.now-title-row` also gained a `#playerOverflowBtn` (its own popover, `#playerOverflowMenu`/`#playerOverflowScrim`, holding `#editCurrentBtn`/`#playerShareBtn`/`#queueBtn`/`#sleepTimerBtn` — moved off the always-visible row under the title; `#lookBtn` stays where it was) and the mini-player lost `#miniFavBtn` (the player already has `#playerFavBtn`). **RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown, set once") replaced the Day view's Auto/Manual toggle, manual-rate slider, full-night-rate target seg and Window/Moment pacing seg with one `.seg.sd-depth-seg` (`[data-depth]`, Off/Gentle/Medium/Deep) plus a one-line `#depthDesc` caption, and removed the Sunrise heading/`#sunriseNext` row and the Day-drift shortcut row (`#dayDriftSummary`/`#dayDriftLink`) outright** — this note supersedes the DA3 sentence above wherever the two disagree (the pre-existing `.sdv1`/dial-based markup from the separate Day Arc v2 work is untouched by RF9 and stays as it was). In the Sound view, RF9 also removed the second preview dial (`#soundDial` and its own sky-background/hint-row) and wrapped the four character cards plus their fine-tune sliders behind a new `#soundAdvancedToggle`/`#soundAdvancedBody` disclosure. **RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter") supersedes every DA3/RF9-era sentence above describing `#route-sundown` as a route or the tab bar as having a Sundown tab.** The tab bar is down to three buttons — Library/Home/Profile — `data-route="sundown"` is gone outright, not redirected (RF8-era `data-route="character"` had already been removed in DA3). `#route-sundown` no longer exists inside `.wrap` at all; its entire former contents (the Day/Sound `.sd-tabs` block, `#sundownDayView`, `#sundownSoundView`, byte-for-byte unchanged) moved into a new sibling overlay, `#sundownSheetScrim` + `#sundownSheet` (containing `#sundownSheetGrabber`, a `.sundown-sheet-head` with `h2.sundown-sheet-title` and `#sundownSheetClose`, the relocated `.sd-tabs` block, and `#sundownSheetBody` wrapping the two unchanged views), placed as a sibling right before the full-player overlay. `#sidebarSundown` (inside `.sidebar-extra`) changed from a plain `<div>` to a real `<button>` — one of the sheet's four entry points. Three more entry points were added as new markup elsewhere: `#miniSunBtn` (a 44px sun button, sibling of `#miniTap` inside the mini-player), `#sunKnob` (a 64px sun knob with `.sun-knob-ring`/`.sun-knob-glyph`/`#sunKnobTime`, a sibling of `#sleeveOut` inside `#platterStage` — deliberately a sibling, never a child of `#platterBox`/`#disc`, so it can never become part of the Scratch/spin pointer-gesture target `player/scrub.js`'s `wireSpinToScrub()` binds to, satisfying the spec's explicit "the knob never moves the platter" rule), and the Settings → Sundown row (`profile/screens.js`, no new markup of its own — a plain click handler). `#trackSpec` also gained a sibling, `#trackSpecPhase`, for the track-spec line's new phase segment (e.g. "GOLDEN HOUR"). A new stylesheet link, `<link rel="stylesheet" href="/src/styles/26-sundown-sheet.css">`, was added after `25-pull-refresh.css`. See `shell/sundown-sheet.js`'s own entry for the sheet's open/close/drag behavior and `player/clock.js`'s updated `drawSun()` entry for how the new phase-tinted elements are kept live. **RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces", point 1)** rewrote the platter label's own markup: the old `#rpmText`/`#modeTag` pair is gone, replaced by an SVG label-arc (`<svg class="label-arc">`, a `<defs><path id="labelArcPath">` arc with `<text><textPath href="#labelArcPath" id="labelArcText">` for the mono "33.3 RPM · SIDE A · STEREO"-style readout) plus a `.label-body` wrapper holding `.label-title#labelTitle` (the album title, or track title with no album tag) and a `.label-spindle`. `#sideLetter` itself is untouched — it's still the separate, transient side-flip overlay CEREMONY's `shell/runout.js` owns, distinct from this persistent label text. Both new elements only ever show in the label's no-art state (`.label.has-art` hides them — see `02-stage-platter.css`'s own entry below), since album art already carries identity once there's a cover to show.
- `src/main.js` (step 7f) is now the short boot file the "wiring" header always implied it should become — everything else named in this bullet through step 6 has moved out into its own module. It imports just what the boot sequence itself needs, calls each of the 13 `wireX()` functions from `src/wiring/` (below) once, then runs the original startup sequence verbatim: `renderSearchChips()`, the 13 wire calls, `setMode('vinyl')`/`fallbackSun()`/`askLocation()`/`drawSun()`/`updateChromeInset()` plus its `resize` listener, `requestAnimationFrame(frame)`, then `restoreLibrary()` (unchanged, still the boot sequence's own async function — opens storage, loads tracks, restores meta/sessions/seals, rebuilds the people index and crate model, renders the library, heals thumbnails/details/crate art in the background, marks the library ready for the launch intro, sets the initial queue to the last-played album/track, and inits `MizikiSocial`, including the dynamic `?social=demo` import) and the launch-intro gate. Since step 06 (B3), also calls `initBackupSync()` and wires the one `onBackup('applied', ...)` listener (the only place outside `backup.js` itself that reacts to a merged pull — see `sync/backup.js`'s own entry for why `backup.js` can't safely import `renderTracks`/`applyDiscVariant`/`renderProfile` directly): `renderTracks()`, `applyDiscVariant(current(), true)` if a track is loaded, and, since step 07 (B4, for a downloaded cover/avatar to actually show up on the currently-open profile tab), `renderProfile()` if `isProfileRouteVisible()` — all three imported for real (`library/view.js`, `record-art/looks.js`, `profile/screens.js`, `shell/routes.js`) specifically because `main.js` is the one place in the app that can safely hold all of them at once (`REDUCED || document.hidden` skips straight to removing `#launchOverlay`; otherwise `runLaunchSequence()`). A module now, not a classic script: nothing it declares at the top level is a property of `window` (confirmed nothing relies on that — see the step 2a PR). `src/miziki-social.js` stays a classic script loaded just before it, so `MizikiSocial` is already a real global by the time this runs. Since step DA1 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), the boot sequence also calls `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` (`sundown/location.js`) right after the existing `drawSun()` call — the same "set it up once at boot, the function re-arms itself" shape `requestAnimationFrame(frame)` just above it already has, just on a once-a-day cadence instead of once-a-frame.
- `src/wiring/` (step 7f, plus `lifecycle.js` in step 06/B3) — the "wiring" IIFE's own ~430 lines, split into one file per UI area, each exporting a single `wireX()` function that attaches event listeners and nothing else. Every function any of the 13 step-7f files calls was already a real export of its owning module before that step — so, unlike every other step 7 PR, none of them needed a single new export added anywhere else in the codebase, and none of them import from `main.js` (rule 8's list stays exactly as empty as it was after step 7e). Two deliberate groupings, each a judgment call since step 7f had no file-list table to follow (the step 7 plan only names the area as a whole): `account.js` combines the profile-picture picker with the social sign-in/nav-stack close buttons, since both are small "identity" concerns and neither is big enough alone to warrant its own file; `routes.js` is kept separate from `library-view.js` (rather than folded in, even though both are tab-switching) since the top-level route tab bar is app-wide navigation, not a library-screen concern. The global Escape-key handler (gatefold → sealed sheet → add menu → search field, in that priority order) lives in `gatefold.js` rather than its own file, since the gatefold and sealed sheet are the first two things it closes.
  - `lifecycle.js` (step 06, B3) — `wireLifecycle()`: the one new wiring file this step adds, since it's a new UI-adjacent concern (backgrounding/foregrounding), not an extraction from `main.js`. Listens for `visibilitychange` (hidden → `flushBackupSync()`, so a backgrounded or closed tab doesn't lose up to 30s of unsynced changes; visible again → `pullBackupOnForeground()`, so a device picks up what another device backed up while this one was away) and `pagehide` (`flushBackupSync()` again, as a second safety net — iOS Safari fires `pagehide`, not always a reliable `visibilitychange`, when a home-screen app is actually terminated). Imports `flushBackupSync`/`pullBackupOnForeground` from `../sync/backup.js`, and, since step DA1 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `computeSun` (`../sundown/solar.js`)/`drawSun` (`../player/clock.js`) — no `main.js` import. All four calls are fire-and-forget: none is awaited, and none can block or throw into the event loop (see `src/sync/backup.js`'s own entry below for why that's safe for the first two). The visible-branch addition — `computeSun(); drawSun();`, right alongside the existing `pullBackupOnForeground()` — is the other half of DA1's "don't trust a stale day model" fix: `sundown/location.js`'s own `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` handles a session left open and *visible* across midnight; this handles a session that was *backgrounded* across midnight (or across a timezone change — flying while the app is in the background, say) and never had that timer actually fire while hidden, the same "returning to foreground means re-check what might have gone stale while away" reasoning this handler already applied to the backup pull.
  - `transport.js` — `wireTransport()`: play/pause (including the `bgPrime()`/`ensureContext()`/pull-ceremony logic `#play` and `#miniPlay` both run), next/prev, shuffle/repeat, the scrub slider, `wireSpinToScrub()`, the Pure/Vinyl mode buttons, the sleep timer open/close, the player's favorite button, the `visibilitychange` listener that calls `touchActiveSession()` on backgrounding, the mini-player tap-to-open, and the player-overlay pointerdown that interrupts whichever in-flight ceremony (start sequence, morph, gatefold hand-off) is running. Imports `S`/`current`, `$`, `bgPrime` (`player/background.js`), `ensureContext` (`audio/engine.js`), `pause`/`play`/`seek` (`player/transport.js`), `nextTrack`/`prevTrack`/`toggleShuffle`/`cycleRepeat` (`player/controls.js`), `setMode` (`audio/mode.js`), `queueSave` (`storage/meta.js`), `wireSpinToScrub` (`player/scrub.js`), `openSleepSheet`/`closeSleepSheet` (`player/sleep-timer.js`), `toggleFavorite` (`library/favorites.js`), `touchActiveSession` (`history/sessions.js`), `updateShellAlbum` (`shell/mini-player.js`), `openPlayer`/`morphInFlight`/`interruptMorph`/`openPlayerOverflowMenu`/`isPlayerOverflowMenuOpen` (`shell/player-overlay.js`), `runStartSequence`/`interruptStartSequence` (`shell/start-sequence.js`), `gfHandoffInFlight`/`interruptGatefoldHandoff` (`shell/handoff.js`), `closeLayer`/`forceCloseLayer` (`shell/nav-stack.js`) — no `main.js` import. Since step D6 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), also wires the desktop-only side panel's three tabs (`#playerSideTabs [data-panel-tab]`): each click flips `aria-selected` on the clicked button and calls `renderPlayerSidePanel` (`shell/player-side-panel.js`), plus one initial call at the end of `wireTransport()` itself, the same "initial call" shape `wireCharacter()`'s own `bgUpdateUI()` call already has. Since RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), also wires the player's own `···` overflow (`#playerOverflowBtn`/`#playerOverflowScrim`, and every row inside it, since none of those rows' own click handlers — wired in `edit.js`/`share.js`/`overlays.js`, and `#sleepTimerBtn` here — know about the menu, so each one also force-closes it); `#playerDismiss` moved to a bare `closeLayer('player')` call (the raw `closePlayer` import was dropped) since there's nothing left to force-close separately. The mini-player's own favorite button (`#miniFavBtn`) is gone — one favorite button now, the player's own.
  - `library-view.js` — `wireLibraryView()`: the Songs/Artists/Playlists/Crate view-mode tabs, the library layout toggle, the crate group toggle, the album-sides/sealed-records/play-in-full-player toggles, the library tools disclosure panel (S1 §5), select mode (toggle/cancel/edit/number/delete), the row overflow menu, and the shared confirm-delete dialog. Imports `S`, `$`, `idxOf` (`library/model.js`), `queueSave` (`storage/meta.js`), `invalidateCrateModel` (`crate/model.js`), `renderTracks`/`enterSelectMode`/`exitSelectMode`/`closeRowMenu`/`openConfirmDelete`/`closeConfirmDelete`/`rowMenuTrackId`/`confirmDeleteAction`/`applyTrackNumbersInOrder` (`library/view.js`), `deleteTracks` (`library/delete.js`), `openEditSheet` (`library/edit.js`), `openCreditsSheet` (`library/credits.js`), `toggleFavorite` (`library/favorites.js`), `playNext`/`addToQueue` (`player/queue.js`) — no `main.js` import. Since step D5, the crate group toggle's click wiring targeted `#crateGroupToggleTab [data-group]` and `#crateGroupToggle [data-group]` in one combined selector; **RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "one home per control") removed the `#crateGroupToggle` half** (the Profile → Library tools copy) outright, so this is now a single selector on `#crateGroupToggleTab [data-group]` alone. RF8 also added the library header's own `···` overflow (`#hdrOverflowBtn`/`#libOverflowScrim`, and `#libOverflowToolsBtn`, which force-closes the menu, opens the Library tools disclosure if it isn't already open, and scrolls `#libTools` into view — imports `openLibOverflowMenu`/`isLibOverflowMenuOpen` from `shell/header.js` and `closeLayer`/`forceCloseLayer` from `shell/nav-stack.js`) and moved `#selectToggle`'s own click handler to also force-close that menu first, since Select is now one of its rows.
  - `search.js` — `wireSearch()`: the library search field's own input/focus/blur handling (the 120ms debounce into `renderTracks`) and the header search icon/Cancel/× (S1 §2.1).   - `gatefold.js` — `wireGatefold()`: the global Escape-key handler described above, plus the gatefold's own back/panel-switch/resize controls and the sealed-record sheet's close button. Imports `$`, `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefold`/`gatefoldSwitchPanel`/`updateGatefoldScale` (`gatefold/view.js`), `isSealedSheetOpen`/`closeSealedSheet` (`gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`), `isAddMenuOpen`/`closeAddMenu`/`isSearchFieldOpen`/`closeSearchField` (`shell/header.js`), `renderTracks` (`library/view.js`) — no `main.js` import.
  - `sundown.js` — `wireSundown()`: the Day/Sound tab buttons and the dial gesture wiring (`wireDialGesture()`, `sundown/dial-gesture.js`). **RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown, set once") replaced this file's old auto/manual toggle, manual-rate slider, full-night-rate target buttons and pacing buttons with a single `[data-depth]` seg** (Off/Gentle/Medium/Deep): a click sets `S.auto`/`S.target` from `DEPTH_TARGETS` (or `S.auto = false` for Off), persists via `queueSave()`, and repaints every `[data-depth]` button's `aria-pressed` plus `#depthDesc`'s caption via the new `paintDepth()` helper, which calls `depthKeyFor`/`depthCaptionFor` (`sundown/solar.js`) — the same two pure functions `storage/meta.js`'s restore-time sync calls, so there's one source of truth for "which button is pressed and what it says." Imports `S`, `$`, `queueSave` (`storage/meta.js`), `DEPTH_TARGETS`/`depthKeyFor`/`depthCaptionFor` (`sundown/solar.js`), `wireDialGesture` (`sundown/dial-gesture.js`) — no `main.js` import.   - `routes.js` — `wireRoutes()`: the one-line route tab bar — see the grouping rationale above. Imports `showRoute` (`shell/routes.js`) — no `main.js` import.
  - `pull-refresh.js` (NV4, `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`) — `wirePullRefresh()`: wires `shell/pull-refresh.js`'s gesture onto the Home and Profile routes, each with its own `onRefresh`/`getStatusLine`. Since RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 4), the two routes diverge in how their keyboard "Refresh" trigger is reached: Home (which has no settings gear) still gets a real, in-flow button via a new `buildRouteRefreshButton(routeEl, runRefresh)` helper (a `.route-refresh-btn`, visually hidden until focus/hover, placed in the header's own flow rather than absolutely positioned over the route — RF11's own "keep NV4's button but in the header's flow" instruction); Profile instead calls `registerProfileRefresh(ctrl.refresh)` (`shell/pull-refresh.js`) so the Profile Settings menu's own "Refresh" item (`profile/storefront.js`) can reach the same refresh closure without this file owning any Profile-route button at all.
- `src/state.js` exports `S` (the one app-state object — audio context, tracks, queue, mode, character settings, sessions, etc.; `S.scrubbing` was a top-level `let` in `main.js` until step 3c, see rule 7), `current()` (`S.tracks[S.index] || null` — moved here in step 3b since nearly every part of the app calls it), plus `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL` and `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`. The foundation every later extraction imports from. Since step 06 (B3), also the local state the backup-doc prerequisites called for back in step 03 (see "Backup: what gets synced" below for the full picture): `S.deviceId`/`S.deviceLabel` (this install's own random id/label, generated once and persisted — the `library_backup` row key), `S.historyClearedAt` (stamped by a full-scope "Clear listening history," `src/history/clear.js`), `S.sealedBroken` (a `Set` of album ids ever sealed-then-broken on this device — the durable signal `mergeSealed()`'s "broken anywhere stays broken" rule needs, since `S.sealed` alone can't tell "broken" apart from "never had this album"), `S.backupUpdatedAt` (one stamp per per-field-last-write-wins key — `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/`profileName`/**`picture`** (step 07)), and `S.backupRestoredFor` (guards the first-sign-in restore summary from firing twice for the same account). `touchBackupField(name)`, also exported here rather than from `storage/meta.js` (where it would otherwise belong next to `saveMeta`) specifically to avoid import-chain fan-out — every one of its call sites (`library/view.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/thumbs.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `profile/screens.js`, `sync/backup.js` (step 07, for `picture`), plus `storage/meta.js` itself for `prefs`) already imports `S` from this same file, so routing it through here adds zero new edges. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), it no longer just stamps `Date.now()`: also new is `S.backupSeq` (a Lamport logical clock, device-only like `deviceId`/`deviceLabel` — never backed up, restored from `meta` the same way, `src/storage/meta.js`'s `restoreMeta()`), which `touchBackupField(name)` always increments first, writing `S.backupUpdatedAt[name] = {seq: S.backupSeq, at: Date.now()}` instead of a bare timestamp — a device's own edit made after it has observed another device's stamp (which bumps `S.backupSeq` to at least that stamp's own `seq` on every pull, `src/sync/backup.js`'s `pullAndMergeBackup()`) is guaranteed a strictly higher `seq` than what it saw, regardless of either device's wall clock; `at` survives only as `src/sync/merge.js`'s `lastWriteWins()`'s tie-break for two edits that never saw each other. Since step 07 (B4), `S.profile` also carries `pictureHash` (the re-encoded profile picture's own SHA-256, set by `sync/backup.js`'s `queueAvatarUpload`/`queueAvatarDownload` — `null` until a picture has ever been uploaded or downloaded on this device). Since step D2 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), also `S.theme` (`'auto'`/`'light'`/`'dark'`, default `'auto'` — saved via `storage/prefs.js` like any other setting, but device-only: `sync/backup.js`'s `buildLocalState()` strips it from the uploaded `prefs` object before it ever reaches the backup doc, per the "Backup: what gets synced" table below). Since step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `S.theme` also takes the value `'nychthemeron'` — no new field, the same existing string setting just gaining a fourth allowed value, resolved live by `sundown/solar.js`'s `currentDayPosition()` (via `src/ui/theme.js`'s `effectiveTheme()`) rather than stored as a separate light/dark flag anywhere. Since step DA1 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `S.sun` gained three fields alongside its pre-existing `lat`/`lon`/`set`/`dusk`/`ok`: `dawn`/`rise`/`noon` (the day model's own civil-dawn/sunrise/solar-noon events, filled by `computeSun()`/`fallbackSun()` the same way `set`/`dusk` always were) and `altitudeAtNoon` (the sun's own highest altitude that day, in degrees — `null` whenever it isn't known, e.g. the fixed-time fallback; used only by `dayPosition()`'s polar-day/polar-night fallback). None of these are separately saved/restored prefs — `S.sun` is recomputed every session from `computeSun()`/`fallbackSun()`, never persisted, so there's no `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs`/`prefs.test.js` entry for any of them. Since step DA5, three more fields: `S.phasePlayCounts` (a flat `{phase: count}` map) and `S.albumPhaseCounts` (one `{phase: count}` map per `albumKey()`) — both backed-up, per-device counters, the same class as `trackPlayCounts`, written by `history/played.js`'s `incrementTrackPlay()` and counted by the real clock only (`sundown/solar.js`'s `realDayPosition()`, never `S.previewMin`) — and `S.rhythmPushedAt` (device-only sync bookkeeping, never backed up, never shown to the user — the last time this device actually pushed the *shared* rhythm snapshot, `sync/backup.js`'s own job). Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), also `S.labelSideFormat` (runtime-only, never saved/restored/backed up) — the platter label's own cached "SIDE A · STEREO"-style arc-text half, set by `player/transport.js`'s `load()` and read every tick by `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` when it writes the full `#labelArcText` string (RPM plus this cached half), so the per-tick render loop never has to recompute the side/format split itself. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob"), `S.backupUpdatedAt`'s six map-shaped keys (`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/`playlistDefs`/`playlistOrder` — every field except `profileName`/`picture`) switched from one whole-field `{seq, at}` stamp to a map of `{key: {seq, at}}`, one stamp per item/pref-name/playlist-name, since the whole point of RF17 is that a change to one key can no longer be stamped (or merged) as a change to the entire field. `touchBackupField(name)` is kept, but now only for the two fields that are still genuinely single-valued (`profileName`, `picture`) — every other call site moved to one of two new exports: `touchBackupKey(name, key)` (bumps `S.backupSeq` and writes `S.backupUpdatedAt[name][key] = {seq, at}` — same Lamport-then-wall-clock shape RF16 already established, just addressed by key now) and `touchBackupMembership(playlistName, localId, kind)` (`kind` is `'added'` or `'removed'` — writes into the new `S.playlistMembershipAt` map, keyed by `playlistName + '\u0000' + localId`, each entry holding its own independent `added`/`removed` stamp pair; this is playlist membership's own add-wins CRDT state, point 2's "a tombstone per (playlist, identityKey)," kept separate from `S.backupUpdatedAt.playlistOrder`/`.playlistDefs` since membership and order/naming are merged by entirely different rules). `S.playlistMembershipAt` is a new top-level field, persisted/restored by `storage/meta.js` alongside `backupUpdatedAt`/`backupSeq`, synced (never applied back from a merge — composed at apply time instead, see `sync/backup.js`'s own entry) the same way. Since RF18 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "pushes that can't go backwards"), also `S.backupPushSeq` — a separate, simpler per-device counter, not to be confused with `S.backupSeq` above (that one stamps individual *fields inside* the doc; this one orders successive *whole-doc pushes* of the same device's row against each other, so a late, stale retry can never overwrite a newer push from the same device). Device-only, persisted/restored via `meta` the same way as `backupSeq`/`deviceId`; bumped and written *before* each network attempt by `sync/backup.js`'s `nextBackupPushSeq()`, sent as `put_library_backup()`'s `p_seq` argument, and checked server-side by that RPC's own `where library_backup.doc_seq < excluded.doc_seq` clause (`supabase/migrations/013_backup_seq.sql`) — see that file's and `backup.js`'s own entries for the full mechanism.
- `src/storage/idb.js` exports `DB` (step 4a) — the raw IndexedDB wrapper: `open`, `put`, `get`, `all`, `clear`, `del`, moved out of `main.js`'s "persistence" section unchanged. Database name `miziki`, version 4, the eight stores and their key paths, the non-destructive `onupgradeneeded`, the 4-second open timeout and the `onblocked` handling are all exactly as they were. `main.js` still calls `DB.put`/`DB.get`/etc. directly about 100 times, and `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` still receives it directly too — both unchanged for now; replacing the ~100 direct calls with a named interface (and commenting the one adapter call site that's allowed to keep using `DB` directly) is step 4c. No `onversionchange` handler yet — a known gap, see "Persistence rules" below.
- `src/storage/prefs.js` exports two pure functions (step 4b): `serializePrefs(S)`, the exact object `saveMeta()` has always written under `meta`/`prefs`, and `parsePrefs(raw)`, which returns a clean object containing only the fields that pass the same checks `applyPrefs()` has always made inline (same type checks, same allowed values — see "Persistence rules" below) — anything that fails a check, or is simply missing, is left out. `applyPrefs()` takes `parsePrefs(raw)`'s output instead of the raw saved object; `saveMeta()` writes `serializePrefs(S)`. Splitting it this way makes the one part that matters for data safety — does an old or malformed saved object still load correctly — testable without a DOM. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 4): `prefs` is now merged per key (`src/sync/merge.js`'s `mergeKeyedLastWriteWins`), which means an older build that doesn't recognize some newer device's pref key simply never contributes a competing stamp for it on merge — but without extra help, that same older build would still *drop* the key the instant it next calls `serializePrefs(S)`, since `S` never held it in memory in the first place, discarding data from a field it was never asked to touch. A new module-level `KNOWN_PREF_KEYS` (a `Set` of every key the object literal below writes — kept in sync with that literal by hand, per this file's own top-of-file rule) and exported `rememberUnknownPrefsKeys(raw)` fix this: `src/sync/backup.js`'s `applyPrefsPatch()` calls `rememberUnknownPrefsKeys(mergedPrefs)` on every pull, *before* `parsePrefs()` ever filters the merged object down to what this build understands, caching whatever keys aren't in `KNOWN_PREF_KEYS` (or clearing the cache to `null` if there are none); `serializePrefs(S)` then `Object.assign`s that cache onto its own return value, so every subsequent local save echoes the unrecognized key back verbatim until a build that does understand it changes the value for real (at which point a later pull's own merged object simply stops marking it unknown). `parsePrefs()` itself is unchanged by this — it still drops anything it doesn't recognize, same as always, since reading an unknown key back into `S` was never the problem RF17 needed solved; only the round trip through an old build's own next save needed fixing.
- `src/storage/repo.js` (step 4c) — the named storage interface that replaced main.js's ~100 direct `DB.*` calls: one small object per store (`tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `achievements`, `collection`, `overlays`, `artwork`, `profile`), each with only the methods something in `main.js` actually calls, plus `storage.available()`/`storage.open()` replacing direct reads of `DB.ok` and the one `DB.open()` call. `meta.get(key)` returns the bare stored value instead of the `{k, v}` row every call site used to unwrap by hand; `meta.thumbs()` is the one place `main.js` scans all of `meta` rather than reading by key (artwork thumbnails), so it gets its own named method instead of a generic `all()`. `grep -n "DB\." src/main.js` finds only the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites (commented, since the adapter's own signature expects `DB`, not this interface) — no other direct `DB.*` call remains in `main.js`. A note at the bottom of the file (not built) records the App Store plan: blobs (`tracks.blob`, artwork blobs, `meta`'s `thumb:` blobs) would move to files on the device behind this same module, with callers unchanged.
- `src/storage/meta.js` (step 7a) — the "persistence" section: `queueSave` (exported; debounces `saveMeta` 600ms), the module-private `saveTimer`/`saveMeta`, `restoreMeta` (exported — the boot sequence's one caller) and `applyPrefs` (module-private, `restoreMeta`'s one caller). Since step 02 (F3 spec), `S.playlists[].items`/`S.albumOrder[album]` are themselves arrays of track ids (not positions — see `library/model.js`'s own entry), so `saveMeta`/`restoreMeta` write and read them straight through with no `idOf`/`idxOf` conversion at all any more; `restoreMeta` no longer drops an entry whose track is currently missing (that was the write-back bug step 5d flagged and this step fixed), and `library/model.js`'s `idOf`/`idxOf` imports were dropped from this file entirely as a result. `saveMeta`/`restoreMeta` read/write `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`storage/prefs.js`) for everything else, unchanged. `applyPrefs` keeps every one of its UI calls in the same order, now importing each from the module that owns it: `setMode` (`audio/mode.js`), `applyCharacter` (`audio/engine.js`), `applyVolume` (`sundown/location.js`), `toggleShuffle`/`cycleRepeat` (`player/controls.js`), `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` (`player/background.js`) — all real — plus `attachLaunchSettingRow` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; the launch-intro section hasn't moved yet). Since step D2, `applyPrefs` also sets `S.theme` from a valid saved value and calls `applyTheme()` (`ui/theme.js`) unconditionally at the end — unconditionally so a restore with no saved `theme` at all (every device before D2) still re-applies the default (Auto) rather than leaving whatever `main.js`'s own boot-time call left in place stale. **Step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`) added `'nychthemeron'` to `applyPrefs`'s own copy of the theme enum check** — a real bug caught in this step's own browser verification, not by any unit test: `storage/prefs.js`'s `parsePrefs()` already accepted the value (confirmed by `prefs.test.js`'s own round-trip test), but `applyPrefs()` keeps an independent re-check of `p.theme` rather than trusting `parsePrefs()`'s filtering, and that second check had been missed — a saved Nychthemeron choice restored as Auto on every reload until fixed, since `main.js`'s own unconditional, synchronous boot-time `applyTheme()` call (line 81, before `restoreLibrary()`/`restoreMeta()` has even started) runs with `S.theme` still at its module default, and `applyPrefs()` is the only thing that later corrects it. `tests/unit/meta.test.js` gained a dedicated regression test for exactly this (`restoreMeta()` actually lands `'nychthemeron'` in `S.theme`), since the existing round-trip test only exercised `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`, never `theme` itself. `ui/theme.js` imports `queueSave` back from here for `setTheme()`'s own save — a real circular pair, the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. `queueSave` is the one name six other modules used to reach circularly through `main.js` (`player/background.js`, `sundown/location.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/import.js`) — all six now import it from here directly instead, each dropping (or shrinking) its own rule-8 entry. The stale "profile tab" / S5 / thumbnails / achievements / duplicate-scan leftover comments that used to sit in `main.js` just above the old persistence header were removed outright rather than moved again — each was pure documentation of code already relocated in steps 5c/5d/6b, and every destination module already carries its own copy of the relevant note. Since RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), `applyPrefs`'s old `[data-target]`/`[data-pace]` DOM sync is gone, replaced by a call to `depthKeyFor`/`depthCaptionFor` (`sundown/solar.js`) to sync every `[data-depth]` button's `aria-pressed` and `#depthDesc`'s caption from the restored `S.auto`/`S.target` — the same pure mapping `src/wiring/sundown.js`'s click handler uses — plus a call to the new `updateToneCurveVisibility()` (`sundown/dial-view.js`) to restore the Sound view's Tone-today chart's drift-gated visibility. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), `saveMeta()` also persists `S.backupSeq` (`meta.put('backupSeq', S.backupSeq)`, right after `backupUpdatedAt`, since `touchBackupField('prefs')` just above it may have bumped it) and `restoreMeta()` restores it back (`Object.assign`-style, mirroring how `backupUpdatedAt` is restored right above it) — see `state.js`'s own `S.backupSeq`/`touchBackupField()` entry for what it's for. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 3): `prefs` moved to per-key last-write-wins the same way the album-keyed maps did, but unlike those, nothing in the app calls `touchBackupKey('prefs', key)` at each individual setting's own control — that would mean touching dozens of call sites for one pref apiece. Instead this module (already the one place that serializes the whole prefs blob on every save) computes the per-key stamps itself: a new module-private `lastSavedPrefs` (seeded from whatever `restoreMeta()` actually loaded, so the very first post-launch `saveMeta()` only stamps keys that genuinely changed from the restored state, not every key as if all were new) and `touchChangedPrefKeys(nextPrefs)`, which diffs `nextPrefs` against `lastSavedPrefs` key by key (`JSON.stringify` comparison, since several pref values — `char`, for instance — are themselves small objects) and calls `touchBackupKey('prefs', k)` only for the keys that actually differ, before updating `lastSavedPrefs` to the new blob. `saveMeta()` calls `touchChangedPrefKeys(nextPrefs)` right after writing the new `prefs` blob to `meta`. `restoreMeta()` also now persists/restores `S.playlistMembershipAt` (`meta.put`/`meta.get('playlistMembershipAt')`, `Object.assign`-style like `backupUpdatedAt`) — point 2's add-wins playlist-membership stamps, which need to survive a reload exactly like every other backup-bookkeeping field on `S` already does.
- `src/shell/` (step 7b) — the navigation shell: the mini-player, the full-player overlay and its two ways in, the start/put-away ceremonies, and the gatefold-to-player hand-off. Together these are the most iOS-gesture-sensitive code in the app outside `player/transport.js` itself (rule 9) — every move below was checked against the pristine original lines with no `await` added, removed or reordered.
  - `nav-stack.js` (NV2, `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`) — the single history-based layer stack every overlay/sheet in the app registers with: `openLayer(id, close, opts)`, `closeLayer(id, opts)`, `forceCloseLayer(id, opts)` (the instant, non-animated teardown twin, for when something other than the user dismissing the layer invalidates it), `idsToClose` (pure, unit-tested — the Back/popstate diff helper), `recordRoute`/`onRouteRestore` (the tab-switch Back exception), `hasOpenLayer()` (NV4's own "pull-to-refresh must never start while a layer is open" check), `initNavStack(root)`. The background (everything except the topmost open layer) goes `inert` via `applyInert()`/`inertSiblingsOnPathTo()` — marking every *sibling* along the path from `root` down to the layer's own container(s), never an ancestor, since `inert` can't be un-set once an ancestor already carries it. **`opts.container` accepts either a single element or an array of them** — most layers are one element (the full player, the gatefold, every `.edit-overlay`/`.share-overlay`, each already its own single container doubling as its own backdrop), but a layer can be made of more than one separate top-level sibling that all need to stay reachable together, which `shell/sundown-sheet.js` is the first (and so far only) layer to need: its own scrim (`#sundownSheetScrim`) is a sibling of the sheet, not a parent of it. A real bug, found after RF26 shipped and fixed in this step: passing only the sheet as `container` left the scrim marked `inert` as "just another background sibling," which silently disabled the scrim's own `.open{pointer-events:auto}` CSS rule (`inert`'s UA style always wins over author CSS with no way for a child selector to override it), so tapping the dimmed backdrop to dismiss the sheet stopped doing anything — confirmed with Playwright using real synthesized taps (`locator.tap()`/`mouse.down()+up()`), not `element.click()`, since a programmatic `.click()` bypasses real hit-testing and pointer-events entirely and would never have shown the regression. `inertSiblingsOnPathTo()` now builds one combined "protected" set (every given target plus all of their ancestors up to `root`) before walking each target's own path and inerting only the siblings that aren't in that set — so two containers that happen to share part of their ancestor chain don't fight each other or get double-inerted. `tests/unit/nav-stack.test.js` has a dedicated regression test for exactly this shape (an array container keeping every one of its elements reachable, not just the first). **`finishClose(closing, opts)` also takes `opts.skipHistory`** (a second bug fix, found investigating a report that playing a track from inside an open gatefold stopped handing off into the full player): `history.go(-n)` is asynchronous — its own popstate fires on a later task, not synchronously — so when `forceCloseLayer()` closes one layer and, in that same synchronous tick, a different layer is opened in its place (never nested inside it) via `openLayer()`, that `openLayer()`'s own `pushState` advances the browser's real history position before the first call's queued `go()` actually runs; the stale `go()` then counts back from that new position instead of the one it was computed against, lands on the old layer's own now-stale entry, and the resulting popstate incorrectly closes the layer that just replaced it. `opts.skipHistory` skips the `go()` call entirely for exactly this "replace, not nest" case, leaving one harmless, never-revisited extra entry in the browser's real history (a first Back from the new layer still closes it correctly, landing one entry early with nothing to close there, so a second Back behaves exactly as if that entry had never existed). Used by both of the gatefold-to-player hand-off's two code paths — see `src/shell/handoff.js`'s and `src/gatefold/view.js`'s own entries below. No imports of its own — just `document`/`history`/`window`, stubbed in its own unit test file.
  - `mini-player.js` — "navigation shell: mini-player, full-player overlay, sleeve pull"'s own data and render step: `SLEEVE_ANIM` (the animation-timing constants, plus its `--sheet-duration`-style CSS custom-property side effects, run once at module load exactly as before), `renderMiniPlayer()`, `updateChromeInset()` (exported — the boot sequence's wiring calls it directly, and again on `resize`), `updateShellAlbum(t)`. `chromeInsetRAF`/`shellAlbumIdentity(t)` stay module-private. `GATEFOLD_ANIM` stayed in `main.js` on purpose — it's the gatefold's own open/close timing, physically interleaved with `SLEEVE_ANIM` in the original file but not mini-player/overlay code, and the gatefold itself hasn't moved yet (step 7e). Imports `S`/`current`, `$`, `albumKey`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `bgSync` (`player/background.js`), `isLibraryRouteVisible` (`routes.js`, real), plus `applyCrateMiniInset`/`centerEmptyState` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; both used only inside `updateChromeInset`'s existing `typeof === 'function'` guards, unchanged). Since step D3 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), also imports `isDesktopShell` (`util/viewport.js`) — `updateChromeInset()` treats the tab bar's own `offsetHeight` as `0` on desktop, since it's a full-viewport-height vertical sidebar there (not bottom chrome at all), not the real measurement `--chrome-inset` needs; without this guard the property would be inflated to roughly the sidebar's own height. `--chrome-inset` itself is moot on desktop regardless — `src/styles/23-desktop.css` overrides `.wrap`'s whole `padding` property there and never reads it — but a small, accurate fallback number beats a wildly wrong one sitting in a custom property nothing happens to consume today. Since step D4, `renderMiniPlayer()`'s own show/hide logic (unchanged) is what makes the strip real on desktop too — the D3-era `.mini-player{display:none !important}` placeholder is gone, replaced by `23-desktop.css`'s real 88px player-bar layout — so `updateChromeInset()`'s `miniH` measurement, and `applyCrateMiniInset()`'s own identical one (`crate/view.js`), now correctly pick up the bar's real height at desktop widths whenever a track is loaded, with no code change needed in either function for that to happen.
  - `sidebar.js` (step D3, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) — the desktop sidebar's own new content: `renderSidebarPlaylists()`, the playlists list the mockup's sidebar shows and the phone layout has never needed. Built every time `renderTracks()` runs (`library/view.js`'s own existing "every `S.playlists` mutation already lands here" hub — see that module's entry) rather than from a second call site at each of `S.playlists`' own mutation points; hidden below the 1024px breakpoint entirely by CSS (`styles/23-desktop.css`), not a JS breakpoint check, so the DOM exists on every viewport, just invisible on a phone, the same way the tab bar's own buttons are shared between the tab bar and the sidebar through pure CSS reflow. Pins a system playlist (Favorites) first, the same order `library/view.js`'s own Playlists tab already uses. The sidebar's other new piece, the Sundown summary card, has no render function of its own here — see `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` entry for why. **Since RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter"), `#sidebarSundown` is a real `<button>` (was a plain `<div>`), one of the sheet's four entry points** — its click wiring lives in `shell/sundown-sheet.js`'s `wireSundownSheetEntryPoints()`, not here; this file's own job (building the summary card's content) is unchanged. Imports `S`, `$`/`el` — no `main.js` import. Playlist names are user-typed, not a file's own embedded tag, but the same tag-injection rule (CLAUDE.md's "Security" section) applies regardless — built with `el()`/`textContent`, never `innerHTML`; `tests/unit/sidebar.test.js` feeds the doc's own required `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` payload as a playlist name and confirms it survives as a single `textContent` string, never split or consumed as markup.
  - `routes.js` — the routing half of "navigation shell: header actions, search field, add menu": `isLibraryRouteVisible()`, `isProfileRouteVisible()`, `showRoute(name)`. Imports `$`, `renderProfile` (`profile/screens.js`, real), `renderHome` (`social/screens/home.js`, real), `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`/`closeSearchField`/`closeAddMenu` (`header.js`, a real circular pair — `header.js` imports `isLibraryRouteVisible`/`isProfileRouteVisible` back), plus `GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; `GF` is read-only here, per rule 7 — `showRoute` never reassigns it, only reads it). Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also exports `selectSundownView(view)`/`lastSundownView()` — the Sundown route's own Day/Sound view-switch state, kept here rather than in `src/wiring/sundown.js` specifically so a shared helper could call it directly without a wiring file ever being imported from a shell/ module (wiring imports from shell/, never the reverse). `selectSundownView()` toggles `[data-sundown-view]`'s own `aria-selected` and `#sundownDayView`/`#sundownSoundView`'s visibility, and mirrors the choice to `localStorage` (try/catch-wrapped, device-only — never part of `prefs`/backup, the same posture `S.theme`'s own `localStorage` mirror, `src/theme-boot.js`, already has) so it's remembered per device across reloads, defaulting to Day; `src/wiring/sundown.js` imports both back from here for its own Day/Sound tab click handlers and its one-time "restore last view" call at boot — a real, one-directional import (wiring → shell/, not the other way), not a circular pair. **RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter") supersedes the rest of this entry's older DA3-era description**: `#route-sundown` is no longer a route at all (it's gone from `miziki.html`'s `.wrap` entirely, replaced by the `#sundownSheet` overlay — see `sundown-sheet.js`'s own entry below), and the tab bar itself is down to three tabs (Library/Home/Profile — `data-route="sundown"` was removed outright, not redirected). `showRoute(name)` now opens the sheet instead of switching to a `.route` element for either of the two old names: `if(name === 'sundown'){ openSundownSheet('day'); return; }` / `if(name === 'character'){ openSundownSheet('sound'); return; }` — both early returns, leaving whichever `.route` was already visible untouched (there's no "redirect to the sundown route" step left to do, since that route doesn't exist). This makes `routes.js` and the new `shell/sundown-sheet.js` a real circular pair (per rule 8, the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`): `routes.js` imports `openSundownSheet` from `sundown-sheet.js`, which imports `selectSundownView`/`lastSundownView` back from here.
  - `header.js` — the rest of "navigation shell: header actions, search field, add menu": `updateHeaderActionsAvailability()`, `updateLibTabsForLayout()`, `refreshLibraryTools()`, the header search field (`openSearchField`/`closeSearchField`/`isSearchFieldOpen`), the header `+` menu (`openAddMenu`/`closeAddMenu`/`isAddMenuOpen`) and, since RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), the library header's own `···` overflow (`openLibOverflowMenu`/`closeLibOverflowMenu`/`isLibOverflowMenuOpen` — Select and Library tools, both of which used to be their own always-visible tab-row controls, same popover shape as the `+` menu). Imports `S`, `$`, `formatBytes`/`totalLibraryBytes` (`library/delete.js`), `updateSearchChipsVisibility` (`library/search.js`), `isLibraryRouteVisible` (`routes.js`), `openLayer` (`nav-stack.js`). RF8 also moved `#libTools`'s static markup from `#route-profile` into `#route-library` (reached from this file's own `#libOverflowToolsBtn` row now, instead of the profile home screen) and re-keyed `refreshLibraryTools()`'s visibility check from `isProfileRouteVisible()` to `isLibraryRouteVisible()` — dropping both that import and the `libraryHasGenres`/`#crateGroupRow` main.js-circular read it used to need (crate grouping's own UI moved entirely onto `#crateGroupToggleTab`, `crate/view.js`'s job now) — so this file has no `main.js` import left at all, circular or otherwise.
  - `player-overlay.js` — `openPlayerViaSheet`, `openPlayer`, `closePlayer`, `morphInFlight`, `runMorphOpen`, `interruptMorph`, and, since RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), the player's own `···` overflow (`openPlayerOverflowMenu`/`closePlayerOverflowMenu`/`isPlayerOverflowMenuOpen` — Edit info/Share/Queue/Sleep timer, moved off the always-visible row under the title; Look stays put, not one of the four RF8 names) — sat under `main.js`'s "launch intro" header only by physical accident (none of it is launch-intro code; flagged by the step 7 prompt itself as one of two stale headers to watch for). Imports `S`/`current`, `$`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `play` (`player/transport.js`), `SLEEVE_ANIM` (`mini-player.js`, real), `runStartSequence`/`interruptStartSequence` (`start-sequence.js`, real, one-directional — `start-sequence.js` has no import back), `openLayer` (`nav-stack.js`) — no `main.js` import at all.

Imports `S`/`current`, `$`/`el`, `sleep`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `albumKey`, `ensureBuffer` (`player/buffers.js`), `isAlbumSealed`/`buildSealOverlay`/`breakSeal` (`library/sealed.js`), `SLEEVE_ANIM`/`renderMiniPlayer` (`mini-player.js`, real), `play` (`player/transport.js`), `clearSpinDown` (`put-away.js`, a real circular pair — `put-away.js` imports `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor` back, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`) — no `main.js` import at all.
  - `handoff.js` — "Part 2 §1: gatefold-to-player hand-off": `gfHandoffInFlight()`, `runGatefoldHandoff(startRect, t, info, startAudio)`, `interruptGatefoldHandoff()`, plus the module-private `HANDOFF_ANIM`/`handoffEaseInOut`. Imports `S`, `$`, `sleep`, `ensureBuffer` (`player/buffers.js`), `SLEEVE_ANIM` (`mini-player.js`, real — only `.holdCapMs`), `clearSpinDown` (`put-away.js`, real), `play` (`player/transport.js`), `forceCloseLayer`/`openLayer` (`nav-stack.js`), `closePlayer` (`player-overlay.js`), plus `isGatefoldOpen` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; the gatefold hasn't moved yet, step 7e). **Bug fix (found investigating a report that a track picked inside the gatefold stopped handing off into the full player): `runGatefoldHandoff()` now closes the gatefold's own nav-stack entry (`forceCloseLayer('gatefold', {skipHistory:true})`) right after the disc/sleeve are hidden, before the player's own `openLayer('player', ...)` call, rather than at the very end of the function (where it used to run, after the flight/overshoot/settle completed).** The old ordering left `layers` as `['gatefold', 'player']` by the time the end-of-function `forceCloseLayer('gatefold')` ran — `closeLayer`/`forceCloseLayer` both close a layer *and* everything stacked above it, which is right for an overlay opened on top of the gatefold but wrong here (the player replaces the gatefold, it doesn't nest inside it), so that call also spliced away the player's own, by-then-already-open layer and ran its default `instantClose` (`closePlayer`), silently closing the player right as the hand-off finished. The reorder alone would reintroduce a second bug (the `history.go()`/`pushState` race `nav-stack.js`'s own `finishClose()` entry above describes), so the moved call also passes `{skipHistory:true}`. The gatefold's own ~400ms exit fade still needs `#gatefold` to keep rendering for that span even though its nav-stack entry (and thus its `.open` class, which is what `display` keys off via CSS) is now closed early — fixed by pinning `gf.style.display = 'block'` as an inline override for exactly that span, cleared (`gf.style.display = ''`, handing control back to CSS) once the fade completes, where the old end-of-function `forceCloseLayer('gatefold')` call used to sit. `interruptGatefoldHandoff()`'s own `forceCloseLayer('gatefold', {skipHistory:true})` call is now a defensive no-op (the gatefold's layer is already closed by the reorder above) kept in case this function is ever reached some other way.
  - `put-away.js` — "put-away: the record goes back in its sleeve": `shouldPutAway(auto)`, `clearSpinDown()`, `putAwayInstant()`, `runPutAwaySequence(t)`, plus the module-private `startSpinDown(ms)`. Imports `S`, `$`, `sleep`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `SLEEVE_ANIM`/`renderMiniPlayer` (`mini-player.js`, real), `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor` (`start-sequence.js`, the real circular pair above) — no `main.js` import at all. `easeOutCubic` moved to `util/motion.js` instead of staying here, despite sitting in the same original section — see that module's own entry for why.
  - `player-side-panel.js` (step D6, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) — the desktop-only side panel beside the full player: `activePlayerSideTab()` (reads which of `#playerSideTabs`'s three buttons is currently `aria-selected="true"`, defaulting to `'upnext'`) and `renderPlayerSidePanel()` (repaints `#playerSideBody` with whichever of the three it is). None of the three renders are new work of their own — Up next is `player/queue.js`'s own `renderQueueSheet`, Liner notes and Credits are `library/credits.js`'s `renderLinerNotes`/`renderCreditsSheet` — this file is only the "which tab, repaint it" dispatcher the doc's own "the Up next... tabs call the same render functions the sheets use, targeted at the panel instead of an overlay" line calls for. Imports `current` (`state.js`), `$` (`util/dom.js`), `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `renderQueueSheet` (`player/queue.js`), `renderCreditsSheet`/`renderLinerNotes` (`library/credits.js`) — no `main.js` import. Called from two places: `src/wiring/transport.js`'s three new tab-click listeners (plus one initial call at the end of `wireTransport()`), and `src/player/transport.js`'s `load()` (so the panel tracks whichever track just loaded, not just tab clicks) — see that file's own updated entry for the real, 3-node import cycle this creates with `player/queue.js`.
  - `sundown-sheet.js` (RF26, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter") — the sheet that replaced the old Sundown/Character tab/route pair: `isSundownSheetOpen()`, `syncSundownSheetHeight(view)` (half-height on Day, full on Sound or on drag-up — a plain class toggle on `#sundownSheet`, not a physics gesture, the same "not much here is pure" shape `shell/put-away.js`'s own choreography already has, covered by the device checklist/Playwright rather than a unit test for the drag feel itself), `openSundownSheet(view)` (registers the sheet with `nav-stack.js`'s `openLayer('sundown-sheet', teardownSundownSheet, {container: [sheet, scrim]})` so Back closes it like any other layer — NV2's "Back closes the top sheet/overlay everywhere" rule, unchanged by this PR; **`container` passes an array here, not a single element** — a real bug found after this step shipped: the sheet and its own `#sundownSheetScrim` are two separate top-level siblings, unlike every `.edit-overlay`/`.share-overlay` in this app, which is one element doubling as its own backdrop, so passing only the sheet left the scrim marked `inert` as "just another background sibling," silently disabling the scrim's own `.open{pointer-events:auto}` CSS rule (`inert`'s UA style always wins over author CSS) and breaking tap-to-dismiss on the backdrop — see `nav-stack.js`'s own updated `inertSiblingsOnPathTo()`/`openLayer()` entry below for the fix), and `wireSundownSheetEntryPoints()` (the sheet's four ways in: the platter's own `#sunKnob`, the mini-player's `#miniSunBtn`, the desktop sidebar's `#sidebarSundown` button, and Settings → Sundown — `profile/screens.js`'s `renderAppSettingsScreen()` — all four call `openSundownSheet()`, none of them touch the platter itself). Module-private: `teardownSundownSheet()` (the close callback actually passed to `openLayer`, never called directly by a UI close button — `#sundownSheetClose`/the scrim both go through `closeLayer('sundown-sheet')` instead, the same `shell/overlays.js`-established pattern), `wireSundownSheetClose()`, `wireSundownSheetDrag()` (the grabber's pointer-drag gesture, `DRAG_OPEN_THRESHOLD`/`DRAG_CLOSE_THRESHOLD` = 60/120px). Imports `openLayer`/`closeLayer` (`nav-stack.js`), `selectSundownView`/`lastSundownView` back from `shell/routes.js` — a real circular pair (see that file's own updated entry above) — no `main.js` import. On desktop, the same sheet markup is re-anchored as a right-side panel by `styles/23-desktop.css` alone (no JS branch) — `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s own established "CSS media query does the reflow, not a viewport check in JS" pattern, same as the tab bar/sidebar in D3. The sun knob on the platter (`#sunKnob`, a sibling of `#platterBox`/`#disc`, never a child or part of the gesture target) never moves the platter and never intercepts the Scratch/spin pointer gesture `player/scrub.js`'s `wireSpinToScrub()` binds to `#platterStage`/`#disc` — the one hard requirement the spec names explicitly.
  - `pull-refresh.js` (NV4, `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`) — the pull-to-refresh gesture: `decidePull`/`pullTravel`/`shouldThrottle` (pure, unit-tested), `buildIndicatorDom(routeEl)` (the dot + `aria-live` status region shown mid-pull), `wirePullToRefresh(routeEl, {onRefresh, getStatusLine})`. **RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 4, "fix the Refresh/Settings overlap") changed what this file owns**: before this step, `buildIndicatorDom` also built a `btn` — a small absolute-positioned keyboard-accessible "Refresh" button layered on top of the route, which on the Profile route sat directly over the new Profile Settings gear (`profile/storefront.js`'s `renderStorefrontSection()`). `buildIndicatorDom`/`wirePullToRefresh` no longer build or wire that button at all — the gesture itself (dot, status line, throttle) is unchanged. Two new exports replace it: `registerProfileRefresh(fn)` (called once at boot by `src/wiring/pull-refresh.js` to hand this module the one stable `runRefresh` closure for the Profile route) and `triggerProfileRefresh()` (called by the Profile Settings menu's "Refresh" item, `storefront.js`, each time the gear's dynamically-rebuilt popover is opened — a registry, not a relocated DOM node, since the gear itself is torn down and rebuilt on every `renderStorefrontSection()` call and can't hold a reference to a button built once at boot). `profileRefreshFn` is a module-private `let`, reassigned only by `registerProfileRefresh()` within this same module (refactor rule 7).
- `src/util/dom.js` exports `$()` (a `document.querySelector` shorthand) and `el()`, the tiny `createElement` helper used ~450 times across the app.
- `src/util/async.js` exports `sleep()`.
- `src/util/text.js` exports `normKey()`, the normalization helper (lowercase, NFKD, strip diacritics/punctuation) used ~40 times app-wide for matching artist/album/title.
- `src/util/math.js` exports `clamp()`.
- `src/util/motion.js` (step 7b) — two small motion-related primitives that used to be top-level `main.js` exports: `REDUCED` (the OS-level reduced-motion preference, read once at module load from `window.matchMedia`, unchanged) and `easeOutCubic()` (the easing curve the put-away sequence's spin-down uses, read by `player/clock.js`'s render loop). Both sat physically inside `main.js`'s "put-away" section, but neither is put-away-specific — `easeOutCubic` is only ever called from `clock.js`, not from `shell/put-away.js` itself. No imports, no `main.js` import.
- `src/util/viewport.js` (step D3, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) — `DESKTOP_BREAKPOINT_PX` (`1024`, matching `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s own desktop-shell breakpoint) and `isDesktopShell()` (a `matchMedia` check against it, called fresh each time rather than cached once at module load the way `util/motion.js`'s `REDUCED` is, since the viewport can change — e.g. resizing a desktop browser window across the breakpoint — in a way `prefers-reduced-motion` effectively never does mid-session). The one place the desktop/phone breakpoint is named in JavaScript; the breakpoint itself still has to be repeated once more, unavoidably, in the CSS media query (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`) a script can't read directly. No imports, no `main.js` import. Used by `shell/mini-player.js`'s `updateChromeInset()` and `crate/view.js`'s `applyCrateMiniInset()` — see each of their own entries for why.
- `src/record-art/tiers.js` — quality tiers (`trackTier`) and variant selection (`albumKey`, `trackIdentityKey`, `VARIANT_DEFS`, `selectVariant`, `variantBackground`) for record-art rendering.
- `src/record-art/metals.js` (step 6b) — the "metal tiers" section: `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor` (threshold ladders in `ALBUM_METAL_THRESHOLDS`/`TRACK_METAL_THRESHOLDS`, exported for their unit test), `metalBackground`, `setDiscBackground`, `METAL_COLORS`. Imports only `S` and `$` — no DOM-dependent top level, no `main.js` import.
- `src/record-art/looks.js` (step 6b) — the "look switching" section: `resolveLook`, `applyDiscVariant` (the rare/metal/variant disc-background renderer — moved here rather than staying a separate export, since it's look-switching's own output, not a distinct concern), `openLookPicker`/`closeLookPicker` (exported; `availableLooks`/`setLookPref`/`lookLabel` stay module-private, used only by these). Imports `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor`/`setDiscBackground` from `metals.js` and `persistAlbumLookPref` from `history/sessions.js` — a real circular import (looks.js ↔ sessions.js: sessions.js imports `applyDiscVariant` back), the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js`. No `main.js` import — this is what let `applyDiscVariant` become a *real* import everywhere it was previously a circular one back to `main.js` (`transport.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`, `history/sessions.js`/`played.js`/`clear.js`).
- `src/sundown/day.js` (step DA1, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) — the day model itself, pure and DOM-free: `dayEvents(date,lat,lon)` (today's dawn/sunrise/solar-noon/sunset/dusk, by calling `solarEvent`/`solarNoon`/`solarAltitudeAtNoon` from `solar.js`) and `dayPosition(now,events)` (`{phase, phaseProgress, nightness, rateProgress}` — one of seven named phases, Night/Dawn/Morning/Midday/Afternoon/Golden hour/Twilight, plus how far through it `now` is). No `prevDusk`/`nextDawn` fields, despite the doc's own prose suggesting that shape — unnecessary, since `solarEvent`'s own Julian-day rounding (`Math.round(...)` in `solarTransit`) always resolves to the astronomically-nearest calendar day regardless of what hour `now` is, so a single `dayEvents(now, lat, lon)` call is already correct "today" in local solar terms at any hour, including the middle of the night; the one discrete day-rollover this produces lands deep inside the flat Night phase where adjacent days' events agree, so it's invisible in `dayPosition`'s own output — confirmed by `tests/unit/day.test.js`'s 48-hour continuity test, not just asserted. `dayPosition` has two fallback branches, both there for a reason, not speculative: a "legacy partial data" branch (`set`+`dusk` present, `dawn`+`rise` absent) that reproduces pre-DA1 `sunProgress()` behavior exactly, needed to keep `tests/unit/solar.test.js`'s own pre-existing `pastDusk`/`midTwilight` fixtures (which construct `S.sun` without the three new fields) passing unchanged; and a "no usable rise/set data at all" branch that calls the new `solarAltitudeAtNoon()` to tell polar day from polar night apart (`solarEvent(...)` alone returns `null` for both, collapsing the distinction), defaulting to day (`rateProgress` 0) if even that's unavailable — the same conservative "no data yet" default `sunProgress()` already had via `if(!S.sun.ok) return 0`. Midday's/Afternoon's own boundary (where the readout would eventually split them further, DA3's own job) is an undocumented judgment call in the doc itself — picked as the midpoint between solar noon and sunset, `clamp`ed so it can never fall before noon or after sunset regardless of how short or long the solar day is. Dawn's own `phaseProgress` is deliberately "percent risen" by construction (`rateProgress = 1 - phaseProgress` there) — it's what `drawSun()`'s own new readout text reads directly, so the percentage shown always matches the direction a user would expect ("50% of the way up," not "50% of the way down"). No `main.js` import; real, one-directional imports from `solar.js` only. Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `dayPosition()`'s return also carries `duskRampProgress` — a second, independent 0..1 progress value, meaningful only while `phase === 'night'`: 0 right at dusk (continuous with twilight's own ending value), 1 once 30 minutes have passed, and 1 for the rest of the night including the pre-dawn tail. It exists because `phaseProgress` itself stays always 0 for `'night'` by design (correct for every existing consumer — `drift.js`'s own `'night'` case is a flat hold that never reads it), which left `clock.js`'s own 8-anchor sky blend (`skyGradientStops()`) with nothing to ramp across for the one interior waypoint its own anchor table needs (the "Dusk" anchor, `docs/design/day-arc/README.md`) — a second field, not a repurposed `phaseProgress`, so none of DA1/DA2's own already-shipped "night's phaseProgress is always 0" behavior changes for anything that doesn't opt into reading this new one.
- `src/sundown/arc.js` (Day Arc v2, `DAY_ARC_BRIEF.md`) — a second, independently-maintained day model (DAWN/DAY/DUSK/NIGHT, four phases, fixed-window sunrise recovery) that the Sundown sheet's Day-view dial reads, deliberately kept apart from `day.js`'s own seven-phase `dayPosition()` above (see this file's own header comment for why): `phaseAt`, `rateInfoAt`/`rateAt`/`trendAt` (the platter-rate curve, including the `RECOVERY_WINDOW_MS` recovery leg), `warmthAt`/`toneWordAt`/`driftOffsetsAt`/`effectiveCharAt` (the character-drift warmth curve, `audio/engine.js`'s one real caller), and the sky lookups `skyPhaseAt`/`skyBackgroundStops`/`skyPaletteNameAt`/`skyRingStops`. Pure, no DOM, no `S` import — reads only whatever `sun`/`settings` object it's given (`src/sundown/dial-view.js` is the DOM-painting half that calls into this module; see that file for how). **RF14 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 3) rewrote `skyRingStops(sun)`**: the dial ring's own eight hand-picked EMBER/INDIGO/ROSE/AZURE-family hexes (`SKY_STOPS`/`SKY_PALETTE_NAME`, still used, unchanged, by `skyBackgroundStops`/`skyPaletteNameAt` — RF14 only touches the ring) are gone from the ring specifically, replaced by `RING_PHASE_COLOR` — the same seven phase names and already-desaturated hexes as `docs/design/day-arc/README.md`'s own new "Moving palette rules (RF14)" table, placed "at the clock angles of today's events" via `RING_MORNING_SPAN_MIN`/`RING_GOLDEN_SPAN_MIN`, the same morning/golden-span clamping shape `day.js`'s own `dayPosition()` uses for its Morning/Golden-hour boundaries — duplicated in minutes-of-day rather than imported, per this file's own header comment (the same "duplicate the small shared formula rather than wire two independent systems together" call CLAUDE.md documents for `sync/images.js`/`player/clock.js`'s own `PHASE_COLOR` duplication). The Midday/Afternoon boundary inside the ring reuses `day.js`'s own "midpoint between solar noon and sunset, clamped so it never falls before noon or after sunset" rule (a direct copy at noon itself, an earlier version of this code, put solar noon exactly on that boundary — where a conic-gradient's own same-position stop-ordering silently resolves to the *second*-listed color, Afternoon, breaking "solar noon always falls inside the midday span"; `tests/unit/arc.test.js` has a dedicated regression test for this). Stops are pushed in strict chronological order (0 → 1440 minutes, Night split into its own pre-midnight and post-dusk halves) so a plain stable `Array.sort` closes the ring and resolves every same-minute boundary tie correctly with no separate wrap-around case.
- `src/sundown/solar.js` — sun position math behind Sundown's auto playback rate. Since step DA1 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), split into the always-existing astronomy (`solarEvent`, now taking an optional `rising` param so the same hour-angle equation computes the dawn/sunrise side too — `Jt − w/360` instead of `Jt + w/360` — defaulting to `false` so every pre-DA1 3-/4-argument call site is byte-for-byte unchanged; `solarNoon`, new, never returns `null` since the sun transits the local meridian every day regardless of latitude, even through polar day/night; `solarAltitudeAtNoon`, new, the one thing that *can* tell polar day from polar night apart once `solarEvent` itself collapses that distinction to `null`) and the day-model consumption that used to be this file's whole job (`computeSun`, `nowClock`, `sunProgress`, `easedProgress`, `computeRate`). `computeSun()` now calls `dayEvents()` (`day.js`, a real circular pair — `day.js` imports `solarEvent`/`solarNoon`/`solarAltitudeAtNoon` back from here, the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`) and fills all five of `S.sun`'s event fields plus `altitudeAtNoon`, keeping the exact pre-existing implausibly-short-twilight guard (`dusk - set < 60000` bumps dusk to `set + 25min`) with no equivalent guard added for dawn/rise — a genuinely degenerate dawn/rise gap is covered by `dayPosition()`'s own polar fallback instead, not a second hand-tuned minimum. `sunProgress()` is now `currentDayPosition().rateProgress` — same name, same meaning (0 at full day-rate, 1 at full night-rate), but continuous across the *whole* day now, not just the sunset-window value it held before DA1. `easedProgress()` keeps Window/Moment pacing (`pace()`, unchanged shape: `smooth(p) = p*p*(3-2*p)`, Moment compresses the back 38%) but now applies it symmetrically to *both* sides — the dusk side exactly as before, and a mirrored dawn side (`1 - pace(1 - rateProgress)`, since "risen" is the dawn-local equivalent of the dusk side's own raw progress) producing a "held back, then a felt move near the very end" shape on the way up too, not just the way down. **`computeRate()` is completely unchanged** — still the one place speed is decided, still returning exactly `1` in Pure before even checking `S.auto`, satisfying the doc's own "the single place speed is decided" requirement by construction rather than by a new guard. New export `currentDayPosition()` (`if(!S.sun.ok)` falls back to a flat midday, `rateProgress:0`, the same "no data yet" default as before) is the one new surface `clock.js`'s `drawSun()` needs directly, for `phase`/`phaseProgress` — every other caller still only ever sees the `rateProgress`-only view `sunProgress()`/`easedProgress()` already gave them. Since step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also exports `SUN_TIMES_STORAGE_KEY`/`persistSunTimesCache(rise, set)` — the device-only, no-coordinates `localStorage` cache (`{date, rise, set}`) `src/theme-boot.js` reads to resolve a pinned Nychthemeron choice before first paint, written here (and from `location.js`'s `fallbackSun()`, which already imports `computeSun` from this file) since this is where "today's" rise/set are actually computed, real or fallback alike; and `ui/theme.js` imports `currentDayPosition` from here, a new real, one-directional edge (this file doesn't import anything back), for `effectiveTheme()`'s own Nychthemeron resolution. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also exports `realDayPosition()` — `dayPosition(new Date(), S.sun)`, computed directly against the real clock rather than `S.previewMin`, the one deliberate difference from `currentDayPosition()` just above it: every Sundown UI readout (the arc, the rate line, the sky) is supposed to follow "Preview the day" when it's active, but a durable listening-history counter never should — `history/played.js`'s `incrementTrackPlay()` is this export's one caller, for exactly that reason (see that module's own entry below). Since RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown, set once"), also exports `DEPTH_TARGETS` (`{gentle:0.85, medium:0.80, deep:0.75}`) and two pure mapping functions, `depthKeyFor(auto, target)`/`depthCaptionFor(key, target)` — the one "How deep the night goes" Off/Gentle/Medium/Deep control replaced the old Auto/Manual toggle, manual-rate slider, full-night-rate seg and Window/Moment pacing seg; `depthKeyFor` reads `S.auto`/`S.target` back onto whichever of the four buttons they correspond to (an unrecognized saved target falls back to Medium, not Off), and `depthCaptionFor` builds the one-line caption ("Medium — 0.80× · 3.9 st") both `src/wiring/sundown.js`'s click handler and `src/storage/meta.js`'s restore-time sync use. `computeRate()` itself is untouched — the cut is UI only, same as every other RF9 change.
- `src/sundown/location.js` — geolocation handling (`askLocation`, `fallbackSun`, `toggleSleevePull`, `applyVolume`). RF6 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) removed Car Mode (`toggleMotion`, the GPS speed watch) outright, as session-only state with nothing to migrate — `applyVolume()` is kept as the plain master-gain setter the sleep-timer fade, the audio engine's sample-rate rebuild, and `applyPrefs()` all still call. Imports `drawSun` from `clock.js` and, since step 7a, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` — a real circular pair with `meta.js` now (`meta.js` imports `applyVolume` back from here for `applyPrefs()`), the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. Since step DA1, `fallbackSun()` fills all five day-model fields instead of just `set`/`dusk` — fixed stand-in times for whenever location is unavailable, unchanged from before DA1 where they already existed (sunset 8:10 pm, dusk 8:36 pm) and newly added for the rest of the day (dawn 6:20 am, sunrise 6:50 am, solar noon 1:00 pm), with `altitudeAtNoon` left `null` since there's no real latitude to compute it from. Also exports the new `scheduleMidnightRecompute()` — a recursive `setTimeout` (not `setInterval`, so each firing is scheduled off the real current time and the daily cadence never drifts) that recomputes `computeSun()`/`drawSun()` a few seconds past local midnight, then reschedules itself — the fix for the doc's own named gap ("sun times go stale if the app stays open past midnight"); `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s own `visibilitychange` handler covers the other half, a session backgrounded across midnight that never had this timer actually fire while hidden. Since step DA4, `fallbackSun()` also calls `persistSunTimesCache()` (imported from `solar.js` alongside `computeSun`) with its own fixed stand-in rise/set — "written whenever events are computed," per that doc, and a fixed stand-in still counts, keeping the cache's own freshness stamp current on a device that never grants location.
