- `src/crate/constants.js` — the small block of crate-view layout constants (`CRATE_ORIGIN_Y`, `CRATE_PALETTE`, `CRATE_VISIBLE_A`, `CRATE_DPR`, `CRATE_ALPHABET`).
- `src/crate/geometry.js` (step 7c) — `flowPose`/`flowZIndex`, the shared 3D-pose math (CRATE spec §3: "one geometry implementation so the two views can never drift apart") used by both the launch overlay's card stack and the crate shelf itself. Pure, zero imports — moved verbatim out of `main.js`'s old launch-intro section.
- `src/crate/tiers.js` (step 7c) — the "S4: crate image tiers" section: `crateArtCache` (the `{S, L}` LRU cache, exported read-only for `launch/intro.js`'s own card-art lookup), `buildCrateTier`, `getCrateArtURL`, `deleteCrateArtTiers`, `healCrateArt`, `prewarmCrateAnchorL`. Imports `S`, `sleep` (`util/async.js`), `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `artwork` (`storage/repo.js`) — no `main.js` import.
- `src/crate/model.js` (step 7c) — the crate data model, split out of the same old "S4" section it had been sitting in only by physical accident (grouping/sorting albums for the shelf isn't image-tier work): `libraryHasGenres`, `ensureCrateModel`, `invalidateCrateModel`, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `persistCrateAnchor`, `crateRailJumpTarget`. Imports `S`, `normKey` (`util/text.js`), `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `queueSave` (`storage/meta.js`), `CRATE_ALPHABET` (`./constants.js`) — no `main.js` import.
- `src/crate/view.js` (step 7c) — the actual crate-shelf rendering (`applyCrateMiniInset`, `renderCrate`, `centerEmptyState`), which had been sitting inside `main.js`'s misleadingly-named "S3: gatefold album view" header purely by physical accident — that header also covers the gatefold itself, the sealed-record sheet and `renderTracks`, none of which moved here. Imports `S`, `$`/`el`, `clamp`, `REDUCED`, the crate-view constants from `./constants.js`, `flowPose`/`flowZIndex` from `./geometry.js`, `isAlbumSealed`/`buildSealOverlay` from `library/sealed.js`, `thumbURL` from `library/thumbs.js`, `setQueue` from `player/queue.js`, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js`, `isLibraryRouteVisible` from `shell/routes.js`, `getCrateArtURL`/`prewarmCrateAnchorL` from `./tiers.js`, `ensureCrateModel`/`invalidateCrateModel`/`isNowPlayingAlbum`/`persistCrateAnchor`/`crateRailJumpTarget`/`libraryHasGenres` from `./model.js`, `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` from `../launch/intro.js` (real, one-directional — `intro.js` doesn't import back, so this isn't a circular pair), and, since the design-layout polish pass, `openCreditsSheet` from `library/credits.js` (also real, one-directional — `credits.js` doesn't import anything from `crate/view.js`). Since step 7d, `renderTracks` is a real import from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — a real circular pair with it (`view.js` imports `centerEmptyState`/`renderCrate` back from here), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. Plus a temporary circular import back to `main.js` for `openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay` (rule 8 — the gatefold and sealed-sheet code is 7e's territory). Since step D3, also imports `isDesktopShell` (`util/viewport.js`) — `applyCrateMiniInset()`'s own phone-layout height budget (`window.innerHeight` minus the fixed tab bar's and mini-player's heights) treats the tab bar's `getBoundingClientRect().height` as `0` on desktop, the same reasoning and the same fix as `shell/mini-player.js`'s `updateChromeInset()` right above it — the tab bar is a full-height vertical sidebar there, not bottom chrome, so its height has nothing to do with this budget. Since step D5 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), this module also carries the crate's own desktop layout — a larger card, scaled stage geometry, and the inline record-detail panel that replaces the gatefold as the way in on a tap, at desktop widths only:
  - `currentCardSize()` (module-private) reads `--crate-card-size`'s live computed value (`10-crate.css`'s own `278px` default, overridden to `340px` at `1024px+` by `23-desktop.css`) rather than hardcoding either number, falling back to the 278px baseline if the property somehow doesn't resolve. `renderCrate(host)` computes `cardSize`/`cardSpread` (`cardSize / 278`) once per render and threads both through: `buildSealOverlay(cardSize)` in place of the old literal `278`, and `flowPose(a, d, speed, {originY: CRATE_ORIGIN_Y, spread: cardSpread})` in `syncCards` — reusing `flowPose()`'s own pre-existing `opts.spread` multiplier (previously only exercised by the launch intro's animated flourish, `launch/intro.js`) to scale the vertical offset between stacked cards proportionally to the card's own new size, so a bigger desktop card never overlaps its neighbours any more tightly than the 278px phone baseline already does — zero changes to `flowPose()` itself (`crate/geometry.js`).
  - `openCrateAlbum(item)` gained one new branch, between the existing sealed check and the `openGatefold(item)` call: `if(isDesktopShell()){ renderCrateDetailPanel(item); return; }`. A sealed record is unaffected either way — it still opens the sealed sheet on both phone and desktop, exactly as before this step. The gatefold itself is untouched and stays the phone's own way in.
  - `renderCrateDetailPanel(item)`/`renderCrateDetailPlaceholder(panel)` (both new, module-private) build the desktop-only inline panel's content into `#crateDetailPanel`: cover art (reusing `thumbURL`/the track's own embedded `art`, the same fallback order the crate card face already uses), a "Play album" button (`setQueue(item.idx, 0, true)`), and the tracklist — built by calling the gatefold's own, already-exported `buildGatefoldTracklist(item, trackIdx, playHandler)`/`gatefoldTrackIndices(item)` (`gatefold/view.js`) directly, rather than reimplementing tracklist rendering a second time. This is a real, one-directional import addition (`gatefold/view.js` doesn't import anything back for this), riding alongside the module's existing circular import of `openGatefold` from the same file. D5's own "first cut" trimmed this down to just those three things; the design-layout polish pass (below) added two more, the same "reuse, don't reimplement" way: a "Credits" button (`.cta.ghost`, next to Play album, both now inside a `.stack.crate-detail-actions` row — Play album itself moved off `.tbtn.play`, the transport row's own 44px circular button, onto `.cta` since the multi-word label was wrapping inside it) calling `openCreditsSheet(t)` (`library/credits.js`) for the panel's own album, and `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML(item)` (now exported from `gatefold/view.js`) appended after the tracklist for the same rating/sleeve-note/pin controls the gatefold's own left panel already has — `src/styles/11-gatefold.css`'s `.gf-left`-scoped rules for that fragment were widened to also match `.crate-detail-panel`. `renderCrateDetailPlaceholder()` is what the panel shows before anything's been tapped ("Tap a record to see its track list."). Also since the polish pass: `openCrateAlbum()`'s own desktop branch checks `$('#crateDetailPanel')` exists before taking it, not just `isDesktopShell()` — a real, pre-existing gap found during that review: `#crateDetailPanel` is only ever built inside `renderCrate()`'s own non-`REDUCED` path, so a desktop user with `prefers-reduced-motion` set (who gets `renderCrateFlat()` instead, which never builds the panel at all) used to have a tap on a record silently do nothing; it now falls back to the gatefold, the one other way in that already works everywhere.
  - `renderCrate(host)` wraps the existing `.crate-wrap` (unchanged — same `nowPlayingBtn`/`stage`/`rail`/`info` children, same `applyCrateMiniInset()` contract, since it's still `position:relative` and still found by the exact same `document.querySelector('.crate-wrap')`-style lookups) and a new sibling `#crateDetailPanel` (`.crate-detail-panel`) inside a new `.crate-layout` wrapper — the same "invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint via `display:contents`" pattern `.sidebar` established in D3, so `.crate-wrap`'s own phone layout, DOM position, and `applyCrateMiniInset()`'s own math are all completely unaffected below 1024px.
  - `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` gained a tab-row mirror: alongside the existing Profile → Library tools copy (`#crateGroupToggle`), a new desktop-only copy (`#crateGroupToggleTab`, new ids, in the Library route's own tab row — "Crate order toggle in the tab row," the doc's third "New" item) is shown/hidden by `isDesktopShell()` and kept in sync with `S.crateGroup`/`hasGenres` every time this function runs (i.e., on every crate re-render) — a mirror of existing state, not a relocation or a clone of the existing control, the same "new ids driving the same state" shape D3's sidebar mirrors and D4's player-bar controls both already established. A bare resize across the breakpoint while already on the crate screen doesn't immediately re-sync this (nothing currently ties it to the existing `resize` listener in `main.js`) — a small, accepted gap, the same class as several other `isDesktopShell()`-is-read-fresh-not-resize-synced spots already in this codebase; the next crate re-render self-corrects. **RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "one home per control") removed the Profile → Library tools copy (`#crateGroupToggle`/`#crateGenreBtn`) outright and un-gated `#crateGroupToggleTab` from `isDesktopShell()`**, so the tab-row copy is now the only home for this control, shown on every platform, not just desktop — `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` always sets `tabToggle.style.display = 'flex'` (when the element exists) rather than branching on viewport width, and the `isDesktopShell` import this file still has is for its other, unrelated uses (the crate detail panel, `applyCrateMiniInset()`).
- `src/launch/intro.js` (step 7c) — the launch intro sequence, moved out of `main.js`'s old "launch intro" section (which also held the player overlay/morph and shared crate geometry — neither moved here, see those modules' own entries): `resolveLastPlayedAlbum`, `resolveLastPlayedTrackIndex`, `sampleStackAlbums`/`computeFlickPosition`/`flickSpeed` (each given `export`, previously private, for their own unit tests — same precedent as `angleDelta` in `scrub.js`, step 3c), `runLaunchSequence`, `attachLaunchSettingRow`, `toggleLaunchIntro`, and `markLibraryReady()` — a small wrapper added around the module-private `libraryReadyResolve`/`libraryReadyPromise` pair (rule 7: an importing module can't reassign an exported `let`, so the boot sequence's two `libraryReadyResolve()` call sites in `main.js` now call this instead). Imports `S`, `$`, `sleep`, `clamp`, `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `albumTracks`/`groupBy` (`library/model.js`), `thumbURL` (`library/thumbs.js`), `queueSave` (`storage/meta.js`), `flowPose`/`flowZIndex` (`../crate/geometry.js`), `crateArtCache` (`../crate/tiers.js`) — no `main.js` import. `toggleScratch`, which had been sitting in this section only because that's where the Character menu's Scratch toggle wiring happened to land, moved into `src/player/scrub.js` instead (that module already owns Scratch — see its own entry).
- `src/player/sleep-timer.js` — the sleep timer (SLEEP spec §5): `updateSleepUI`, `stopSleepState`, `sleepStopPlayback`, `sleepCheckDeadline`, `openSleepSheet`, `closeSleepSheet`. Imports `pause` from `transport.js` and `drawTime` from `clock.js`. No longer imports anything from `main.js` (step 3c).
- `src/audio/engine.js` — the audio graph (`makeContext`, `buildGraph`, `satCurve`, `applyCharacter`, `routeSource`) and `ensureContext()` (from the "file loading" section — rebuilds the context when a file's sample rate differs). Imports `applyVolume` from `location.js`, `stop` from `transport.js`, and, since step 6c, `applyOutputRoute` from `player/background.js` (a real circular import — background.js imports `ensureContext` back from here for its Media Session "play" handler, the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js`). No `main.js` import at all, since step 6c.
- `src/player/transport.js` — `load`, `startSource`, `play`, `stop`, `pause`, `seek` (step 3b). The iOS-gesture-critical path: `bgPrime()` is the first call in `play()`, before any `await`, because iOS only allows audio to start inside the original tap; no `await` was added, removed or reordered when this moved, and the `S.gen` generation counter / `onended` logic (how a stale source is kept from advancing the queue) and the encoder delay/padding handling in `startSource` (PLAYBACK spec §1) are unchanged. Imports `drawTime`/`settleTrackEnd` from `clock.js`, `setPathNote` from `ui/path-note.js`, `advance`/`preloadNextTrack` from `player/queue.js`, `ensureBuffer` from `player/buffers.js`, `sessionOnLoad`/`touchActiveSession` from `history/sessions.js`, `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent`/`updateFavoriteButtons` from `library/credits.js`/`library/favorites.js` (all real imports as of step 5a/5c/5e, previously circular through `main.js`), and, since step 6c, `bgPrime`/`bgRouteActive`/`bgIdleNow`/`bgPosition` from `player/background.js` (a real circular import — background.js imports `play`/`pause`/`seek` back from here for its Media Session action handlers, same shape as above). Since step 7d, `renderTracks` is a real import from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — only `updateGatefoldNowPlaying` (gatefold, 7e) remains a circular import back to `main.js`. See rule 8 and the step 3b/3c/5a/5c/5e/6c/7d changelog entries below for the full list and the future-cleanup note. Since step D6 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), `load()` also calls `renderPlayerSidePanel` (`shell/player-side-panel.js`) at its own end, the same single choke point `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent()`/`drawTime()` already use for "something that must track `current()`" — keeps the desktop side panel's active tab in step with whichever track just loaded. `S.queue`/`S.qpos` are always updated by `load()`'s own caller (`setQueue()`/`advance()`, both in `player/queue.js`) before `load()` itself runs, so the panel's own "Up next" branch never sees stale queue state. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `play()` also imports `realDayPosition` from `sundown/solar.js` (real, one-directional — `solar.js` doesn't import anything back) and passes `realDayPosition().phase` as `MizikiSocial.nowSpinning()`'s new third argument — the real clock only, never `currentDayPosition()`/`S.previewMin`, since what a friend sees has to be this listener's own actual phase, never fooled by their own Preview-the-day slider. Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), `load()` also imports `albumTracks` (`library/model.js`) and `splitSides` (`shell/ceremony-timing.js`), writes the current track's album (or title, if no album tag) into the new `#labelTitle`, and computes the record's side letter/format via `splitSides(albumTracks(...), S.albumSides)` the same way `shell/runout.js`'s side-flip logic already does — caching the static "SIDE A · STEREO"-style half of the label's mono arc text into the new, unsaved `S.labelSideFormat` and writing the arc's initial text immediately (not waiting for the next throttled `drawSun()` tick) so a track load never shows a stale RPM/side reading even for a moment.
- `src/player/clock.js` — the render loop (step 3c): `tick`, `settleTrackEnd`, `frame`, the hidden-page `setInterval` fallback, `fmt`, `drawTime`, `drawSun`. Imports `sleepCheckDeadline` from `sleep-timer.js`, `computeRate`/`sunProgress`/`easedProgress` from `solar.js`, `addPlayedRange`/`checkTrackCompletion` from `history/played.js` (real since step 5c), and, since step 6c, `bgPosition` from `player/background.js` (real, not circular — clock.js calls it, background.js never calls back into clock.js), plus `easeOutCubic`/`REDUCED` back from `main.js` (circular, same reason as above). The `maxDt` caps (0.1s for frames, 120s for the hidden-page timer), the order of work in `tick` (sleep deadline, then rate, then position, then played ranges and completion) and the 90ms/220ms redraw throttles are all unchanged — sessions, metals and the 95% rule depend on this clock. Since step D3 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), `drawSun()` also mirrors its own `#rateWhy`/`#legStart`/`#legEnd` readout into the desktop sidebar's own `#sideRateWhy`/`#sideLegStart`/`#sideLegEnd` ids (null-guarded — those ids exist only in the sidebar's own DOM, `src/shell/sidebar.js`, hidden below the breakpoint by CSS, so the guard is "does the sidebar card exist," never a breakpoint check) — per that doc's own instruction to reuse this exact readout rather than a second module calling `sunProgress()`/`computeRate()` on its own. Since step D4, `drawTime()` gained the same kind of guarded mirror, into the desktop player bar's own `#barElapsed`/`#barTotal`/`#barScrub` — `#tElapsed`/`#tTotal`/`#scrub` stay the full player's own ids (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s own "duplicate element ids are not allowed" rule), so the bar's seek readout is a second small mirror of the same already-computed values, not a second position computation. Since step DA1 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `drawSun()` also imports `currentDayPosition` from `solar.js` and inserts two new `#rateWhy` branches ahead of the existing day/night/twilight ones — `phase === 'dawn'` ("Dawn — rising, N% of the way up," reading `phaseProgress` directly, "percent risen" by construction — see `day.js`'s own entry) and `phase === 'morning'` ("Morning — full speed") — leaving every other existing branch's wording byte-for-byte unchanged; every other phase (midday/afternoon/golden hour, all still "full daylight" as far as the rate is concerned) still falls through to the pre-existing "Daylight — full speed" line, since DA1 only asks for Dawn's and Morning's own text, not a distinct line per phase (that richer split is DA3's own job). Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `skyGradientStops(p)` became `skyGradientStops(dayPos, theme)` — 8 named anchors (`docs/design/day-arc/README.md`'s own sky-token table), smoothstep-blended between neighbours by `dayPos`'s own `phase`/`phaseProgress` (and, for `'night'`, `duskRampProgress` — see `day.js`'s own updated entry), the same architecture DA2's own `drift.js` already uses and for the same reason: every phase boundary is continuous by construction. `drawSun()` now always animates `--sky1`/`--sky2` in both themes (the D2-era "Light keeps its static tokens, no inline override" behavior — and the later sky-fix entry's own "Light simply keeps the light tokens" rule — are both superseded on purpose, now that the design README gives Light a full, contrast-checked anchor table of its own). `drawSun()` also gained a new, separate phase block (`phaseText()`, a small pure helper — big phase name plus a muted sub-line, e.g. "Twilight"/"60% of the way down") written into `#phaseName`/`#phaseSub` (and mirrored into the desktop sidebar's own `#sidePhaseName`/`#sidePhaseSub`) — kept distinct from the existing, shorter `#rateWhy` copy just above it, which is untouched; a day-arc SVG position (`dayArcPosition()`, pure, schematic/not-to-scale per the design README's own note — the night phase's own moon dot sits at one fixed representative point rather than animating across the night, a deliberate, documented simplification, since `dayPosition()` exposes no finer-grained night progress for it to track) written via a new `updateDayArc()`; **RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) removed the Day-drift shortcut row this sentence used to describe (`#dayDriftSummary`, backed by `driftSummaryText()`) outright — the Day/Sound tabs are the one way into Sound now — so `drawSun()` no longer calls `driftSummaryText()` at all, and `sundown/drift.js` dropped that now-fully-unused export**; and each Sound slider's own "now" badge (`effectiveChar()`, reusing the exact drifted value `applyCharacter()` already computed, never recomputed a second time) — shown only once it would actually read differently from the slider's own user value. Since step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `drawSun()` also calls `ui/theme.js`'s `syncNychthemeronTheme()` once per tick — the live Nychthemeron light/dark watcher, piggybacking this same already-ticking loop rather than a second timer; see that file's own entry for what it does. Since NV1 (`docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`), `drawSun()` keeps the last `--sky1`/`--sky2` strings it actually wrote and skips the `setProperty` calls when the new ones match — the custom-property write forces a repaint of `.sky-bg` (`src/styles/00-base.css`'s own entry below), and `skyGradientStops()`'s output changes far less often than this ~220ms-throttled tick runs. Since RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter"), `drawSun()` also writes `dataset.phase = dayPos.phase` onto `#sunKnob`/`#miniSunBtn` (null-guarded, the same "exists only in the DOM, hidden or shown by CSS" shape every other mirror on this function already uses) plus the knob's own `#sunKnobTime` readout text and `#trackSpecPhase`'s text (the track-spec line's new phase segment, e.g. "GOLDEN HOUR") — one more small, duplicative mirror of state this function already computes, the same pattern as the sidebar/player-bar mirrors above. The phase-to-color mapping those new `[data-phase]` elements key off of lives entirely in CSS (`styles/26-sundown-sheet.css`), duplicating three of `profile/rhythm-card.js`'s own `PHASE_COLOR` table values as plain CSS custom properties rather than importing that module here — this file is the single most widely-imported render loop in the app, and adding an import edge into it for three color strings isn't worth the new dependency; the same "duplicate rather than add an edge" call `sync/images.js` already made for its own crop logic. **RF14 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "a moving palette with fixed rules")**: the dark Golden/Twilight sky anchors (`SKY_ANCHORS.dark`) were cooled slightly (Golden `[[61,42,31],...]` → `[[53,42,36],...]`; Twilight `[[59,33,48],...]` → `[[51,35,46],...]`; sky2 unchanged for both) so `--accent`/`--accent-text` stays the warmest, most saturated thing on screen even during the two phases closest to it in hue (point 2) — verified against the existing accent-family contrast test (`tests/unit/contrast.test.js`) and a new per-minute sweep (below). The anchor-blend switch statement inside `skyGradientStops()` was pulled out into a shared `blendAnchors(anchors, dayPos)` so a second anchor table could reuse the exact same blend logic without a second copy of the switch: `skyGradientStops(dayPos, theme)` is now `blendAnchors(SKY_ANCHORS[...], dayPos)`, and a new, exported `mutedGradientStops(dayPos, theme)` (point 1) is `blendAnchors(MUTED_ANCHORS_DARK, dayPos)` in dark (a `MUTED_COOL`/`MUTED_WARM` pair, warm only during Golden/Twilight, cool everywhere else including Dusk) or a single flat `MUTED_LIGHT` pair in light (no warm/cool split needed there — every light sky anchor, including Golden, already clears both floors against the one pair). `drawSun()` calls both: `--muted`/`--muted-2` are written inline the same "only when changed" way `--sky1`/`--sky2` already are (two new module-level `lastMuted`/`lastMuted2` caches, same pattern as `lastSky1`/`lastSky2`), each clearing ≥4.5:1 (muted) / ≥3:1 (muted-2) against the sky at every sampled minute of a real day, in both themes — `tests/unit/clock.test.js` has the per-minute sweep, plus exact-value tests per phase and a `drawSun()` inline-write test. `docs/design/day-arc/README.md`'s new "Moving palette rules (RF14)" section has the literal hex table for both the cooled anchors and the muted/muted-2 pairs; `docs/design/refine/README.md`'s own pre-existing "Colour (RF12, RF14)" section is the design source both were taken from. (RF14 point 3, the dial ring's own color source, and point 4, the `--raised`/`--panel` dark value steps, live in `sundown/arc.js`'s `skyRingStops()` and `styles/00-base.css`/`22-theme.css` respectively — see those files' own entries.) Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), `drawSun()`'s old `#rpmText` write is gone, replaced by a write to the new `#labelArcText` (the platter label's mono arc, SVG `<textPath>`, `miziki.html`) — the RPM figure plus the static side/format half `player/transport.js`'s `load()` already cached into `S.labelSideFormat`, e.g. "33.3 RPM · SIDE A · STEREO".
- `src/player/background.js` (step 6c) — "background playback (optional)": `bgPrime`, `bgRouteActive`, `bgIdleNow`, `bgPosition`, `bgSync`, `bgUpdateUI`, `toggleBgAudio`, `applyBgAudioSetting`, `applyOutputRoute`, and the Media Session / lock-screen handlers (`mediaSessionSync`, `bgInstallHandlers`, `bgRemoveHandlers`, kept module-private). `bgPrime()` is still the very first call in `play()` (`player/transport.js`), before any `await` — moving where it's *defined* changed nothing about when it *runs* (rule 9). The lock-screen `previoustrack`/`nexttrack` handlers still skip tracks rather than seek (PR #42's behavior, untouched), and every Media Session action handler and position-state update installs/fires in the same order as before. Imports `ensureContext` from `audio/engine.js` and `play`/`pause`/`seek` from `player/transport.js` (both real circular imports, see those modules' own entries above), `prevTrack`/`nextTrack` from `player/controls.js` (real, for the lock-screen handlers), `setPathNote` from `ui/path-note.js`, and, since step 7a, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` — a real circular pair with `meta.js` now (`meta.js` imports `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` back from here for `applyPrefs()`) — for `toggleBgAudio`'s own save.
- `src/player/controls.js` (step 6c) — `nextTrack`, `prevTrack`, `toggleShuffle`, `cycleRepeat` (previously sitting under `main.js`'s "persistence" header, where they'd landed only because that's where this part of "visible queue" happened to be — none of them touch persistence). Imports `seek` from `player/transport.js`, `advance`/`reorderQueue` from `player/queue.js`, `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny` from `history/sessions.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import at all.
- `src/audio/mode.js` (step 6c) — `setMode` (previously sitting under `main.js`'s "share image" header, for the same reason as above — it's mode switching, not sharing). Imports `routeSource` from `audio/engine.js` (real) — no `main.js` import at all. F1's Pure-rate rule (`computeRate()` in `sundown/solar.js` returning exactly `1` in Pure) lives entirely in `solar.js` and was untouched by this move — `setMode` only sets `S.mode` and re-routes the current source; `computeRate()` reads `S.mode` the same way it always did. Since step D4, `setMode()` also mirrored the same pressed state onto the desktop player bar's own mirror of the mode control, per `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s "duplicate element ids are not allowed" rule — new ids, never clones of the full player's own. Since CEREMONY PR 2 (`docs/CEREMONY.md` §1), both are a single toggle rather than a two-button group: `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl` and `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` are gone; `setMode()` instead sets `aria-checked` and the visible label text ("Vinyl"/"Pure") on `#modeToggle`/`#modeToggleLabel` and, null-guarded the same way, on `#barModeToggle`/`#barModeToggleLabel` — same mirror, same reasoning, one element instead of two. RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) deleted the three-line Vinyl explanation `setMode()` used to write into `#modeNote` on every call (the Sound view already explains Vinyl) — `#modeTag`/`#modeToggle`/`#modeToggleLabel` are untouched; `setMode()` just no longer has a third element to write to. Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), `#modeTag` itself is gone (the platter label's old plain "vinyl"/"pure" text tag, superseded by the new title/arc label — see `miziki.html`'s own entry) and `setMode()` instead toggles a new `mode-pure` class on `document.documentElement` (`classList.toggle('mode-pure', !vinyl)`, right alongside the existing `--accent` custom-property write) — the hook `02-stage-platter.css`'s new Pure-mode label-flatten rule reads, so the object itself reads "untouched" in Pure without this file needing to touch the label's own DOM at all.
- `src/player/scrub.js` — spin-to-scrub (step 3c): `wireSpinToScrub`, `angleDelta` (exported for its unit test, same as `solarEvent` in step 2c), and their constants, plus `toggleScratch` (step 7c — the Character menu's Scratch toggle handler, moved here from `main.js`'s old launch-intro section, where it had been sitting only by physical accident; this module already owns everything else about Scratch). Imports `drawTime` from `clock.js`, `pause`/`play`/`seek` from `transport.js`, and `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` (for `toggleScratch`'s own save) — no `main.js` import. Since F2, this is also where true scratching lives: when a gesture is claimed in Vinyl with `S.char.scratch` on, it lazily loads `src/audio/scratch-processor.js` into an `AudioWorkletNode`, copies a ±10s window of the current track's decoded channel data to it, and streams `target`/`window` messages to it on every `pointermove` (see `SCRATCH_SEC_PER_ROTATION`, `SCRATCH_WINDOW_SEC`). Scratch off, or Pure mode, falls back to the original silent fast-scrub unchanged.
- `src/audio/scratch-processor.js` — the F2 `AudioWorkletProcessor` scrub.js loads to produce real scratch audio. A plain classic script — no `import`, no `export` — both because Vite copies it verbatim via `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` in `scrub.js` rather than bundling it, and because Safari's AudioWorklet module loader has not reliably supported ES module syntax in the processor file itself (an early version that used `export` worked on desktop but silently produced no scratch audio on an iPhone). `tests/unit/scratch-processor.test.js` loads its source with Node's `vm` module to reach its pure math (`clampIndex`, `sampleAt`, `onePole`, `velocityEnvelopeTarget`) instead of importing it.
- `src/ui/path-note.js` — `setPathNote()` (step 3c), the general on-screen note helper. It happened to live in the spin-to-scrub section but isn't about scrubbing, so it got its own module rather than moving into `scrub.js`. No `main.js` import.
- `src/ui/theme.js` (step D2, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) — the Auto/Light/Dark(/Nychthemeron, step DA4) appearance preference: `applyTheme()` (sets/removes `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` per `S.theme`, mirrors a pinned choice to `localStorage` for `src/theme-boot.js`'s own anti-flash read, updates `<meta name="theme-color">` since a meta attribute can't read a CSS custom property, installs/tears down a `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: light)')` listener so Auto keeps following the device live — e.g. at sunset — and syncs `aria-pressed` on every `[data-theme-choice]` button currently in the DOM, phone or desktop) and `setTheme(choice)` (validates, sets `S.theme`, persists via `queueSave()`, applies — the one function both the Profile › Settings screen's own four buttons and the desktop sidebar's segmented control, D7, call). Imports `S` from `../state.js` and `queueSave` from `../storage/meta.js` — a real circular pair with `meta.js` (which imports `applyTheme` back, for `applyPrefs()`'s own theme handling), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. No `main.js` import — `main.js` itself imports `applyTheme` directly (one-directional) to call it once, unconditionally, in the boot sequence, the same shape `setMode('vinyl')` already has there. Since step DA4 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`): `effectiveTheme()` resolves `'nychthemeron'` via a new, one-directional real import of `currentDayPosition` from `../sundown/solar.js` (light for the four phases between sunrise and sunset — morning/midday/afternoon/golden — dark otherwise, per `LIGHT_PHASES`, also exported as `isDaylightPhase(phase)` for `profile/screens.js`'s own switch-time caption); `applyTheme()`'s Nychthemeron branch sets `dataset.theme` to that *resolved* light/dark value (reusing the existing token sets verbatim, not a third palette) while mirroring the literal `'nychthemeron'` to `localStorage`; a new `syncNychthemeronTheme()`, called every tick from `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (the one place already recomputing the day position that often — not a second timer), re-applies on an actual light/dark crossing and briefly adds a `.theme-transition` class (`22-theme.css`) for the doc's own "~1.5s colour transition, none under reduced motion" requirement, skipping both the class and any reapplication when nothing's actually changed, and never animating the very first read after switching onto Nychthemeron; and a new, exported pure `resolveBootThemeFromSunCache(cache, now)` is the testable twin of `theme-boot.js`'s own inlined, import-free copy of the same decision (kept in sync by hand, the same "two sources reviewed together" discipline as `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` vs. `profile/legal.js`).
- `src/theme-boot.js` (step D2; extended step DA4) — the anti-flash head script: a plain classic script (no import/export, like `src/miziki-social.js`), loaded early in `<head>`, before the stylesheet `<link>` tags, so it blocks parsing and sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` from the `localStorage` mirror `src/ui/theme.js` keeps in sync — before any CSS is even applied — for a *pinned* Light, Dark, or (since DA4) Nychthemeron choice. For Nychthemeron, it additionally reads the device-only `miziki-sun-times` cache (`SUN_TIMES_STORAGE_KEY` in `sundown/solar.js`, duplicated here as a literal for the same "stay import-free" reason `THEME_STORAGE_KEY` already is) and resolves light/dark from today's own cached rise/set times — falling back to the fixed 6:50am/8:10pm stand-in (matching `sundown/location.js`'s own `fallbackSun()`) whenever the cache is missing, malformed, or dated anything other than today. Auto needs none of this (no JS at first paint): with no `data-theme` attribute set, `src/styles/22-theme.css`'s own `@media (prefers-color-scheme)` block handles it on its own. The `copy-theme-boot` Vite plugin (`vite.config.js`) keeps it at this same `src/` path under the build output, the same way `copy-social-client` already does for `miziki-social.js`.
- `src/library/model.js` (step 5a) — pure library-indexing helpers, moved out of main.js's "library", "favorites" and "persistence" sections: `byName`/`trackSort` (sort comparators), `groupBy`, `allIdx`, `albumTracks` (per-album auto/title/manual ordering), `idOf`/`idxOf` (track id ↔ index lookups, used by every saved order/playlist). No DOM, no `main.js` import — reads only `S.tracks` and the small per-album prefs on `S`. Since step 02 (F3 spec), `albumTracks`'s manual mode keeps `S.albumOrder[album]` as an array of ids, not positions — an id resolves via `idxOf` and is filtered against the album's current membership for display, but a missing or out-of-album id is never dropped from the saved array itself, so a track's manual position survives being forgotten (`forgetLibrary()`) and reconnects once the same file is re-added. See `src/library/favorites.js`/`src/library/view.js`/`src/library/delete.js`/`src/storage/meta.js`'s own entries for the same change applied to playlists.
- `src/player/queue.js` (step 5a) — the "queue, shuffle, repeat" section plus the "visible queue" section's `advance()`/`preloadNextTrack()`: `buildOrder`, `setQueue`, `reorderQueue`, `preloadNextTrack`, `advance`. The rest of "visible queue" — `queueInsert`/`playNext`/`addToQueue`, `queueRemoveAt`, `queueMove`, and the queue-sheet UI (`renderQueueSheet`/`openQueueSheet`/`closeQueueSheet`) — moved in here too, in step 5e (`nextTrack`/`prevTrack`/`toggleShuffle`/`cycleRepeat`, which happened to sit in the same section, stayed in `main.js` — none of them are visible-queue manipulation). Imports `load`/`play`/`pause`/`seek` from `transport.js` (a real circular import — transport.js imports `advance`/`preloadNextTrack` back from here, the same shape already established between `engine.js` and `transport.js`), `drawTime` from `clock.js`, `sleepStopPlayback` from `sleep-timer.js`, `albumKey` from `tiers.js`, `ensureBuffer` from `buffers.js`, `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny` from `history/sessions.js` (real as of step 5c, previously circular), and five screen functions back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8). Since step D6 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), `renderQueueSheet` is exported and takes an optional `host` (defaulting to `#queueBody`, the phone overlay's own element, unchanged for that call site) — the desktop side panel's "Up next" tab (`shell/player-side-panel.js`) targets it at `#playerSideBody` instead, same render, different host. A new module-private `queueSheetHost` remembers whichever host was last painted into, so `queueMove`/`queueRemoveAt` (a move/remove click made from *inside* whichever one is actually visible) re-render that same one rather than always defaulting back to `#queueBody`. `shell/player-side-panel.js` picks up a real, one-directional import of `renderQueueSheet` from here (`queue.js` imports nothing back from it, so not a circular pair on its own) — but since `player-side-panel.js` is in turn imported by `player/transport.js`'s `load()`, and `queue.js` already imports `load`/`play`/`pause`/`seek` from `transport.js`, the three files together form a real 3-node cycle (`transport.js` → `player-side-panel.js` → `queue.js` → `transport.js`) — unremarkable by this codebase's own standards (`library/view.js` alone has seven such pairs), noted here since it's new as of this step.
- `src/player/buffers.js` (step 5a) — the decoded-audio LRU cache pulled out of the old "clear listening history" section: `touchLRU`, `ensureBuffer`. Imports `tracks` from `storage/repo.js`, `setPathNote` from `ui/path-note.js`, `ensureContext` from `audio/engine.js`. No `main.js` import.
- `src/library/tags/` (step 5b) — the byte-level format parsers that used to be `main.js`'s whole "file loading" section, split by format: `bytes.js` (shared low-level byte/text primitives: `txt`, `u32be`, `synch`, `artURL`, `decodeText`, `clean`), `details.js` (the shared liner-notes/credits helpers every format's `*Details()` uses: `emptyDetails`, `splitNames`, `parsePerformerEntry`, `parseYear`, `creditRoleBucket`), `flac.js`, `mp4.js` (MP4/ALAC/AAC), `mp3.js` (the raw MPEG header `mp3Info()` scans for — ID3 tag/details/artwork reading is in `id3.js`, since ID3 is also how MP3 and AIFF files carry tags), `id3.js`, `wav.js`, and `index.js` — the public surface (`readHeader`, `readTags`, `readDetails`, `findArtworkBlob`, plus `emptyDetails` re-exported for convenience) that dispatches to the right format module by sniffing the file's magic bytes. Pure functions of bytes throughout — no DOM, no `S`, no `main.js` import. None of the per-format helpers (`flacTags`, `mp4Cover`, `id3Details`, etc.) are called from outside this directory; only the five names `index.js` exports are.
- `src/library/import.js` (step 5b) — `addFiles()`'s import loop (and its drag-and-drop file-walking helpers `filesFromDataTransferItems`/`walkEntry`/`readDirEntries`), `addTestTone`, `storeTrack` (moved here from the "metadata editing" section, where it sat only because that's where step 4c's call-site migration found it — see its own changelog entry), and `findDuplicateTrack`/`DUP_DURATION_TOLERANCE_SEC`. "What to do with a File object," not "how to read one" — the byte-level parsing is `library/tags/`'s job. Imports `readHeader`/`readTags`/`readDetails`/`findArtworkBlob`/`emptyDetails` from `tags/index.js`, `askDuplicateResolution` from `duplicates-ui.js`, `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey` from `tiers.js`, `setPathNote`, `ensureContext`, `touchLRU`, `setQueue`/`allIdx`, `storage`/`tracks` from their respective modules, `loadOverlayFor` from `edit.js`, `deleteTracks` from `delete.js`, `rebuildPeopleIndex` from `credits.js` (all real imports as of step 5b/5d/5e), `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (step 7a), and, since step 7d, `renderTracks` from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — no `main.js` import at all, since step 7d. The import loop's behavior is unchanged: one file at a time, the "Importing N of M" note, a failed file skipped (not aborting the batch), new albums sealed once per batch (SEALED spec §2).
- `src/library/duplicates-ui.js` (step 5b) — the single-file duplicate prompt shown during import (`askDuplicateResolution`, `closeDupOverlay`) plus `closeDuplicateScan` (closes the separate library-wide duplicate-scan overlay opened by `openDuplicateScan()`, see `duplicate-scan.js` below — kept here rather than moved there since it doesn't touch `findAllDuplicateGroups`/`renderDupScan`, just the dialog plumbing shared with `askDuplicateResolution()`). Only imports `$` from `util/dom.js` — no `S`, no `main.js` import.
- `src/library/edit.js` (step 5d) — the "metadata editing: sidecar overlay" section: `loadOverlayFor`, `cropSquareImage`, `applyEdit`, `revertTrack`, `applyEditWithUndo`, `undoLastEdit`, the undo banner, and the edit sheet (`openEditSheet`/`closeEditSheet`/`saveEditSheet`). Imports `idxOf` from `model.js`, `albumKey` from `tiers.js`, `overlays`/`artwork`/`storage` from `repo.js`, `applyDiscVariant` from `record-art/looks.js`, `persistSealedAlbums` from `sealed.js`, `renderMiniPlayer` from `shell/mini-player.js`, `deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier` from `crate/tiers.js` (all real), and, since step 7d, `renderTracks`/`exitSelectMode` from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — a real circular pair with it (`view.js` imports `applyEdit`/`showUndoBanner` back from here). Since step 07 (B4), also imports `scheduleBackupSync`/`queueCoverUpload` from `sync/backup.js` (real, one-directional — `backup.js` doesn't import anything back from here): `applyEdit()` calls `queueCoverUpload(id, artBlob)` fire-and-forget whenever a custom cover is set, and `revertTrack()`/`undoLastEdit()`'s "no prior overlay" tombstone branch now writes `cover: null` alongside the existing `fields: null, reverted: true` — a revert clears the backed-up cover claim the same way it clears the text fields, both as one tombstone write. Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), `recomputeTrack(i)` also writes the retagged album (or title) into `#labelTitle` when the edited track is the one currently loaded, so retagging the now-playing track's album keeps the platter label's own title in sync.
- `src/library/delete.js` (step 5d) — the "deletion" section: `countForAlbum`/`countForArtist`, `formatBytes`/`totalLibraryBytes`, `deleteTracks`, `deleteAlbum`/`deleteArtist`, `forgetLibrary`. `deleteTracks()` removes files from Miziki's library only, never from disk; listening history is untouched by design (LIBRARY spec §1 — see the module's own header comment) — since step 02, dropping a deleted id from `S.playlists`/`S.albumOrder` is a plain `.filter()` (items are ids, not positions, see `library/model.js`'s own entry), replacing the old snapshot-to-ids-then-rebuild-to-indices dance the index-based representation used to need. **`forgetLibrary()`'s own meaning changed in step 02 (F3 spec): it now removes only the `tracks` store, this album's crate-art tiers, and thumbnails nothing else still references — `meta`/`sessions`/`overlays`/`achievements`/`collection` are never cleared**, so play counts, sessions, metal/rare progress, playlists (including Favorites), track edits, custom covers, manual order/sort and sealed state all wait and reconnect automatically once a file with the same id (name|size|lastModified) is re-added; "Clear listening history" (`history/clear.js`) stays the only path that resets stats. Imports `idOf`/`idxOf`/`allIdx` from `model.js`, `tracks`/`overlays`/`artwork` from `repo.js` (step 02 dropped the now-unused `meta`/`sessions` imports, since `forgetLibrary()` no longer touches either store directly), `stop` from `transport.js`, `setQueue` from `queue.js`, `stopSleepState` from `sleep-timer.js`, `drawTime` from `clock.js`, `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny` from `history/sessions.js`, `rebuildPeopleIndex` from `credits.js`, `applyDiscVariant` from `record-art/looks.js`, `persistSealedAlbums` from `library/sealed.js`, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (step 7a), `clearSpinDown` from `shell/put-away.js`, `closePlayer` from `shell/player-overlay.js`, `renderMiniPlayer` from `shell/mini-player.js`, `deleteCrateArtTiers` from `crate/tiers.js`, `invalidateCrateModel` from `crate/model.js`, `renderTracks` from `library/view.js` (since step 7d — a real circular pair with it, `view.js` imports `countForAlbum`/`countForArtist`/`deleteAlbum`/`deleteArtist` back from here), and, since step 02, `pruneThumbs` from `library/thumbs.js` (real, one-directional — `thumbs.js` doesn't import anything back from `delete.js`, so not a circular pair) for `forgetLibrary()`'s own "drop thumbnails nothing else needs" step. Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), both the "library now empty after deleting the current track" branch and `forgetLibrary()`'s own full-wipe branch also clear `#labelTitle`'s text alongside the existing `#label`/`#labelArt` clears, so the platter label never shows a stale album title once there's nothing loaded. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 2), imports `touchBackupKey`/`touchBackupMembership` in place of `touchBackupField`: `deleteTracks()`'s playlist-filtering loop now stamps a `touchBackupMembership(p.name, id, 'removed')` tombstone for each id it actually removes from a playlist (so a remote device that still has the track in that playlist loses the merge on this pair specifically) plus one `touchBackupKey('playlistOrder', p.name)` for the order array change; its `albumOrder`-filtering loop stamps `touchBackupKey('albumOrder', a)` for each album whose manual order actually shrank. An explicit deletion is the one place membership is genuinely, permanently dropped — unlike `forgetLibrary()` (F3 spec), which never touches playlists/albumOrder at all, so nothing there needs a tombstone.
- `src/library/duplicate-scan.js` (step 5d) — the "duplicate scan (library-wide)" section: `findAllDuplicateGroups`, `openDuplicateScan`, `renderDupScan` — the "scan my whole library" screen reached from settings, separate from the single-file prompt shown during import. Imports `trackIdentityKey` from `tiers.js` and `deleteTracks` from the real `delete.js` — no `main.js` import.
- `src/library/credits.js` (step 5e) — combines two adjacent sections: "liner notes & credits" (CREDITS spec §1 — `trackHasCreditsContent`, `splitComments`/`commentQualifies`, the credits sheet `renderCreditsSheet`/`openCreditsSheet`/`closeCreditsSheet`, `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent`, `albumLinerNotes`, `albumCreditRoll`) and "credits that link: person view" (CREDITS spec §2 — `openPersonOrArtist`, `creditRow`/`creditNamesCell`/`creditLinkButton`, the lazily-rebuilt `peopleIndex` Map and `rebuildPeopleIndex`, `renderPersonView`). `peopleIndex` itself stays module-private — `personEntryFor(name)` is a new named accessor added for `search.js`'s one lookup (the "People" result group), the same shape as `storage/repo.js`'s `meta.get`/`meta.thumbs()`. Imports `byName`/`albumTracks` from `model.js`, `emptyDetails` from `tags/index.js`, `setQueue` from `player/queue.js`, `closePlayer` from `shell/player-overlay.js`, `showRoute` from `shell/routes.js` (all real), and, since step 7d, `renderTracks`/`songRow` from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — a real circular pair with it (`view.js` imports `trackHasCreditsContent`/`albumLinerNotes`/`albumCreditRoll`/`creditRow`/`renderPersonView` back from here). Since step D6 (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`): `renderCreditsSheet` is exported and takes an optional `host` (defaulting to `#creditsBody`); the `$('#creditsTitle').textContent = t.tags.title` line that used to open the function moved up into `openCreditsSheet` instead, since it's overlay-sheet chrome, not part of "build credits content for this track" — `renderCreditsSheet` itself now also guards a falsy `t` (never reachable from the existing `#trackInfoBtn` call site, but reachable from the desktop side panel before anything has ever played) with a plain "Nothing is playing" fallback instead of throwing. A new `renderLinerNotes(host, album)` — the one genuinely new render function this step adds, since `albumLinerNotes()` itself was always a pure string accessor with no render counterpart of its own on the phone — paints that string (or a "No liner notes in these files." fallback, the exact copy the gatefold's own left-panel minimal-note already uses) into a host. Both are called by the new `shell/player-side-panel.js`'s "Credits"/"Liner notes" tabs, targeted at `#playerSideBody` instead of either the phone's own `#creditsBody` or anything gatefold-specific.
- `src/library/favorites.js` (step 5e) — "favorites: a real playlist, not a separate flag" (CREDITS/FAVORITES spec §4): `isFavorite`, `toggleFavorite`, `updateFavoriteButtons`. Favorites are a system playlist (`S.playlists`, `system:'favorites'`) — the `system:'favorites'` tag has to survive every place playlists are copied (`saveMeta`/`restoreMeta`, `deleteTracks`' id filter) or it's lost on reload. Since step 02 (F3 spec), playlist items are track ids, not positions (see `library/model.js`'s own entry) — `isFavorite`/`toggleFavorite` take a track id and read/write `pl.items` directly with no index lookup at all any more, so the module's `idxOf` import from `model.js` is gone; favoriting a track id that isn't currently in the library (forgotten, not deleted) still records it, same as any other playlist entry. Imports `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (step 7a), `REDUCED` from `util/motion.js` (step 7b), and, since step 7d, `renderTracks` from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — a real circular pair with it (`view.js` imports `isFavorite` back from here). RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) dropped the mini-player's own favorite button (`#miniFavBtn`) — the player already has one (`#playerFavBtn`) — so `toggleFavorite`'s pop-animation and `updateFavoriteButtons()` each touch just the one button now, not a two-element `[$(...), $(...)].forEach(...)` pair. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 2), imports `touchBackupKey`/`touchBackupMembership` in place of `touchBackupField`: `ensureFavoritesPlaylist()` stamps `touchBackupKey('playlistDefs', pl.name)` right after creating a brand-new Favorites playlist (an adopted pre-existing "Favorites" playlist needs no stamp — its def already exists); `toggleFavorite(id)` stamps `touchBackupMembership(pl.name, id, at >= 0 ? 'removed' : 'added')` (the add-wins membership claim for this one `(playlist, track)` pair) and `touchBackupKey('playlistOrder', pl.name)` (the order array itself still changed, since the item was spliced in or out of `pl.items`).
- `src/library/search.js` (step 5e) — combines "library-wide search + sort" (`librarySortBar`/`openSortMenu`/`closeSortMenu`, `librarySortComparator`, `sortAlbumGroups`) and "tag-aware search" (CREDITS spec §3 — `parseSearchQuery`, `trackMatchesField` and its field-specific helpers, `searchLibrary`, `highlightMatches`/`highlightRowLabel`, `renderSearchResults`, `renderSearchChips`, `updateSearchChipsVisibility`). Free text matches artist/album/title plus people/genre/label/catalog; fielded tokens (`artist:`/`album:`/`title:`/`genre:`/`year:`/`label:`/`cat:`/`credit:`/`composer:`/`producer:`/`format:`/`rate:`/`bits:`/`is:`) combine with free text and each other by AND; an unknown field name is just treated as plain text. Imports `byName`/`trackSort`/`allIdx` from `model.js`, `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey`/`trackTier` from `tiers.js`, `emptyDetails` from `tags/index.js`, `setQueue` from `player/queue.js`, `isFavorite` from `favorites.js`, `openPersonOrArtist`/`personEntryFor`/`PERSON_ROLE_KEYS` from `credits.js`, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (step 7a) (all real), and, since step 7d, `renderTracks`/`groupRow`/`songRow` from `library/view.js` instead of `main.js` — a real circular pair with it (`view.js` imports `librarySortBar`/`librarySortComparator`/`sortAlbumGroups`/`renderSearchResults` back from here). Since RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "a quieter player and library"), `librarySortBar()` takes an optional `(count, unit)` and renders one line — "By Artist ▾ · 240 Songs" — where the sort label + chevron is itself the button (replacing the old hamburger-icon toggle plus a separate "Sort: Artist" label); its two call sites (`library/view.js`) now pass the songs/albums count through. `renderSearchChips()`/`updateSearchChipsVisibility()` also changed: chips are hidden until the search field has text (not shown on a bare empty focus any more), and only three (`artist:`/`album:`/`year:`) plus a "More" chip show by default — tapping More swaps in the full set via a module-private `searchChipsExpanded` flag, reset back to collapsed once the field empties out again.
- `src/library/view.js` (step 7d) — the "library" section's row/group renderers and selection/deletion UI helpers, plus `renderTracks()` itself. `renderTracks()` used to physically sit ~900 lines further down in `main.js`, inside the misleadingly-named "S3: gatefold album view" header (which also covered the gatefold and the sealed-record sheet, moved separately into `src/gatefold/` in step 7e — see that pair's own entries below) — purely a physical-position accident. Exports `songRow`, `groupRow`, `enterSelectMode`, `exitSelectMode`, `closeRowMenu`, `rowMenuTrackId` (a module-private `let`, exported read-only — `main.js`'s wiring only ever reads it, never reassigns it, so this doesn't trip rule 7), `openConfirmDelete`, `closeConfirmDelete`, `confirmDeleteAction` (same read-only shape as `rowMenuTrackId`), `applyTrackNumbersInOrder`, and `renderTracks`. Module-private: `artFor`, `toggleEditSelect`, `updateSelectBar`, `openRowMenu`, `togglePick`, `moveIn`, `syncQueueOrder`, `moverCtl`, `sortBar`, `renderAlbumDetail`. Since step 02 (F3 spec), playlist items and `S.albumOrder[album]` are track ids, not positions (see `library/model.js`'s own entry) — `togglePick`, the playlist picking-mode "already picked" check, the playlist's own track-list render and `renderAlbumDetail`'s manual-order render all resolve ids to indices (`idOf`/`idxOf`, now imported from `model.js`) for display, skipping — never dropping — an id whose track isn't currently present; `moverCtl`'s reorder buttons move the track within the full id-based order (its real position there, not its position among only the currently-visible rows), so a hidden/forgotten entry elsewhere in the same list keeps its own relative place. Imports `S`, `$`/`el`, `fmt` from `player/clock.js`, `setQueue` from `player/queue.js`, `trackHasCreditsContent`/`albumLinerNotes`/`albumCreditRoll`/`creditRow`/`renderPersonView` from `library/credits.js`, `isFavorite` from `library/favorites.js`, `queueSave` from `storage/meta.js`, `byName`/`groupBy`/`allIdx`/`albumTracks`/`idOf`/`idxOf` from `library/model.js`, `overlays`/`artwork` from `storage/repo.js`, `applyEdit`/`showUndoBanner` from `library/edit.js`, `countForAlbum`/`countForArtist`/`deleteAlbum`/`deleteArtist` from `library/delete.js`, `isAlbumSealed` from `library/sealed.js`, `albumKey` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `librarySortBar`/`librarySortComparator`/`sortAlbumGroups`/`renderSearchResults` from `library/search.js`, `centerEmptyState`/`renderCrate` from `crate/view.js`, `setPathNote` from `ui/path-note.js`, `updateLibTabsForLayout`/`updateHeaderActionsAvailability`/`refreshLibraryTools` from `shell/header.js`, and, since step D3, `renderSidebarPlaylists` from `shell/sidebar.js` (real, one-directional — `sidebar.js` imports nothing back) — no `main.js` import at all. `renderTracks()` calls `renderSidebarPlaylists()` as its own first line, since every `S.playlists` mutation already lands here regardless of which call site touched it — the one place the desktop sidebar's own playlists list needs to stay in sync from. This is the hub of the whole library screen, so it ends up as a real circular *pair* with six sibling modules (`library/credits.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`) and `crate/view.js` — each of those imports `renderTracks` (and, for three of them, `songRow`/`groupRow` too) back from here, the same peer-import shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js`, just repeated several times since this module is where every library-mutating action's "now redraw the screen" call lands. Since step D5, `renderTracks()` also defaults `#crateGroupToggleTab` (the new desktop-only tab-row crate-order toggle, `miziki.html`/`crate/view.js`) to hidden right after its existing `refreshLibraryTools()` call — the same "hide by default, let the branch that actually applies turn it back on" shape this function already uses for `#crumb` just below — since only the crate branch (`renderCrate()` → `crate/view.js`'s `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()`) knows whether to show it, and `renderTracks()` itself has no per-mode cleanup pass that would otherwise catch a stale "visible" state left over from a prior crate render. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", points 1-2), imports `touchBackupKey`/`touchBackupMembership` in place of `touchBackupField`, at the five call sites that used to stamp a whole field on any playlist/album-order edit: `togglePick(i, node)` (the playlist picking-mode toggle) now stamps `touchBackupMembership(pl.name, id, at >= 0 ? 'removed' : 'added')` for the one track whose membership actually flipped, plus `touchBackupKey('playlistOrder', pl.name)` for the order array change; `sortBar(album)` stamps `touchBackupKey('albumOrder', album)` when it seeds a fresh manual order and `touchBackupKey('albumSort', album)` for the sort-mode change, each independently since they're two different fields now; `renderAlbumDetail`'s own `moverCtl` reorder callback stamps `touchBackupKey('albumOrder', album)`; new-playlist creation stamps `touchBackupKey('playlistDefs', nm)`; and the playlist-items `moverCtl` reorder callback stamps `touchBackupKey('playlistOrder', pl.name)`.
- `src/gatefold/` (step 7e) — the gatefold itself and the sealed-record sheet it shares its tracklist-building code with, both of which had sat inside `main.js`'s misleadingly-named "S3: gatefold album view" header alongside the crate-shelf rendering (moved to `src/crate/view.js`, step 7c) and `renderTracks()` (moved to `src/library/view.js`, step 7d) — see each of those modules' own entries for the full history. Both files together are what the engagement's plan called "the gatefold (~1,450 lines, the biggest single piece)" before those two earlier extractions had already shrunk it to the ~640 lines that moved in this step.
  - `view.js` — the gatefold (GATEFOLD spec §A2-A7): `GF` (the ephemeral open-gatefold state, an exported `let`, read-only outside per rule 7 — `shell/routes.js` is the one external reader), `isGatefoldOpen`, `openGatefold`, `closeGatefold`, `closeGatefoldInstant`, `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`, `updateGatefoldScale`, `gatefoldSwitchPanel`, `buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices` (exported for `sealed-sheet.js`'s own reuse — SEALED spec §5's "the sealed sheet reuses this exact rendering"), `gatefoldSideSplits` (exported for its own unit test, same precedent as `angleDelta` in `scrub.js`, step 3c), `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML` (exported since the design-layout polish pass — the rating/sleeve-note/pin controls builder, reused verbatim by `crate/view.js`'s own desktop detail panel), and `GATEFOLD_ANIM` (the gatefold's own open/close timing, which had sat physically just above the old "S3" header in `main.js`, not inside it, but is gatefold code through and through — moved here alongside the functions that actually use it). Module-private: `gatefoldViewportMode`, `gatefoldNowGlyph`, `gatefoldTrackRow`, `gatefoldCreditLink`, `buildGatefoldLeftPanel`, `gatefoldOnPlatter`, `updateGatefoldSleeveState`, `gatefoldSleeveFlourish`, `gatefoldPlayTrack`, `gatefoldRestoreRowNumber`, `gatefoldInfoAndSleeveHTML`, `gatefoldSetCoverArt`, `gatefoldTeardownGestures`, `gatefoldWireGestures`, `gatefoldSettledState`, `gatefoldSkipToSettled`, `runGatefoldOpen`. Imports `S`/`current`, `$`/`el`, `sleep`, `clamp`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `albumTracks` (`library/model.js`), `albumLinerNotes`/`albumCreditRoll`/`openPersonOrArtist` (`library/credits.js`), `thumbURL` (`library/thumbs.js`), `getCrateArtURL` (`crate/tiers.js`), `fmt` (`player/clock.js`), `setQueue` (`player/queue.js`), `play` (`player/transport.js`, a real circular pair — `transport.js` imports `updateGatefoldNowPlaying` back from here, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`), `runStartSequence` (`shell/start-sequence.js`), `openPlayerViaSheet` (`shell/player-overlay.js`), and `runGatefoldHandoff` (`shell/handoff.js`, a second real circular pair — `handoff.js` imports `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back from here) — no `main.js` import at all. `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML`'s `MizikiSocial` calls reach it as the real global (see `src/main.js`'s own entry), so this file is in `eslint.config.js`'s `MizikiSocial`-global block too. **Bug fix: the module-private `gatefoldPlayTrack()`'s `REDUCED` branch** (picking a different album's track under reduced motion, where the hand-off has no flight to run — the player opens with the record already seated) **now calls `forceCloseLayer('gatefold', {skipHistory:true})`** before opening the player, instead of a bare `forceCloseLayer('gatefold')` — the same `history.go()`/`pushState` race `nav-stack.js`'s `finishClose()` entry above describes (the very next `openLayer('player', ...)`, inside `openPlayerViaSheet()`, pushes a new history entry in the same synchronous tick, so the earlier call's still-pending `go()` would otherwise land on that new entry instead of the one it meant to close, and close the player that was just opened). See `shell/handoff.js`'s own entry for the animated path's matching fix.
  - `sealed-sheet.js` — the sealed-record sheet (SEALSHEET, SEALED spec §5): `isSealedSheetOpen`, `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, `openSealedSheet`, `closeSealedSheet`, `sealedQuietPlay` (exported for the crate's own Play affordances on a sealed record — `crate/view.js`). Module-private: `sealedHandoffPlay`, `closeSealedSheetInstant`. Deliberately not folded into `library/sealed.js`: that module stays about the sealed-records *data* (`S.sealed`, `isAlbumSealed`, the persist calls), independent of listening history by design (SEALED spec §2) — this file is the sheet's own UI, which needs `buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices`/`GF` from `view.js` (`GF` read-only, per rule 7) the same way the gatefold itself does, so it lives alongside it instead. Imports `S`/`current`, `$`, `sleep`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `thumbURL` (`library/thumbs.js`), `getCrateArtURL` (`crate/tiers.js`), `buildSealOverlay` (`library/sealed.js`, a real circular pair — `library/sealed.js` imports `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` back from here for `breakSeal`'s own sheet-fade side effect), `setQueue` (`player/queue.js`), `play` (`player/transport.js`), `runStartSequence` (`shell/start-sequence.js`), `openPlayerViaSheet` (`shell/player-overlay.js`), and `GF`/`buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices` from `./view.js` (one-directional — `view.js` doesn't import anything back) — no `main.js` import at all.
- `src/library/sealed.js` (step 6b) — "S5: sealed records": `restoreSealedAlbums`, `persistSealedAlbums`, `isAlbumSealed`, `breakSeal`, `buildSealOverlay`. `S.sealed` stays its own saved list, independent of listening history on purpose (SEALED spec §2 — "Clear listening history" must never reseal an album). Imports `S`, `el`, `meta` (real), `renderTracks` from `library/view.js` (a real circular pair — `view.js` imports `isAlbumSealed` back from here) for `breakSeal`'s own library-screen re-render, and, since step 7e, `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` from `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` instead of `main.js` — the real circular pair with it described above. No `main.js` import at all, since step 7e.
- `src/library/thumbs.js` (step 6b) — the "persisted artwork: thumbnails" section plus `healDetails` (the liner-notes/credits healer that happened to sit in the same old "file loading"-adjacent area, same batched-re-read shape as `healThumbs` — see its own comment for why it landed here rather than a `tags/`-adjacent module): `restoreThumbs`, `healThumbs`, `pruneThumbs`, `persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`, `snapshotAlbumDisplay`/`snapshotTrackDisplay`, plus the private thumbnail-generation helpers (`makeThumb`, `ensureThumb`, `averageColorHex`, …). `thumbURL` (`'album:<id>'`/`'track:<key>'` → live object URL) is exported as a plain object, not wrapped in an accessor — `main.js`'s own crate/gatefold/sealed-sheet art-fallback call sites (none moved in this step) still read it directly by key, the exact same expression as before the move; nothing reassigns the object wholesale, so this doesn't trip rule 7. `persistable` (the "strip the runtime `art` URL before persisting" helper) is also exported, for `history/achievements.js`'s `upsertAchievement`/`upsertCollectionEntry`. Imports `S`, `$`, `sleep`, `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey` from `tiers.js`, `storage`/`tracks`/`meta` from `repo.js`, `cropSquareImage` from `library/edit.js`, `readDetails`/`emptyDetails` from `tags/index.js`, `rebuildPeopleIndex`/`updateCreditsButtonForCurrent` from `library/credits.js`, and, since step 6e, `renderProfile` from `profile/screens.js` (all real), plus `invalidateCrateModel` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8). Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 1), this module imports `touchBackupKey` instead of `touchBackupField`: `persistAlbumDisplay(keys)`/`persistTrackDisplay(keys)` each now take a key (or an array of keys — `snapshotAlbumDisplay`/`snapshotTrackDisplay` pass a single album id/track key per call, `history/clear.js`'s bulk-clear branches pass an array) and stamp only that/those key(s) via `touchBackupKey('albumDisplay'/'trackDisplay', key)`, in place of the old single `touchBackupField(name)` call that stamped the whole field regardless of which album/track actually changed.
- `src/history/` (step 5c) — the only place that writes listening counters (play counts, session counts, metal-eligible shadow counters, rare unlocks, last-played timestamps). See the persistence rule below.
  - `played.js` — played-duration tracking and the 95% rule: `addPlayedRange`, `playedCoverage`, `checkTrackCompletion`, plus `incrementTrackPlay` (moved here from "metal tiers," its only caller's module). Imports `sessionTrackComplete`/`persistTrackPlayCounts`/`persistTrackMetalEligiblePlays` from `sessions.js`, `snapshotAlbumDisplay`/`snapshotTrackDisplay` from `library/thumbs.js`, `applyDiscVariant` from `record-art/looks.js`, `checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` from `history/achievements.js` (all real as of step 6b, previously circular through `main.js`), and, since step 6e, `persistTrackLastPlayed` from `profile/screens.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` — the one `meta` setter `incrementTrackPlay`/`checkTrackCompletion` have always called. No `main.js` import at all, since step 6e. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `incrementTrackPlay(t)` also tallies `S.phasePlayCounts[phase]` and `S.albumPhaseCounts[albumKey(t)][phase]`, persisting both via two new `sessions.js` imports (`persistPhasePlayCounts`/`persistAlbumPhaseCounts`) — `phase` comes from a new `realDayPosition()` import (`sundown/solar.js`), deliberately **not** `currentDayPosition()`, since that one folds in `S.previewMin` and this file's whole job is counting what actually happened, never what the Preview-the-day slider is showing.
  - `sessions.js` — the "record art: session tracking" section: a qualifying session is one full in-order play-through with shuffle off, zero skips, every track to 95% (spec §7-8). `albumExpectedOrder`, `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`, `touchActiveSession`, `sessionOnLoad`, `sessionTrackComplete`, `restoreSessions`, and the `persist*` functions for session counts, metal eligibility, track play counts, rare unlocks and album look preference. `persistAlbumLastPlayed` moved here too, from the old "profile tab" section — `S.albumLastPlayed` is written only by `sessionTrackComplete`, so its setter belongs beside its one writer. `persistSealedAlbums` moved into `src/library/sealed.js` in step 6b, alongside `restoreSealedAlbums`/`isAlbumSealed`/`breakSeal`, the rest of the cohesive sealed-records unit it was always tightly coupled to (`S.sealed` stays deliberately independent of listening history, SEALED spec §2 — that didn't change, just which module owns the persist call). Imports `albumTracks` from `library/model.js`, and — since step 6b — `applyDiscVariant` from `record-art/looks.js` and `restoreThumbs` from `library/thumbs.js` (both real now; `looks.js` imports `persistAlbumLookPref` back from here, a real circular pair, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`). No `main.js` import at all, since step 6b. Since step DA5, two new thin `meta.put` wrappers — `persistPhasePlayCounts()`/`persistAlbumPhaseCounts()` — sit next to `persistTrackMetalEligiblePlays`, with matching restore logic added to `restoreSessions()`; same shape as every other counter this file already persists. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 1), `persistAlbumLookPref(keys)` now takes a key (an album id) or an array of keys and stamps only those via `touchBackupKey('albumLookPref', k)` for each — `record-art/looks.js`'s `setLookPref(albumId, look)` passes the one album id it just changed; `history/clear.js`'s bulk-clear branches pass an array. Imports `touchBackupKey` in place of `touchBackupField`.
  - `clear.js` — `clearListeningHistory` (the "clear listening history" section, minus the decoded-audio cache that moved separately in 5a) and its helpers. Imports seven `persist*` functions from `sessions.js`, `persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`/`pruneThumbs` from `library/thumbs.js`, `applyDiscVariant` from `record-art/looks.js` (all real as of step 6b), and, since step 6e, `persistTrackLastPlayed`/`renderProfile` from `profile/screens.js` instead of circularly from `main.js`. No `main.js` import at all, since step 6e. Since step DA5, a full-scope clear also resets `S.phasePlayCounts`/`S.albumPhaseCounts` to `{}` (persisting both); the orphaned-only scope prunes `S.albumPhaseCounts` by identity the same way it already prunes `sessionCounts`/etc., but leaves `S.phasePlayCounts` alone on purpose — a flat `{phase: count}` map has no per-identity shape to prune by at all, the same reason that scope already leaves a handful of other flat counters untouched. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 1), `persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`/`persistAlbumLookPref`/`persistTrackDisplay` each now need the keys that actually changed, not a blanket field-level touch — `resetLocalHistory()`'s full-scope branch captures `Object.keys(S.albumDisplay)`/`Object.keys(S.albumLookPref)`/`Object.keys(S.trackDisplay)` *before* wiping each map (so the keys being cleared are still stamped, each as its own per-key tombstone once the matching value is gone), then passes those arrays into the matching `persist*` calls; `clearListeningHistory()`'s orphaned-only branch computes the equivalent `dropped*` arrays (`.filter(id => !albumIds.has(id))`/`!trackKeys.has(k)`, computed before each map's own `filterKeep()` prune) and passes those instead, so only the keys this scope actually drops get stamped — a key this scope leaves untouched never competes with another device's edit to it.
  - `achievements.js` (step 6b) — the "profile tab" section's achievement/Collection/top-lists half (the rendering half stays in `main.js` for 6e): `checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection`, `crateFilled`, `topAlbums`, `topTracks`, `achievementsList`, `collectionGroups`, plus the private `upsertAchievement`/`upsertCollectionEntry`/`albumPlayedThrough`.   - `storefront.js` — the shared card primitives (`storeAwning`, `storeStat`, `storeSwitch`, `storePickTile`, `localAlbumArt`, `timeAgoShort`, `renderOpenStoreCard`, `renderStoreError`, `storeRequestsCard`, `STORE_TIER_LABEL`/`STORE_VIS_LABEL`, `renderStorefrontSection`) plus "my own storefront" (`localStaffPicks`, `renderLocalStorefront`, `loadAndRenderStorefront`, `cycleStoreVisibility`, `renderStorefront`). The primitives are genuinely shared between the profile's own storefront and `social/screens/friend-store.js`'s rendering of someone else's — a judgment call, since the step 6e prompt didn't name this file explicitly, but splitting them out was the only way to give `social/screens/` a real (non-`main.js`) import for them. Imports `S`, `$`/`el`, `normKey`, `albumKey`/`trackTier` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `albumMetalFor` from `record-art/metals.js`, `groupBy` from `library/model.js`, `DB` directly from `storage/idb.js` (the one adapter-shaped exception, same reasoning as the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites from step 4c), plus `persistProfile`/`formatDisplayName`/`renderProfile` from `screens.js` and `beginSocialSignIn` from `settings.js` — a real, 3-way circular cluster among `storefront.js`/`settings.js`/`screens.js` (see below), the same permitted peer-circular-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a, just three modules instead of two. Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `renderStorefrontSection(body)` also appended a universal `.profile-settings-btn` ("Settings ›") at the very top, before any of its three branches — added here, once, rather than inside each branch separately, since before this step only the signed-in `renderStorefront()` branch had its own gear button at all, leaving the other two states (the common `renderLocalStorefront` default, and `renderOpenStoreCard`'s "social enabled but not signed in" state) with no settings entry point whatsoever. **RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Profile is your crate") supersedes that gear's own shape and `renderProfileExtras`' place in this file**: the bare `.profile-settings-btn` is now a real menu button (`.profile-settings-wrap` > `.profile-settings-btn[aria-haspopup="menu"][aria-expanded]` + a `.profile-settings-menu[role="menu"]` holding two items, "Refresh" and "Settings") — point 4's fix for the Refresh/Settings overlap (see `shell/pull-refresh.js`'s updated entry). The menu's "Refresh" item calls a new import, `triggerProfileRefresh` (`../shell/pull-refresh.js`, real, one-directional), in place of a DOM button NV4 used to own; "Settings" still navigates to `S.profileView.screen = 'settings'`. `renderOpenStoreCard`/`renderLocalStorefront`/`renderStorefront` also each dropped their `el('div','store-kicker','YOUR PROFILE')` eyebrow (point 3) and now append a new exported `storeListeningRow()` — a single row reading "Listening ›" that routes to `S.profileView.screen = 'listening'` and calls `renderProfile()` — right after the stats row, replacing the old `renderProfileExtras()` grid cell this file used to import from `screens.js` (gone outright — see that module's own updated entry for where its content moved). `storePicksSection(full)` (point 2) no longer pads staff picks out to a fixed 8 tiles: its heading reads `'Staff picks — ' + full.staffPicks.length + ' of 8'` and it renders exactly one empty "next" slot (not seven) once there are fewer than 8 picks, so a new profile reads as "waiting for its first pin," not "broken." Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces", point 4), that one empty "next" slot's own markup changed from a dashed-border placeholder to a `.store-pick-empty` div holding a `.store-pick-empty-tab` ("+") and a `.store-pick-empty-card` ("Pin a record") — a cream bin-divider tab, the same visual family as the crate's own existing `#` alphabet divider, replacing the old "Slot N of 8" sub-line text (redundant with the heading's own count) with an `aria-label` instead.
  - `settings.js` — the social sign-in flow, now also the **Account screen** (step 06, B3 — `renderSocialSettingsScreen` was the "Settings" sub-screen before this step; it's relabeled "Account" in `screens.js`'s own `labels` map and rewritten to match point 3 of the B3 spec): module-private `socialAuth`, `beginSocialSignIn` (now pre-fills the handle field from the local profile's username via `handleFromUsername()`, triggering an immediate debounced availability check), `closeSocialAuth`, `refreshSocialEntryHost`, the auth steps (`socialField`, `socialPrimaryStack`, `renderSocialAuth` and its per-step renderers — the handle step now shows a live "taken" vs. "reserved" vs. "not quite a valid handle" status line, `checkPrefilledHandle()`, distinguishing all three rather than one generic error), `socialSyncStatusLine`, `renderSocialSettingsScreen` (@handle + email via `MizikiSocial.getMyProfile()`/`getClient().auth.getUser()`, `accountBackupStatusLine()`, `renderDeviceList()` from `listBackupDevices()`, Sign out / Sign out everywhere, a link to the Privacy screen via `wirePrivacyLink()`, and `renderDeleteAccount()` — type-the-handle-to-confirm, calling `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()`, then signing out). Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob", point 4), `accountBackupStatusLine()` checks `getBackupStatus().newerVersionSeen` first, ahead of its existing `waiting`/`lastSyncedAt` checks, and returns the exact string the spec names, `'Another device has a newer Miziki.'` — `src/sync/backup.js`'s `pullAndMergeBackup()` is what actually sets that flag on every pull, whenever at least one fetched row's `schemaVersion` was higher than this build's own `SCHEMA_VERSION` (`sync/backup-doc.js`). The old shareNowSpinning/shareMilestones toggle rows moved to the new Privacy screen below, where point 4 of the spec actually asks for them. Since step 08 (P3, part A), the email step of the sign-in wizard (`renderSocialAuthEmail`) also appends a one-line "By continuing, you agree to our Privacy Policy and Terms of use" note plus `legalLinksRow(renderProfile, closeSocialAuth)` — the one link-row call site that needs the `before` callback (see `legal.js`'s own entry below for why), since tapping either link here has to close the sign-in modal first or it would stay open on top of the profile screen underneath it. Imports `S`, `el`/`$`, `timeAgoShort` from `storefront.js`, `renderProfile` from `screens.js` (part of the same 3-way cluster), and, since step 06, `wirePrivacyLink` from `./privacy.js` and `getBackupStatus`/`listBackupDevices` from `../sync/backup.js`; since step 08, `legalLinksRow` from `./legal.js` (real, one-directional — `legal.js` doesn't import anything back).
  - `privacy.js` (step 06, B3) — the **Privacy screen** (point 4 of the spec), linked from both the profile home and the Account screen: `renderPrivacyScreen(body)` (backup status line, the Download-my-data button, and — once `renderSocialSection` resolves, since it's signed-in-only and loaded async — visibility picker (private/friends/public, default friends), share-now-spinning/share-milestones toggles (default off), follow requests, and blocked accounts with unblock; signed out shows a calm prompt instead of any of the social controls, the same posture `settings.js`'s own "not set up" state already uses elsewhere), `wirePrivacyLink(host, renderProfile)`, `backupStatusLine()`, `visibilityPicker()`, `downloadBlob()`/`todayFilename()` (the Download-my-data button's own save-to-file mechanics — `miziki-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json`).
