The screen's own backup-status line and copy carry the spec's required exact sentence verbatim: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."* **`wirePrivacyLink` takes `renderProfile` as a parameter rather than importing it from `./screens.js`** — the one deliberate design choice in this file: `screens.js` already has a real circular pair with `storefront.js`/`settings.js` (see above), and routing a fourth edge through `privacy.js` as well, on top of this file's own real import of `sync/backup.js`, was enough additional surface to transitively reach `library/view.js`'s whole web during this step's own test runs (see the step 06 changelog's account of the chain-explosion bugs) — passing the already-imported function in from each of the two call sites (`screens.js`'s `renderProfileHome`, `settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) avoids the new edge entirely. Imports `el` (`util/dom.js`), `storeSwitch`/`timeAgoShort` (`./storefront.js`), `getBackupStatus`/`collectDownloadData` (`../sync/backup.js`) — no `./screens.js` import, no `main.js` import. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`, point 3), `renderSocialSection`'s signed-in branch also gains a "Share my listening rhythm" switch (default off, matching the row above/below it), and the file's `../sync/backup.js` import widens to also pull in `pushOrClearListeningRhythm` — the switch's own handler calls `MizikiSocial.setSetting('shareListeningRhythm', on)` (the local flag, the same as the `shareNowSpinning`/`shareMilestones` rows right next to it) and `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(on)` (fire-and-forget, the one call that actually pushes or clears the *shared* snapshot immediately, per the doc's own "updated... immediately when the switch is flipped" rule — see `sync/backup.js`'s own entry above for the daily-interval half this bypasses).
  - `legal.js` (step 08, P3, part A) — the **Privacy Policy** and **Terms of use** screens, linked from the sign-in wizard's first step (`settings.js`), the Account screen (`settings.js`) and the Privacy screen (`privacy.js`): `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen(body)`, `renderTermsScreen(body)`, and `legalLinksRow(renderProfile, before)` — a small "Privacy Policy · Terms of use" footer row, reused by all three call sites. `before`, when given, runs immediately before navigating; only the sign-in wizard's own call site passes one (`closeSocialAuth`, from `settings.js`), since tapping either link there has to close the sign-in modal first — the Account/Privacy call sites just redraw `#profileBody` in place and don't need it. The actual wording lives in `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`, the reviewable source for either page's text — this file is the in-app rendering of the exact same words, kept in sync by hand (there's no markdown renderer in this app, and a build step to generate one from the other would be more moving parts than the two-file-review discipline this project already uses for CLAUDE.md itself), via the same `el()`/`textContent` DOM-building primitives every other screen in the app uses — not because this static, developer-authored text could carry a tag-injection payload (unlike a file's own embedded tags, it can't), but because there's no reason for this one screen to be the sole exception to how the rest of the app builds its DOM. A `LAST_UPDATED` constant drives the "Last updated: …" note both screens open with. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`'s own "What's collected"/"Who can see what" bullet lists mention listening-rhythm sharing (the one-line verdict and per-phase percentages, never raw plays or times, off by default, needing 20 counted plays) — text-only, no code change to this file's own structure. Imports `S` (`../state.js`), `el` (`../util/dom.js`) — no import of `screens.js` (kept one-directional, the same `wirePrivacyLink`-style parameter-passing shape `privacy.js` already established, for the same reason: avoiding a fourth edge into `screens.js`'s already-dense circular-import cluster), no `main.js` import.
  - `screens.js` — `persistTrackLastPlayed` (the one `meta` setter `history/played.js`/`history/clear.js` call), `persistProfile`, `formatDisplayName`, `fmtCount` (not a 999/1.2k-style abbreviator — just `n + ' ' + (singular or plural word)`, picking the word by `n === 1`; tested as "the code's actual rules," per the step 6e prompt's own escape valve, not the prompt's own 999/1.2k example), `topTile`, `renderProfile`, `renderProfileHome`, `renderTopAlbumsScreen`, `renderTopSongsScreen`, `renderCollectionScreen`. **RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Profile is your crate") superseded `renderProfileExtras`** (was exported, the always-visible grid cell below the storefront holding top album/song, metals, collection and the rhythm card): it's now a module-private `renderListeningScreen(body)`, reached from a new `labels.listening = 'Listening'` entry and a new `else if(screen === 'listening') renderListeningScreen(body);` dispatch branch (the first branch checked, since Listening is the one screen every storefront state links to via `storefront.js`'s new `storeListeningRow()`) rather than rendered inline on the profile home screen every time. `renderListeningScreen()` computes `topAlbums()`/`topTracks()`/`achievementsList()`/`S.collection` up front and, when all four are empty (a wholly new profile), renders a single `.profile-empty.profile-empty-first` card ("Play an album start to finish to earn your first metal.") instead of the top row/rhythm-card/achievements/collection/share sections each showing their own separate "nothing yet" box — RF11 point 2's "a new profile looks new, not broken." `renderProfileHome(body, refresh)` correspondingly dropped the rhythm-card host it used to build and refresh alongside the storefront host — the rhythm card (`renderRhythmCard`) moved into `renderListeningScreen()` alongside the rest of the stats it was always peer to. `labels` gained two entries in step 06: `privacy:'Privacy'` and the `socialSettings` tab was relabeled `'Account'` (from `'Settings'`); step 08 (P3, part A) added two more, `privacyPolicy:'Privacy Policy'` and `termsOfUse:'Terms of use'`. `renderProfile()`'s dispatch gained `else if(screen === 'privacy') renderPrivacyScreen(body);` in step 06; `renderProfileHome()` calls `wirePrivacyLink(body, renderProfile)` right after `renderStorefrontSection(body)`, passing itself in for the reason given in `privacy.js`'s own entry above. Since step 08, the dispatch also appends `legalLinksRow(renderProfile)` to the `socialSettings` and `privacy` branches (after their own render call, so the footer sits below each screen's content) and gained two new terminal branches, `else if(screen === 'privacyPolicy') renderPrivacyPolicyScreen(body);` and `else if(screen === 'termsOfUse') renderTermsScreen(body);` — both leaves, nothing dispatches past them. Imports `S`, `el`/`$`, `meta`/`profile` from `storage/repo.js`, `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList`/`crateFilled`/`collectionGroups` from `history/achievements.js`, `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` from `badges.js`, `renderStorefrontSection` from `storefront.js`, `renderSocialSettingsScreen` from `settings.js`, `openShareSheet` from `share/sheet.js`, `renderPrivacyScreen`/`wirePrivacyLink` from `./privacy.js` (since step 06), and, since step 08, `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`/`renderTermsScreen`/`legalLinksRow` from `./legal.js` (all real), and, since step DA4, `setTheme`/`isDaylightPhase` from `../ui/theme.js` and `currentDayPosition` from `../sundown/solar.js` (both real, one-directional — neither imports anything back from `screens.js`), plus `refreshLibraryTools` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; `screens.js`'s only `main.js` dependency). Since step 07 (B4), `persistProfile()` also imports `queueAvatarUpload` from `sync/backup.js` (real, one-directional) — called fire-and-forget whenever `S.profile.artBlob` exists, on every save (including a name-only change), since the re-encode/hash in `queueAvatarUpload`'s own flush cycle is what actually dedupes an unchanged picture, not this call site. Since step DA3 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`, artboard `ProfileSettings`), `labels` gained a `settings:'Settings'` entry and `renderProfile()`'s dispatch gained `else if(screen === 'settings') renderAppSettingsScreen(body);`; the new `renderAppSettingsScreen(body)` (module-private) is the Appearance control's new home, moved off the old Character tab (gone — see `miziki.html`'s own updated entry) — four `[data-theme-choice]` buttons since step DA4 added Nychthemeron (Auto/Light/Dark/Nychthemeron, in a 2×2 grid, `src/styles/17-profile.css`'s `.settings-appearance`), each wired with its own inline click listener calling `setTheme()` directly (imported from `ui/theme.js`) rather than relying on `wireCharacter()`'s existing boot-time `querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` pass, since this screen's buttons are built fresh on every render and that pass can never reach them — the same reasoning the D7 desktop sidebar's own static `#sidebarAppearance` control didn't need to worry about. Since step DA4, the Nychthemeron button alone also gets an `aria-label` ("Nychthemeron: follows sunrise and sunset" — the other three's own visible text already says enough) and the note below the segmented control gained a sentence explaining it plus `nychthemeronSwitchCaption()`'s own one-line "Turns dark/light at HH:MM today" (or "...sunrise tomorrow," if today's own `S.sun.rise` has already passed for the night phases where that's the honest answer — see `src/ui/theme.js`'s `isDaylightPhase` for the light/dark split this reads). **RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) point 5 kept that one-line caption exactly as it was** ("every helper is one line of caption text... keep 'Turns dark at 8:10 PM today.'" — the doc's own example) and added a sibling `<details class="sd-info"><summary>(i)</summary>...</details>` disclosure right after it for the rest of the explanation that used to run on as a second sentence — the same native `<details>`/`<summary>` pattern RF9 established in `miziki.html`'s Character "Starting point" row (`src/styles/25-day-arc-v2.css`'s `.sd-info`), reused here rather than a new disclosure primitive. Below Appearance, three link rows (Account, Privacy, Library tools — the last one opens the existing `#libToolsPanel` disclosure in place, via the same `classList`/`aria-expanded` toggle `src/wiring/library-view.js`'s own `#libToolsToggle` handler already uses, then scrolls it into view) plus the existing `legalLinksRow(renderProfile)` footer. Since RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown becomes a sheet from the platter"), `renderAppSettingsScreen()` also gets a new `row('Sundown', () => openSundownSheet())` — one of the sheet's four entry points (see `shell/sundown-sheet.js`'s own entry) — added before the Account/Privacy rows, since Settings is now one of the ways in rather than the home of the Appearance-adjacent Character tab it used to redirect from; `screens.js` picks up a new, one-directional import of `openSundownSheet` from `../shell/sundown-sheet.js` for it (`sundown-sheet.js` doesn't import anything back from `screens.js`, so not a circular pair).
- `src/social/` (step 6e) — "Social Step D" (the friend-facing screens) plus demo mode, kept apart from `src/profile/` above since neither touches the player's own profile data.
  - `screens/friend-store.js` — the social nav stack (module-private `socialNav`, `pushSocialScreen`/`closeSocialNav`/`renderSocialNavTop`), `popSocialScreen`, a friend's locked/unlocked storefront (`openFriendStore`, `loadAndRenderFriendStore`, `friendStoreHeader`, `renderLockedFriendStore`, `shareFriendStore`, `friendNowSpinningRow`, `friendPicksSection`, `friendFreshSection`, `friendDiggingPreview`, `loadFriendCrate`, `friendCrateSection`, `friendActivitySection`, `renderFriendStoreContent`), and the record sheet (`openRecordSheet`/`closeRecordSheet`, `renderRecordSheetBody`). Imports `$`/`el`, `storeAwning`/`storeStat`/`storePickTile`/`localAlbumArt`/`timeAgoShort`/`renderStoreError`/`STORE_TIER_LABEL` from `profile/storefront.js`, plus `feedEventText` from `home.js` — a real circular pair (`home.js` imports `openFriendStore` back), documented in each file's own header comment, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `renderFriendStoreContent(body, full)` also appends `renderSharedRhythmCard` (new, one-directional import from `profile/rhythm-card.js` — that module doesn't import anything back, so not a circular pair) right after the existing "now spinning" row, gated behind a plain `if(full.listeningRhythm)` — `MizikiSocial.loadProfile()`'s own `get_listening_rhythm()` RPC call already came back `null` for every case this screen shouldn't show anything (not shared, not enough plays, this viewer isn't allowed to see it per the crate's own visibility rules, or signed out), so the truthiness check is the whole gate; no new network call or visibility logic lives in this file. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also imports `spinPhaseWording` from `../../sundown/spin-wording.js` (real, one-directional) — `friendNowSpinningRow(full)` appends the resolved wording ("at golden hour") onto its existing artist/album sub-line whenever `full.nowSpinning.phase` resolves to one, the doc's own "Now spinning [Album] at golden hour" example; a `null`/unresolvable phase (an older row, or one from before migration 008 is applied) just omits the suffix rather than rendering nothing or "undefined". Since RF20 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "fresh from pins and notes; the rating is decoration"), `renderFriendStoreContent(body, full)` also appends a new `friendInCommonSection(full)` — "You both have," `full.inCommon`'s own records (`MizikiSocial.loadProfile()`'s new `records_in_common` RPC call, migration `015_fresh_from_pins_notes.sql`'s sibling `002_rpc.sql` function, previously unused by any client code), each a plain clickable cell opening the record sheet the same way a staff-pick/fresh tile already does — and it's appended **first**, ahead of `friendPicksSection`/`friendFreshSection`, since "what you already share" is the doc's own stated priority over "what's new." A new module-private `storeRuleCaption(text)` builds the one-line caption `el('p','store-rule-caption', text)` every algorithmically-chosen section now carries right under its heading (shared by `friendInCommonSection`'s "Records in both your crates, N total." and `friendFreshSection`'s own new "Records [handle] pinned or wrote about that you don't have, newest first." — wording that matches `fresh_for_you`'s own new pin/note-driven rule, not the old rating-threshold one it replaces). Staff picks keeps no caption of its own — it's a person's own deliberate picks, not a rule's output, so there's nothing to explain.
  - `screens/home.js` — the social home feed: `feedEventText`, `groupFeedEvents`, `homeFeedGroupCard`, `homePeopleSearch`, the module-private spin-state tracking (`homeSpinState`, `homeSpinUnsub`, `homeFollowingHandles`), `homeNowSpinningRow`/`paintHomeSpinRow`, `renderHome`. Imports `$`/`el`, `renderOpenStoreCard`/`storeRequestsCard` from `profile/storefront.js`, plus `openFriendStore` from `friend-store.js` (the circular pair above). Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also imports `spinPhaseWording` from `../../sundown/spin-wording.js` — `paintHomeSpinRow()` appends the resolved wording onto each tile's artist line the same way `friend-store.js`'s own row does, reading `evt.phase`, which `MizikiSocial.subscribeNowSpinning()`'s realtime payload now carries.

  No file under `src/social/screens/` or `src/profile/` other than `profile/screens.js` has any `main.js` import of its own.
  - `demo.js` — `installSocialDemo` only: the fixture data and `MizikiSocial.*` method monkey-patching behind `?social=demo` (dev-only, per CLAUDE.md's own social-config note — must never run in production). Loaded via a **dynamic `import()`** in `main.js`'s `restoreLibrary()`, at the exact point `installSocialDemo()` has always run from, only when the URL has `?social=demo` — this, not a static `import`, is what makes Vite put it in its own chunk (`dist/assets/demo-*.js`, confirmed by build-output inspection) so real users never download it. Imports `S`, `$`, `DB`, `albumKey`/`trackTier`, `albumMetalFor`, `groupBy` — no `main.js` import.
- `src/share/` (step 6d) — the "share image" section, split along its own existing sub-headers. Composed off-screen, not a screenshot of the tab (PROFILE spec §6, §8): each card composer is a pure function of its options, returning a canvas, touching nothing else.
  - `styles.js` — the "style x format system" (Part 2 §2): `SHARE_BG`, `SHARE_FORMATS` (the three formats' pixel dimensions and safe-zone bands), `shareFitText`/`shareFitLines` (auto-shrink/wrap/truncate), `shareSwatchGradient`, `loadImageEl`, `drawCover`, `roundRectPath`, `drawShareWordmark`, `shareGrainTile`, `shareDominantColor`/`hslStr`, `resolveShareStyle` (Night/Cover/Paper → a kit of ink/sub/accent colours plus a `paintBg` painter), `shareChip`. Also `hexMix` and `drawDiagonalWordmark` (the black-square fallback jacket for art-less tracks) — a deliberate judgment call: both sat physically between the two card composers in the pre-step-6d file with no sub-header of their own, and both are used by *both* composers (`hexMix` for the metal-tier disc gradient, `drawDiagonalWordmark` for the fallback jacket), so homing either in just one of `crate-card.js`/`now-playing-card.js` would have made the other import across a card-to-card boundary for no reason — they belong with the rest of the shared style primitives instead. No `S`, no DOM at module top level (only inside functions, once actually called) — no `main.js` import, not even a circular one.
  - `crate-card.js` — the My Crate card (`composeShareImage`): avatar/name/counts, hero = top album, a grid of the next covers, top songs, metals + collection, brand. `opts`: `{style, format, sections}`. Imports `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList` from `history/achievements.js`, `albumKey`/`VARIANT_DEFS` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `METAL_COLORS` from `record-art/metals.js`, the shared primitives above from `styles.js` (since step DA6, also `shareChip`), and, since step 6e, `fmtCount` from `profile/screens.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import at all, since step 6e. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also imports `computeRhythmShares`/`rhythmVerdictLabel`/`RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS` from `sundown/rhythm.js` (real, zero-import module, no new circularity) — once `sections.rhythm !== false` (the new "Listening rhythm" Include toggle, default on) and `S.phasePlayCounts`' own total reaches `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS`, a small chip (`shareChip`, reused verbatim from the FLAC/mode-chip style already used elsewhere) draws the verdict ("NIGHT LISTENER") just above the wordmark — gated the same way the DA5 profile card itself is, inline rather than behind a new exported pure function, the same escape valve this file's own `tests/unit/share.test.js` entry already documents for `composeShareImage`'s hero/top-items selection.
  - `now-playing-card.js` — the Now Playing card (`composePlayerShareImage`): the record peeking from behind its sleeve (`fillShareDisc`, module-private — same look precedence `applyDiscVariant()` uses on the real platter), kicker + title (auto-shrink to 2 lines) + artist + album·year, a live progress line, spec chips, brand footer. `opts`: `{style, format, data}`, `data` gathered by the caller (`renderPlayerShareCanvas`, in `sheet.js`) from `current()`/`S.pos`/`resolveLook()` — the composer itself touches nothing but its arguments. Imports `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey`/`VARIANT_DEFS`/`selectVariant` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor`/`METAL_COLORS` from `record-art/metals.js`, `fmt` from `player/clock.js`, and the shared primitives from `styles.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also imports `spinChipText` from `sundown/spin-wording.js` — appends a "SPUN AT DUSK"-style chip to the existing spec/mode chip row (reusing that row's exact `shareChip` styling, not a new drawing primitive) whenever `data.phase` resolves and `data.includePhase` (the new "Time of day" Include toggle, default on) isn't explicitly off.
  - `sheet.js` — the share sheets' wiring: style/format read from the DOM (the sheet's own chips), everything else (the pixels) comes from the pure composers above. Live preview re-renders are debounced ~100ms so rapid taps don't each trigger a full recompose. `shareSelectedStyle`/`shareSelectedFormat`, `updateShareActionButtons`, `shareDoSave`/`shareDoShare`/`shareDoCopy`, `queueShareRender`/`openShareSheet`/`closeShareSheet`, `queuePlayerShareRender`/`openPlayerShareSheet`/`closePlayerShareSheet`. Imports `composeShareImage` from `crate-card.js`, `composePlayerShareImage` from `now-playing-card.js`, `resolveLook` from `record-art/looks.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also imports `currentDayPosition` from `sundown/solar.js` (real, one-directional) — `renderPlayerShareCanvas()`'s own `data` object gains `phase: currentDayPosition().phase` (not `realDayPosition()`: the doc's own framing is that the card is "a picture of what's on screen," so it follows Preview the day the same way every other on-screen Sundown readout does) and `includePhase: S.playerShareSelection.phase !== false`. Since RF10 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "share cards, one good default"), `openShareSheet()`/`openPlayerShareSheet()` also force their own `#shareMoreBody`/`#playerShareMoreBody` closed (`hidden = true`, `aria-expanded` back to `'false'`) on every open — a prior session's "More options" tap must not carry over into the next open, the same "always starts collapsed" rule the sheet's one visible format choice (Story/Square) already implies. Nothing else in this file changed: `shareSelectedStyle`/`shareSelectedFormat`/the composers still read whatever the DOM (or `S.shareStyle`/`S.shareSelection`/`S.playerShareSelection`) says regardless of whether that DOM happens to be inside the now-collapsible panel — see `miziki.html`'s own updated share-sheet entries and `src/wiring/share.js`'s own entry for the markup/wiring side of this.

  The navigation-shell functions that used to sit under the old "share image" header purely by physical accident (`isLibraryRouteVisible`/`isProfileRouteVisible`, `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`, `updateLibTabsForLayout`, `refreshLibraryTools`, the header search field, the header + menu, and `showRoute`) stayed in `main.js`, now under their own correctly-named header, for step 7's navigation-shell module.
- `src/sync/` (step 03, B2 — pure merge logic; step 06, B3 — the engine that actually wires it in; step 07, B4 — custom-cover/avatar images) — the account-backup merge logic plus the client that calls it. `merge.js` and `backup-doc.js` are still pure functions only, no DOM, no network, no `S` import; `images.js` (new, step 07) is a leaf too. Only `backup.js` touches the network/DOM/IndexedDB. Together they implement the two invariants the B2 doc called out by name: no counter is ever lost or double-counted across devices, and "Clear listening history" wins everywhere once synced — plus, since step 07, "upload once, cache forever" for images (same content, same name, never re-uploaded).
  - `images.js` (step 07, B4) — the one leaf this step adds: `sha256Hex(bytes)` (pure, async — a Blob/ArrayBuffer's SHA-256 as lowercase hex, works identically in a browser and in Node 22+ since the Web Crypto API is global in both, which is what makes it the one unit-testable piece here — `tests/unit/images.test.js`), `prepareCoverUpload(blob)`/`prepareAvatarUpload(blob)` (re-encode through a module-private `cropSquareStepped` — a miniature, duplicated-not-imported copy of `crate/tiers.js`'s own stepped-downscale square crop, kept separate specifically so this file stays a leaf with zero imports of its own — covers: max 1200px @ ~0.85 quality; avatars: 512px square @ ~0.85 — then hash the *re-encoded* bytes, not the source file's, so the upload is named by what actually leaves the device). Re-encoding through canvas strips whatever EXIF/GPS metadata the source photo carried — the file header comment calls this out explicitly, since it matters most for the avatar (the one image in this pipeline that's publicly readable). No `main.js` import, no DOM-free module import either.
  - `backup.js` (step 06, B3; extended step 07, B4 for image upload/download/cleanup) — the sync engine: `scheduleBackupSync(delay)` (debounced ~30s, piggy-backing the same timing `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()` already uses — called from every place a backed-up field actually changes, not just from `saveMeta()`), `flushBackupSync()` (pushes immediately, no debounce — called on backgrounding/`pagehide` by `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`), `pullBackupOnForeground()` (pulls and merges on launch/foreground, never pushes), `initBackupSync()` (the boot-time hookup — does an initial pull-if-signed-in, then wires a `MizikiSocial.on('status', ...)` listener so sign-in later in the session also triggers the first-sync/restore-summary path), `getBackupStatus()` (`{backedUp, lastSyncAt, lastError}`, for the Account screen's "Backed up · X ago"/"Waiting for connection" line), `listBackupDevices()` (every device's own row, for the Account screen's device list), `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` (called from `library/import.js` on every newly-added track — resolves any pending cross-device playlist/albumOrder/overlay/**cover** entry that was waiting on this exact identity key), `collectDownloadData()`/`collectLocalDownloadData()` (the Download-my-data export, B3 point 5), and a small `onBackup(evt, fn)` event emitter (just one event, `'applied'`, fired after a pull merges new data in). Since step D2, `buildLocalState()`'s own `prefs` field is `serializePrefs(S)` with `theme` deleted from the copy before upload — the one deliberate exception to "prefs syncs as a whole, last-write-wins": `S.theme` is device-only by `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s own default, so a pinned appearance choice must never follow the account to a different device the way the rest of `prefs` does. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `buildLocalState()` also carries `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts` straight through (both already portable per-device counters, the same class as `trackPlayCounts`, needing no per-field conversion) — and `pushBackup()` gained one more trailing call, `await maybePushListeningRhythm()`, right after the existing `flushPendingImages()` call: a small, separate section (module-private `RHYTHM_PUSH_INTERVAL_MS`/`ensureRhythmPushedAt()`/`setRhythmPushedAt()`/`buildRhythmPayload()`/`maybePushListeningRhythm()`, plus the one exported `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(on)`) implementing the doc's own "updated at most daily, and immediately when the sharing switch is flipped" rule by piggybacking this file's already-debounced/offline-safe push cycle rather than a timer of its own — `maybePushListeningRhythm()` is the daily-interval-gated half (called from every ordinary push; a no-op unless the Privacy switch is on, `RHYTHM_PUSH_INTERVAL_MS` has actually elapsed since `S.rhythmPushedAt`, and `sundown/rhythm.js`'s `computeRhythmShares()` clears its own `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS` gate), and `pushOrClearListeningRhythm(on)` is the immediate half the Privacy screen's own switch handler calls directly, bypassing the interval check entirely for exactly the two moments the doc calls out by name — turning it on for the first time, and turning it off (which always clears the server column right away via `MizikiSocial.setListeningRhythm(null)`, even when nothing was ever shared — a harmless no-op write, simpler than first checking whether a value exists). Both ends share `buildRhythmPayload()` (`null`, not a thrown error, below the 20-play gate — the ordinary "just signed up" case, not a failure) and both are wrapped in try/catch, the same best-effort posture `cleanupOrphanedImages()` already has, since a hiccup in this one extra write must never turn an otherwise-successful backup push into a failed one. `S.rhythmPushedAt` itself is device-only sync bookkeeping (`meta.put('rhythmPushedAt', ...)`, a direct leaf write, not routed through `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()` — the same `backupUpdatedAt`-style reasoning `queueAvatarUpload()` already established in step 07, for the same "never import `storage/meta.js`" rule this file's own header already states) — never backed up, never shown to the user. `collectLocalDownloadData()`'s own `local` object gained `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts`, read straight off `S`, for the same reason every other local counter is already in that export. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), `pullAndMergeBackup()` bumps `S.backupSeq` to the max of itself and `merge.js`'s new `maxSeqAcross(remoteRows)` right after fetching remote rows and before calling `fromDocs()` — persisted via a direct `meta.put('backupSeq', ...)` only when it actually moves forward — so any edit made after this pull (including one the caller's own post-pull push might trigger) is guaranteed a higher `seq` than every stamp this device has ever observed, on any device, regardless of clock skew. `maxSeqAcross` is imported directly from `./merge.js` (a leaf pure function, the same safe-to-import-directly shape as `crate/tiers.js`/`sundown/rhythm.js` just above it — `merge.js` itself has no imports at all). Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob"), `buildLocalState()` also carries `playlistMembershipAt: S.playlistMembershipAt` straight through (device-local add-wins stamps, converted to identity-key space by `backup-doc.js`'s `convertPlaylistMembership()`, not here). `applyMerged()` was rewritten for the new per-key `merged.itemWrites`/`merged.playlistMembership` shape in place of the old whole-field `merged.lastWrite`: `S.albumSort` is assigned directly from `merged.itemWrites.albumSort`; `S.albumDisplay`/`S.trackDisplay` go through the existing `reattachArt()` helper the same way they always did, just reading from `merged.itemWrites.albumDisplay`/`.trackDisplay` instead of `merged.lastWrite.albumDisplay.value`/etc.; `S.albumEdge`/`S.albumLookPref` are assigned directly; and a new `applyPrefsPatch(rawPrefs)` replaces the old inline `parsePrefs(merged.lastWrite.prefs.value)` call — it first calls `storage/prefs.js`'s `rememberUnknownPrefsKeys(rawPrefs)` (point 4's forward-compat rule — so a key this build doesn't recognize, from a device running a newer build, is cached and echoed back on this device's own next save rather than silently dropped) and only then calls `parsePrefs()` on the result, same as before. Playlists are no longer read straight out of a single merged field at all — a new `composePlaylists(merged)` recombines `merged.itemWrites.playlistDefs`/`.playlistOrder` with `merged.playlistMembership` back into the familiar `[{name, system, items}]` shape `S.playlists` has always held: for each playlist name seen in either map, `items` is `playlistOrder[name]` filtered down to only the identity keys `playlistMembership[name]` currently contains (an order entry for a since-removed member drops out), with any member present in `playlistMembership[name]` but missing from the saved order appended at the end (point 2's "order stays one LWW array per playlist, filtered against the merged membership on apply" — an order array that's stale, missing, or never uploaded by any device still produces a complete, correct playlist). `applyMerged()` calls `applyPlaylists(composePlaylists(merged), byIdentity)` and `applyAlbumOrder(merged.itemWrites.albumOrder, byIdentity)` in place of the old whole-blob reads. `buildRestoreSummary()`'s own playlist count reads `Object.keys(merged.itemWrites.playlistDefs || {}).length` instead of `merged.lastWrite.playlists.value.length`. `pullAndMergeBackup()` also sets `setStatus({ newerVersionSeen: !!merged.newerVersionSeen })` — point 4's other half, surfaced by `profile/settings.js`'s `accountBackupStatusLine()` as "Another device has a newer Miziki." whenever at least one fetched row's own `schemaVersion` was higher than this build understands. Since RF18 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "pushes that can't go backwards"): `pushBackup()` no longer does a plain `.upsert()` — it calls `nextBackupPushSeq()` (bumps `S.backupPushSeq`, a device-only, strictly-per-push counter distinct from `S.backupSeq`'s per-field Lamport clock — see `state.js`'s own entry — and persists it to `meta` *before* the network call, so a push that throws mid-flight still leaves a higher number behind than a later retry of the same attempt would reuse) then calls `client.rpc('put_library_backup', {p_device, p_seq, p_doc, p_device_label, p_schema_version})` (migration `013_backup_seq.sql`), whose own `where doc_seq < excluded.doc_seq` clause makes a stale, late-arriving retry a quiet no-op server-side rather than a race that can overwrite a newer doc this same device already landed. `fetchRemoteRows()` is now a two-pass fetch, point 3's own "unchanged rows aren't re-downloaded": a cheap `select('device_id, updated_at')` cursor pass first, diffed against `B.rowCache` (`{deviceId: {updatedAt, row}}`, persisted to `meta` as `remoteRowCache`, the same lazy-load-once shape as `B.uploadedHashes`); only devices whose `updated_at` actually moved get a second, `.in('device_id', changedIds)`-filtered `select('device_id, doc, updated_at')` for the real doc bytes — every other device's row is served straight from cache. The row cache is cleared on sign-out (`initBackupSync()`'s `signedOut` branch) since `S.deviceId` is a per-browser-install id, not scoped to whichever account is signed in — a different account signing in on the same device later would otherwise risk comparing its own rows' `updated_at` against a stale cache built under a previous account. `runPull()` now takes the same `B.syncing` gate `runPush()` already had, point 2's "a pull's trailing push can't interleave with a debounced one" — `pullAndMergeBackup()` always ends with its own `await pushBackup()`, so without a shared lock that trailing push could race a separately-scheduled `runPush()` for the exact same row. Each caller queues its own kind of retry once the lock clears (`B.again` for a push requested mid-sync, `B.againPull` for a pull), via a small shared `releaseSyncQueue()` rather than duplicating the "finally" logic in both functions. Since RF19 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "verify, and keep a way back", points 1-2): `pushBackup()` computes `const checksum = await sha256Hex(new TextEncoder().encode(canonicalStringify(doc)))` right after building `doc`, and passes it as `put_library_backup()`'s new `p_checksum` argument (migration `014_backup_history.sql`) — a server-opaque value, never checked by Postgres itself, purely for the *next* device that pulls this row to verify against. `fetchRemoteRows()`'s own full-row select widened to also fetch `checksum`; for each row that carries one (a pre-RF19 row has none, and is accepted unverified, same backward-compatible posture every other schema addition in this file already takes), the same `canonicalStringify`/`sha256Hex` pair is recomputed from the fetched `doc` and compared — a mismatch (corruption, a partial write, a doc tampered with in transit) means that one row is dropped from this pull's merge entirely (never cached, never passed to `fromDocs()`) rather than silently merged. A dropped row is reported, not thrown: a module-level `lastChecksumFailure` (reset at the top of every `fetchRemoteRows()` call) is read by `runPull()` right after `pullAndMergeBackup()` resolves and threaded into that call's own `setStatus({..., lastError: lastChecksumFailure})` — not a bare `lastError: null` — since a checksum failure must survive the otherwise-unconditional "pull succeeded" status write that follows it (the rest of the merge still proceeds with every other, verified row, so the pull as a whole didn't throw). Point 2's `validateDoc()` (`backup-doc.js`'s own entry above) runs one layer up, inside `fromDocs()` itself, as a second, independent gate — a row can fail checksum (bytes don't match what was signed) or fail `validateDoc()` (the bytes are intact but structurally nonsensical — a negative counter, a malformed stamp), and either failure alone is enough to drop that one row without touching any other.
    - **Step 07's own additions**: `queueCoverUpload(trackId, blob)` (called fire-and-forget from `library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()` whenever a custom cover is set — re-encodes/hashes via `images.js`, writes `{hash, updatedAt}` onto that track's overlay row, schedules a sync), `queueAvatarUpload(blob)` (same shape, called from `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()` whenever a profile picture exists — re-encodes/hashes, stamps `S.profile.pictureHash`/`touchBackupField('picture')`, persists the profile record), `uploadOne`/`flushPendingImages` (the actual network upload, re-deriving the canonical re-encoded blob+hash fresh from the locally-stored source blob *every* sync cycle rather than persisting a second copy of pre-encoded bytes — deduped only by a lightweight persisted `Set` of already-confirmed `'bucket:hash'` tags, `loadUploadedHashes()`/`persistUploadedHashes()`, so an unchanged image is never re-uploaded), `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)` (pure, exported for its own unit test — covers keep zero unreferenced files, avatars keep the single newest one, per B4 point 5's cleanup rule), `cleanupOrphanedImages`/`listBucketFiles`/`parseHashFromFilename` (the Storage-side half of cleanup, best-effort, wrapped in try/catch), `queueCoverDownload`/`queueAvatarDownload` (download-on-restore — only when a matching local track exists, fetched lazily, written into the local `artwork` store, letting the existing crate-tier/thumbnail code rebuild from there), and `applyCovers` (the per-track-identity-key half of `applyMerged`, mirroring how playlists/overlays already resolve a cross-device claim against whatever's in the library right now).
    - **Never imports `library/view.js`, `record-art/looks.js` or `storage/meta.js`**, even though a successful pull logically wants to re-render the library/refresh the current disc's look, and a profile-picture upload logically wants to flush `S.backupUpdatedAt` the normal way — all three would drag in the single most circularly-connected part of the codebase (`library/view.js`'s seven circular pairs; `record-art/looks.js`'s own circular pair with `history/sessions.js`, which itself reaches `library/thumbs.js` → `library/edit.js` → `library/view.js`'s whole web; `storage/meta.js`'s own chain into `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js` again), which is both a real risk for any future module that imports `backup.js` and, concretely, broke several existing unit tests' mocking during steps 06 and 07 until removed (see each step's own changelog entry for its chain-explosion bugs). Instead: `applyMerged()` calls `emit('applied')`, and `main.js` — which already safely imports `renderTracks`/`applyDiscVariant`/`isProfileRouteVisible`/`renderProfile` — is the one listener; and `queueAvatarUpload()` writes `S.backupUpdatedAt` straight to IndexedDB with `meta.put('backupUpdatedAt', S.backupUpdatedAt)` (the already-safely-imported, true-leaf `meta` from `storage/repo.js`) instead of going through `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()`/`saveMeta()`.
    - **Never awaited from anywhere in the playback path** (refactor rule 2/9's own territory, even though this is a feature PR, not a refactor): `scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync`/`pullBackupOnForeground` are all synchronous entry points that kick off their own internal `async` work and return immediately — nothing in `player/transport.js`'s `play()`, or anywhere else on the tap-to-play path, calls into this file at all. Same for `queueCoverUpload`/`queueAvatarUpload` — both fire-and-forget, never awaited by their own callers (`library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`, `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()`), matching the existing codebase style for background work that must never block a save. The debounce/backoff timers (`BACKOFF = [15000, 30000, 60000, 120000, 300000]`, the same shape and first two values as `miziki-social.js`'s own sync engine) run entirely in the background.
    - **Pending cross-device references** (a playlist/albumOrder item, an overlay, or (step 07) a **cover** that names a track this device doesn't have yet) are kept in a small module-private `B.pending` structure (now `{overlays, playlistItems, albumOrderItems, covers}`), persisted to `meta` — never injected as a bare identity-key string into `S.playlists[].items`/`S.albumOrder[album]`, which the rest of the app assumes only ever hold real local file ids. `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` is what resolves a pending entry (of any of the four kinds) once a matching file actually shows up.
    - Imports: `S`/`touchBackupField` (`../state.js`), `meta`/`overlays`/`artwork`/`profile` (`../storage/repo.js` — `artwork`/`profile` are step 07's own additions, for writing a downloaded cover/avatar into local storage and reading a locally-staged picture back out), `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`../storage/prefs.js`), `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` (`../record-art/tiers.js`), `idxOf` (`../library/model.js`), `toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`resolveIdentityKey`/`SCHEMA_VERSION` (`./backup-doc.js`), `deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier` (`../crate/tiers.js` — step 07, a safe leaf confirmed DOM-free the same way `images.js` is, for rebuilding crate art once a downloaded cover lands locally), `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload` (`./images.js`). Reaches `MizikiSocial` as the real global, never imported (see `src/miziki-social.js`'s own entry), through two tiny defensive accessors (`getSocial()`/`getClient()`) so a test or a signed-out/social-config-empty session never throws trying to call through a missing global — every call site goes through them rather than touching `MizikiSocial` directly.
  - `merge.js` — the generic, doc-shape-agnostic primitives, one per row in the key-inventory table: `sumCounters` (per-device counters, summed), `maxField` (`trackLastPlayed`/`albumLastPlayed`, max), `unionFlags` (`rareUnlocked`, union), `unionEarliest` (`achievements`/`collection`, union by key, earliest earned date wins on overlap), `mergeSealed` (broken anywhere stays broken everywhere), `lastWriteWins` (one winner per *field*, not per whole doc — `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/`profileName`/**`picture`** (step 07) each carry their own `{seq, at, deviceId, value}` (since RF16, see below — was `{at, value}`), so a later unrelated field never clobbers an earlier one), `mergeOverlays` (per-track-identity-key, last write — edit or revert-tombstone — wins), `mergeCovers` (step 07, B4 — the same per-track-identity-key last-write-wins shape as `mergeOverlays`, kept as its own primitive rather than folded in, since a cover change and a tag change are independent edits with their own timestamps; a `null` hash is the tombstone, winning exactly like any other newer value), `computeHistoryClearedAt`/`freshRows` (the clear-wins-everywhere gate), and `mergeRows(rows)`, the one entry point that composes all of the above over every row for a user — its return now also includes `covers: mergeCovers(rows)` and `lastWrite.picture: lastWriteWins(rows, 'picture')`. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), also exports `sumNestedCounters(rows, field)` — the same per-device-sum rule as `sumCounters` above, one level deeper (`{albumId: {phase: count}}` rather than a flat `{key: count}`), kept as its own primitive rather than teaching `sumCounters` to recurse, since every other per-device counter in this file is genuinely flat and `phasePlayCounts` itself (flat) uses plain `sumCounters` directly with no new primitive needed; `mergeRows(rows)`'s own return gained `counters.phasePlayCounts`/`counters.albumPhaseCounts` alongside its existing counter fields, from exactly these two calls. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), `lastWriteWins(rows, field)` no longer compares `at` alone — it picks the winner via a module-private `isNewerStamp(a, b)` that compares `seq` first (a Lamport counter, `S.backupSeq`/`src/state.js`, immune to clock skew — a stamp missing `seq` entirely, from a v2-or-earlier doc, reads as `0` and always loses to any real edit), then `at` (the original tiebreak, now only reached by two edits that genuinely never observed each other), then `deviceId` (a final, deterministic string comparison, so two stamps tied on both `seq` and `at` still resolve the same way regardless of row order — `mergeRows`' own "any order, same answer" invariant needs a last tiebreak that can't itself depend on order). Also new: `maxSeqAcross(rows)`, a small exported pure helper scanning every `lastWrite[*].seq` across every given row for the highest value seen — `src/sync/backup.js`'s pull path uses it to bump this device's own `S.backupSeq` forward on every pull, so its next edit is guaranteed to out-rank everything it has just seen. Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob"), `lastWriteWins`/`isNewerStamp` are superseded for every field except `profileName`/`picture`, which stay exactly as RF16 left them (`lastWrite: {profileName, picture}` is all `mergeRows`' own return still carries under that key). `isNewerStamp` is now exported (point 1's "per key, the way `mergeOverlays` already works" needs the same comparator one level down, not a second copy of it) and reused by two new primitives: `mergeKeyedLastWriteWins(rows, field)` (point 1 — the per-key sibling of `lastWriteWins`: every row's `itemWrites[field]` is a flat array of `{key, seq, at, deviceId, value}` entries rather than one whole-field stamp; for each key seen across every row, the entry with the newest `isNewerStamp` wins, and a key whose winning entry carries no `value` at all — i.e. was deleted, not merely never written — is left out of the returned map entirely, so a tombstoned key simply doesn't appear, the same "absence is the deletion" shape `mergeOverlays`' revert tombstone already established) and `mergePlaylistMembership(rows)` (point 2 — the add-wins CRDT: every row's `playlistMembership` array holds `{playlist, identityKey, added, removed}` entries; for each `(playlist, identityKey)` pair, the single globally-latest of its `added`/`removed` stamps (by the same seq→at→deviceId total order) decides whether that pair is currently a member — returned as `{playlistName: [identityKey, ...]}`, one array per playlist, in no particular order). `maxSeqAcross(rows)` was widened (via a small internal `bumpMax(map, entry)` helper) to also scan `row.itemWrites`' six per-field arrays and `row.playlistMembership`'s own `added`/`removed` stamps, not just `row.lastWrite` — a seq bump that only looked at the two remaining whole-field stamps would badly undercount what a pull actually saw, letting this device's own next `S.backupSeq` land far too low. `mergeRows(rows)`'s own return replaced every one of its old per-field entries (except `profileName`/`picture`) with `itemWrites: {albumOrder, albumSort, albumDisplay, trackDisplay, albumEdge, albumLookPref, prefs, playlistDefs, playlistOrder}` (each via `mergeKeyedLastWriteWins`) and `playlistMembership: mergePlaylistMembership(rows)`. No imports at all.
  - `backup-doc.js` — the doc format and the local-state ↔ doc conversion: `SCHEMA_VERSION` (**2** as of step 07 — was 1), `toDoc(localState)`, `fromDocs(ownLocal, remoteRows)`, `resolveIdentityKey(key, tracks)`. `localState` is a plain snapshot shaped like the relevant slice of `S` (not `S` itself), documented in full at the top of the file — this is what keeps the module importable and testable without a browser or a database. The only real import is `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` from `record-art/tiers.js`, the same portable keys duplicate detection and the play counts already use — `toDoc` converts `playlists`/`albumOrder` items and `overlays` from local file ids (`name|size|lastModified`, meaningless on another device) to these keys on the way out; an id that doesn't currently resolve to a track (forgotten but kept, per F3) is kept as the bare local id rather than dropped, since there's nothing left to derive an identity key from — not portable to another device, but still reconnects on the *same* device after a reinstall, the same way F3's own id match already does. `fromDocs` builds this device's own fresh doc internally (never trusting a possibly-stale copy of itself inside `remoteRows` — any row sharing `ownLocal`'s own `deviceId` is dropped, and remaining rows are de-duplicated by `deviceId` too, so even a duplicate row from the server can never double-count), then calls `mergeRows`. Its result's `totals.*` are the cross-device grand totals for *display only* — they must never be written back into this device's own per-device counters, or the next upload would double-count what's already synced; its `ownReset` flag is true exactly when another device's clear is newer than this one's, meaning the caller must zero its own local history counters and adopt the newer `historyClearedAt` before the next upload. `resolveIdentityKey` isn't called by `fromDocs` itself — resolving a pending identity-key entry to a real local track has to happen at *apply* time, against whatever's in the library right now, since a resolution computed once at merge time would go stale the moment the next file is imported. **Step 07's own additions**: a new `convertCovers(overlaysRaw, idToKey)` (parallel to, but kept separate from, `convertOverlays` — a cover claim and a tag claim are independent, each with its own timestamp; a whole-overlay revert tombstones the cover too, `{hash: null}`, at the same `revertedAt`; a plain tag-only edit that never touched the cover emits no cover claim at all), feeding a new top-level `covers` array in the doc; and `lastWrite.picture`, using the exact same `lw()` helper every other last-write-wins field already uses. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), `lw(updatedAt, field, value, deviceId)` takes a fourth argument and returns `{seq, at, deviceId, value}` instead of `{at, value}` — `updatedAt[field]` is `src/state.js`'s `touchBackupField()` stamp, itself now `{seq, at}` rather than a bare number (a falsy/bare-number stamp, from before this field existed on this device, reads `seq`/`at` both as `0`); `deviceId` is attached once per `toDoc()` call from the doc's own top-level `s.deviceId`, not stored redundantly inside the stamp at touch time, since one `toDoc()` call/row is always exactly one device's contribution and `S.deviceId` can still be `null` long after a field was first touched (before the very first sign-in). Every one of the ten `lw()` call sites inside `toDoc()` now passes `s.deviceId` as that fourth argument. A v1 doc (no `covers`/`picture` at all) still loads without throwing — every reader already treats a missing field as "no claim" by construction (`row.covers || []`, etc.) — see `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js`'s own schema-v1-compatibility tests. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `toDoc()` also writes `counters.phasePlayCounts`/`counters.albumPhaseCounts` straight through from the local snapshot (`s.phasePlayCounts || {}`/`s.albumPhaseCounts || {}`) — both already portable, flat-or-one-level-deep maps keyed by phase name and by `albumKey()`, so neither needs the identity-key conversion `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`overlays` require; a v1/pre-DA5 doc missing either is read as the empty map by the exact same `|| {}` convention, with no schema-version bump needed for this addition (unlike step 07's image fields, since nothing here changes what a reader must assume about a field's *presence*, only adds two more that default safely to empty). **RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob") bumps `SCHEMA_VERSION` to 3** and replaces every one of the old whole-field `lw()` calls (except `profileName`/`picture`, which keep the RF16 shape exactly as it was) with per-key conversion. A new `itemWriteEntries(valueMap, stampMap, deviceId)` builds one field's `itemWrites` array: one entry per key seen across either the current value map or the stamp map, each `{key, seq, at, deviceId, value}` — a key with a stamp but no current value becomes a tombstone (`value: null`, point 1's "a removed key is a null tombstone"); a key with a value but no recorded stamp at all (data older than RF17, or a field this build has never called `touchBackupKey()` for) falls back to `{seq:0, at:0}`, the same "untouched data always loses to a real edit" convention RF16 established for a bare/missing stamp. `toDoc()` calls it for all seven map fields (`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`) plus, via a new `buildPlaylistDefsAndOrder(playlists, idToKey, stamps)`, `playlistDefs`/`playlistOrder`. A new `convertPlaylistMembership(playlists, membershipAt, idToKey)` builds point 2's add-wins membership array: every item *currently present* in a playlist asserts its own `'added'` claim (a real stamp from `S.playlistMembershipAt` if one was ever recorded for that `(playlist, localId)` pair, or the same `{seq:0, at:0}` fallback otherwise — present-but-unstamped still outranks no-claim-at-all, so pre-RF17 membership doesn't silently vanish on the first post-upgrade sync); a second pass then emits a tombstone (`removed`) for any `(playlist, localId)` pair recorded in `S.playlistMembershipAt` but no longer present in `playlists` (a local remove), so a remote device whose own copy still has that pair loses the merge. An entry whose local id doesn't currently resolve to a track is dropped — nothing a remote device could match it against. A new, module-private `upconvertToV3(doc)` is the forward side of "SCHEMA_VERSION 3, with one upconverter" (point 1's own opening line): given any doc whose `schemaVersion` is below 3 (a no-op, returning the doc unchanged, for one already at 3+), it explodes each of that doc's old whole-field `lastWrite[field]` stamps into the one-entry-per-current-key shape `itemWriteEntries()` would have produced had that device always written per-key (every key inherits the one stamp the whole field had — correct, if coarser, since a whole-blob write from before RF17 genuinely did touch every key in that blob at once), and does the same for `lastWrite.playlists` (exploding it into `playlistDefs`/`playlistOrder`/`playlistMembership` entries, one `added` membership claim per item, all sharing the blob's own one stamp). `fromDocs(ownLocal, remoteRows)` now reads each remote row's own `schemaVersion` first: a row *higher* than this build's own `SCHEMA_VERSION` is left out of the merge entirely (point 4's forward-compat rule — never guessed at or misparsed) and the whole call's return carries `newerVersionSeen: true`; every row this build does understand (its own current doc, plus any remote row at schemaVersion ≤ 3) is passed through `upconvertToV3()` before `mergeRows()` ever sees it, so the merge path itself only ever has to handle one (current) shape. See `src/sync/merge.js`'s own entry for `mergeKeyedLastWriteWins`/`mergePlaylistMembership`, the two merge-side primitives this per-key doc shape feeds. Since RF19 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "verify, and keep a way back"), also exports `canonicalStringify(value)` (a pure, key-order-independent `JSON.stringify` — object keys sorted recursively, array order left alone — so two devices that build the identical doc never disagree on its checksum just because `Object.keys()` iteration order differs) and `validateDoc(doc)` (point 2's structural guard: non-negative-integer checks on every counter map and max-field map, string checks on `deviceId`/sealed-id arrays, shape checks on every `itemWrites.<field>` entry, `playlistMembership` entry and `overlays`/`covers` entry, and a lenient `isStampLike()` for `lastWrite.profileName`/`.picture` that treats a missing `seq`/`deviceId` as valid — not required — since a `playlistMembership` stamp built by `convertPlaylistMembership()` never carries a `deviceId` at all, and a v1/v2 doc's `lastWrite.profileName`/`.picture` predates RF16's own `seq` field entirely; both are ordinary, if maximally low-priority, stamps to `merge.js`'s own `isNewerStamp()`, so `validateDoc` has to agree). `fromDocs()` calls `validateDoc()` on every row right after `upconvertToV3()`, skipping (never throwing on) a structurally invalid one the same way a too-new `schemaVersion` is already skipped — a bad row never gets a vote in the merge.
- `tests/unit/` holds `node:test` unit tests for pure functions extracted out of `main.js` (`tiers.test.js`, `solar.test.js`, `engine.test.js`, `clock.test.js`, `scratch-processor.test.js`, `idb.test.js`, `prefs.test.js`, `repo.test.js`, `library-model.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `tags.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `search.test.js`, `metals.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `share.test.js`, `profile.test.js`, `meta.test.js`, `shell.test.js`, `crate-geometry.test.js`, `crate-model.test.js`, `launch.test.js`, `library-view.test.js`, `gatefold.test.js`, `backup-merge.test.js`, `sidebar.test.js`, `theme.test.js`, `day.test.js`, `arc.test.js`, `contrast.test.js`, `backup-restore.test.js`), run via `npm run test:unit`. Separate from `tests/client.test.js`, which needs Postgres. `engine.test.js` and `clock.test.js` use Node's module-mocking (`node --experimental-test-module-mocks`, see Commands) to replace whichever real modules in their import chain would otherwise need a browser (`main.js`'s DOM-dependent top-level code) or hang the test process (`clock.js`'s real `setInterval` — see the step 3c changelog entry) — each mocks the *whole* module it needs to avoid pulling in, not just its own direct imports, since `mock.module`/`mock.timers` replace by resolved specifier/global, not per-importer. `scratch-processor.test.js` instead loads that file's source with Node's `vm` module (it has no `export`, so nothing to `import`) into a sandbox stubbing the bare `AudioWorkletProcessor`/`registerProcessor`/`sampleRate` globals its top level needs — see F2. `idb.test.js` tests the real `DB` against `fake-indexeddb` (the second dependency, after Vite — see Commands), giving each test a fresh `IDBFactory` instance assigned to the bare `indexedDB` global so tests can't see each other's databases — see step 4a and "Persistence rules" below for what it protects. `prefs.test.js` needs none of that (`serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` touch no DOM, no database, no `S`-the-global — `serializePrefs` takes a plain object shaped like `S`) — it round-trips a fake prefs object, loads old/partial saved objects missing newer fields, and feeds back wrong types, unlisted enum values and `null`/`undefined` to confirm bad or missing values are left out rather than thrown on — see step 4b. `repo.test.js` uses the same `fake-indexeddb`/fresh-`IDBFactory` approach as `idb.test.js` to round-trip every store through the real interface, confirm `meta.get`'s unwrapping and `meta.thumbs()`'s filtering, and confirm the storage-unavailable case returns the same `false`/`null`/`[]` `DB` itself does — see step 4c. `library-model.test.js` needs none of the above — it imports `model.js` directly, no mocking, and checks `byName`'s case/accent-insensitive natural-number ordering, `trackSort`'s disc/track/title order, `groupBy`'s grouping, and `albumTracks`'s three sort modes (auto/title/manual, including the manual-order append/drop-stale-entries behavior) — see step 5a. `queue.test.js` mocks `main.js`, `transport.js`, `clock.js`, `sleep-timer.js`, `audio/engine.js` and `ui/path-note.js` the same way `engine.test.js` does, to load `queue.js`/`buffers.js` without a browser, then checks `buildOrder`'s shuffle (every index exactly once, the given "first" kept at the front), `reorderQueue`'s shuffle-on/off behavior, `advance`'s repeat off/all/one branches at the end of the queue (using `mock.fn()` on the mocked `play`/`pause`/`seek` to assert which ran), and `ensureBuffer`/`touchLRU`'s LRU eviction (stubbed decoding; the 3-track cap; the currently-playing track is never evicted even when it's the least recently used) — see step 5a. Since step 5e, it also mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a capable stub (`appendChild`, `addEventListener`, cached by selector so, e.g., `openQueueSheet` and a later `$('#queueOverlay')` read the same object) and checks `playNext`/`addToQueue` (insert position, moving rather than duplicating an already-queued track, a no-op on the currently-playing track) and `openQueueSheet`/`closeQueueSheet`'s overlay toggle, run through the same `renderQueueSheet` tree-building the real UI uses. `tags.test.js` builds small synthetic byte fixtures in the test itself (no real music files committed) to check `readTags`/`readHeader`/`readDetails`/`findArtworkBlob` against FLAC and ID3v2 (title/artist/album/track, a UTF-16 text frame, FLAC STREAMINFO's sample rate/channels/bit depth, a FLAC PICTURE block and an ID3 APIC frame, and that a file with neither returns no artwork), that truncated or garbage bytes never throw and fall back to the empty/default result, and `findDuplicateTrack`'s duration tolerance — it needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js` to load `library/import.js` (where `findDuplicateTrack` lives) without a browser, even though that one function only reads `S.tracks` — see step 5b. `history.test.js` mocks `main.js` with the union of what `played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js` need from it (since `played.js` and `clear.js` are loaded for real, and `played.js` itself pulls in the real `sessions.js`), plus `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` (so `clear.js`'s one `$('#route-profile')` call doesn't need a real `document`) — then checks `addPlayedRange`/`playedCoverage` (contiguous merges, a scrub-skip leaving a real gap, overlapping replays not double-counted, the 0.05s joining tolerance), `checkTrackCompletion` (counts once at ≥95% coverage, never twice for the same play-through, never below 95% even at the track's end, never for a zero-duration track), and `clearListeningHistory` against `fake-indexeddb` (full scope wipes every counter and leaves the library/other settings alone; orphaned-only scope drops just the identities no longer in `S.tracks`) — see step 5c. `edit.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub element (settable `textContent`/`style`, a real `classList`) rather than pulling in a real `document`, plus `main.js` with what `edit.js` needs from it directly, then checks `applyEdit` (writes fields onto the track and into the overlay store; a second edit merges onto the existing overlay rather than replacing it; retagging a sealed album carries the seal to the new album id and persists it, an unsealed one never touches `S.sealed`), `revertTrack` (clears the overlay, restores the embedded tags) and `applyEditWithUndo`/`undoLastEdit` (undo restores the exact prior overlay when one existed, or clears it entirely when there was none) against `fake-indexeddb` — see step 5d. `delete.test.js` needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js`, since `delete.js`'s real `setQueue` import pulls in the same `player/queue.js` → `buffers.js` → `audio/engine.js` chain — it checks the pure helpers (`countForAlbum`/`countForArtist`, `formatBytes`, `totalLibraryBytes`), `deleteTracks` against `fake-indexeddb` (removes only the given tracks; un-seals an album that just lost its last track and persists that, but leaves a still-populated album's seal alone; clears `S.index` when the playing track is deleted; an empty id list is a no-op; playlist/queue entries referencing a deleted track are dropped while others survive by identity, not by stale index), `forgetLibrary` (clears the whole library and every session/metal counter, but keeps achievement/collection thumbnails in `meta`), and `findAllDuplicateGroups` (`src/library/duplicate-scan.js` — pure and import-free beyond `S`/`tiers.js`, tested here alongside `deleteTracks` rather than in a fourth mock-heavy file, since it hands `deleteTracks` its "keep newest only" choice) — see step 5d. `favorites.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub (for the "pop" animation's `classList`/`offsetWidth`) and `main.js`'s `queueSave`/`renderTracks`/`REDUCED`, then checks `isFavorite`/`toggleFavorite` (creates the system `favorites` playlist on first use; adopts a pre-existing user playlist literally named "Favorites" rather than duplicating it; toggles on and back off; `ensureFavoritesPlaylist()` still runs, but no item is added, for an id no longer in `S.tracks`; reads `S.playlists` by the *current* index, not a remembered id — surviving a reorder is `deleteTracks`' job, not this module's) and `updateFavoriteButtons` — see step 5e. `credits.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to the same capable stub `queue.test.js` added (for `renderPersonView`'s/`creditRow`'s tree-building), stubs the bare `document.createTextNode` global directly (`creditNamesCell` calls it without going through `util/dom.js`), and needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js` since `credits.js`'s real `setQueue` import pulls in the same chain — it checks `trackHasCreditsContent` (true for any of: a credit-role bucket, performers, genre/year/label/catalog/isrc, or *any* non-empty comment, even a rip-tool signature — that junk filtering is `renderCreditsSheet`'s job, not this gate's), `openPersonOrArtist` (routes to the artist view for an exact library-artist match, the person view otherwise, and remembers `personReturn` when leaving a non-person view), `creditRow`, `openCreditsSheet`/`closeCreditsSheet`, `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent`, `albumLinerNotes` (longest qualifying comment across the album), `albumCreditRoll` (de-duplicated by role + name across every track), and `rebuildPeopleIndex`/`renderPersonView` (every track a person is credited on, routed through the mocked `songRow`; the "no credits found" fallback for an unknown name) — see step 5e. `search.test.js` needs the same DOM/chain mocking as `credits.test.js` (plus a `document.createElement`/`createDocumentFragment` stub for `openSortMenu`/`highlightMatches`, which also call the bare global directly) — it checks `parseSearchQuery` (splits a known field token from free text; an unknown field name stays plain text), `searchLibrary`'s field matching (`artist:`, `year:` exact/comparison/range, `rate:`/`bits:` with a `k` suffix, `credit:`, `is:favorite` delegating to `favorites.js`, and that an unknown field name is never a filter), `librarySortComparator`/`sortAlbumGroups` (`added` and `sessions` ordering), `highlightMatches`, and `renderSearchResults`/`renderSearchChips`/`updateSearchChipsVisibility` (the "no matches" fallback, grouped results calling the mocked `songRow`, one chip per field/smart-filter, and chips hiding once the search box has focus and text) — see step 5e. Since step 6b, `clock.test.js`/`queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js`/`history.test.js`/`edit.test.js`/`delete.test.js`/`credits.test.js`/`search.test.js` all needed their `main.js` mocks adjusted the same way: `applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs`/`persistSealedAlbums`/`checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` stopped being `main.js` exports, so each test now mocks `record-art/looks.js`/`library/thumbs.js`/`library/sealed.js`/`history/achievements.js` directly for whichever of those names its own real chain still needs (loading any of the four for real would reach `library/edit.js` and, through that, `library/sealed.js`/`library/credits.js` again, each still circularly importing a few names from `main.js` — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as the existing `audio/engine.js` mocks, just one layer further out now). `metals.test.js` mocks `library/thumbs.js`'s `restoreThumbs` (so loading `record-art/looks.js` — needed for its `resolveLook` tests — doesn't reach `library/thumbs.js`'s real chain via `history/sessions.js`) and checks `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor` at, just below and just above each threshold in `ALBUM_METAL_THRESHOLDS`/`TRACK_METAL_THRESHOLDS` (read from the exported arrays, not hardcoded numbers, so the test tracks the code's own thresholds), and `resolveLook` (rareUnlocked overrides an earned metal and the plain variant; with no rare unlock an earned metal wins over the variant; a saved look preference wins while still available and falls back cleanly once it isn't) — see step 6b. `sealed.test.js` mocks `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`/`renderTracks` (sealed.js's own circular import) and `library/thumbs.js`'s `restoreThumbs`/`thumbURL`/`persistable` (reached via `history/achievements.js` → `history/sessions.js`) and checks `isAlbumSealed` (true right after import, false once the sealed-records setting is off), `breakSeal` (clears the seal once, calls both of its side effects; breaking an already-broken seal is a no-op), `restoreSealedAlbums` (grandfathers to "none sealed" with no saved row — no `fake-indexeddb` needed, since `DB.ok` stays false and `meta.get` resolves `null` by the same "storage unavailable" design every other module relies on), and `topAlbums`/`topTracks` from `history/achievements.js` (count order, ties broken by most-recently-played, an orphaned id still getting a display fallback) — see step 6b. Since step 6c, `clock.test.js`'s and `engine.test.js`'s `main.js`/`player/background.js` mocks needed the same kind of adjustment step 6b's mock-chasing did: `bgPosition` and `applyOutputRoute` stopped being `main.js` exports (real imports from `player/background.js` instead), so each test's `main.js` mock dropped that key and a new `player/background.js` mock (or, in `clock.test.js`'s case, letting the real `background.js`/`controls.js` load, since their own dependencies were already satisfied by existing mocks) took its place. `controls.test.js` (step 6c) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a small stub (`dataset`, `title`, `classList`, matching `queue.test.js`'s shape), `history/sessions.js`'s `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`, and the same `main.js`/`player/transport.js`/`player/clock.js`/`player/sleep-timer.js`/`ui/path-note.js`/`player/background.js` mocking `queue.test.js` needs (since `controls.js`'s real `advance`/`reorderQueue` import pulls in the real `player/queue.js`) — then checks `cycleRepeat`'s exact cycle order (off → all → one → off, read off the running code rather than assumed), `toggleShuffle` (flips `S.shuffle` both ways while leaving `S.playing`/`S.pos`/`S.index` untouched and never calling `play`/`pause`/`seek`), `setMode` (a fake `S.source`/`S.nodes` confirms Pure routes to `master` and Vinyl to `wobble`), and that `setMode` leaves F1's Pure-rate rule intact (`computeRate()` from `sundown/solar.js` stays exactly `1` in Pure regardless of `S.auto`, and Vinyl still follows the manual rate afterward) — see step 6c. `share.test.js` (step 6d) needs no mocking at all — `src/share/styles.js` has no imports of its own, DOM-free at module top level — and sticks to the two genuinely pure parts of the share-image code, since canvas drawing itself can't run in Node: `SHARE_FORMATS`' per-format pixel dimensions and safe-zone bands, and `resolveShareStyle` returning a valid kit (ink/sub/accent/chip colours plus a `paintBg` function) for every one of the nine style × format pairs — `resolveShareStyle('cover', ...)` without a real DOM `Image` constructor rejects internally and falls through to the Night kit by design, so even the Cover branch is exercised without a browser. There is no separately-testable "pick the hero record / top items" function — `composeShareImage`/`composePlayerShareImage` read `topAlbums()`/`topTracks()` directly inline, woven into the same function that does the canvas drawing — so per the step 6d prompt's own escape valve this isn't tested here; the selection algorithm those two calls use is already covered by `sealed.test.js`'s `topAlbums`/`topTracks` tests. Since step 6e, `clock.test.js`/`queue.test.js`/`history.test.js` each needed one more mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's and 6c's did: `persistTrackLastPlayed` (and, for `history.test.js`, `renderProfile` too) stopped being `main.js` exports once `history/played.js`/`history/clear.js` started importing them for real from `src/profile/screens.js` — each test dropped those two keys from its existing `main.js` mock and added a new `mock.module` targeting `src/profile/screens.js` directly instead, so loading the real `played.js`/`clear.js` doesn't pull in the real profile/social chain just to satisfy those two names. `profile.test.js` (step 6e) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `el`/`$` to a tiny stub element (`appendChild`/`childNodes`, matching `favorites.test.js`'s shape) and stubs the bare `document.createElement` global directly (`metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` call it without going through `util/dom.js`), then mocks `profile/storefront.js`, `profile/settings.js`, `share/sheet.js`, `history/achievements.js` and `main.js` (for `refreshLibraryTools`) directly — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, since loading any of the first four for real would reach the rest of the profile/social chain (and, through `settings.js`, `social/screens/home.js`) just to read `fmtCount` or the two badge functions. It checks `fmtCount` (singular vs. plural word selection — explicitly not a 999/1.2k-style abbreviator, testing "the code's actual rules" per the step 6e prompt's own escape valve) and `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` (a rare tier with art uses the art as a background image; a rare tier without art falls back to a plain gradient; a metal tier like gold/bronze uses `metalBackground()`/`variantBackground()` and a capitalized or record-named label; art appends a real `<img>` to the badge) against the real `METAL_COLORS`/`VARIANT_DEFS` tables, so the fixture data used stays in sync with what the app actually ships. Since RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Profile is your crate"), the `history/achievements.js` mock's `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList` read from mutable module-level `let mockTopAlbums/mockTopTracks/mockAchievements` (reassignable per test, in place of three fixed-empty-array closures) so a single test file can drive both branches of the new `renderListeningScreen()` (`profile/screens.js`, the old exported `renderProfileExtras` made module-private and routed through a `screen === 'listening'` dispatch); the file's existing NV4 "build once, refresh in place" tests were updated to expect one profile-home child host, not two (the rhythm-card host moved off the home screen into Listening, so `renderProfileHome` no longer builds a second one) and zero rhythm-card calls on the home screen; and two new tests cover `renderListeningScreen` itself — a wholly-new profile (every one of `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList`/`S.collection` empty) renders a single consolidated `.profile-empty.profile-empty-first` card rather than one empty box per section, and the full breakdown (top row, rhythm card, achievements, collection, share) renders once anything at all exists. `meta.test.js` (step 7a) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub element, `audio/mode.js`'s `setMode`, `audio/engine.js`'s `applyCharacter`, `sundown/location.js`'s `applyVolume`, `player/controls.js`'s `toggleShuffle`/`cycleRepeat`, `player/background.js`'s `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` and `main.js`'s `attachLaunchSettingRow` directly, plus the bare `document.querySelectorAll` global — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, since `applyPrefs()` (reached via `restoreMeta()` whenever a saved prefs row exists, which `saveMeta()` always writes) calls all of these for real, and `setMode` alone would otherwise need `document.documentElement.style.setProperty`. Against `fake-indexeddb`, it checks the full `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` round trip (playlists, manual album order and album sort survive, read back by id rather than position), a playlist entry whose track no longer exists is dropped rather than thrown on, `queueSave()`'s 600ms debounce (several calls collapse into exactly one `saveMeta()` run, confirmed by spying on `meta.put`'s `'playlists'` calls), and that with storage unavailable all three functions quietly no-op — see step 7a. Since step 7a, `clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `engine.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` each needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's/6c's/6e's did: `queueSave` stopped being a `main.js` export once `player/background.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`library/search.js`/`library/delete.js`/`library/favorites.js`/`library/import.js` started importing it for real from `src/storage/meta.js` instead — and `storage/meta.js` itself real-chains into `audio/mode.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`player/controls.js`/`player/background.js` for its own `applyPrefs()` helpers, each of which could in turn reach wide chains (`player/queue.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `library/sealed.js`'s own circular `main.js` import) this suite already stops at the door elsewhere. Since step 7d, `clock.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` each needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape as every prior step's: `renderTracks` (and, for several of them, `exitSelectMode`/`songRow`/`groupRow` too) stopped being `main.js` exports once `library/edit.js`/`library/credits.js`/`library/favorites.js`/`library/search.js`/`library/sealed.js`/`library/delete.js`/`library/import.js`/`crate/view.js`/`player/transport.js` started importing them for real from `src/library/view.js` instead — and `view.js` itself real-chains widely (reaching, for instance, `shell/put-away.js`'s own `shell/mini-player.js` needs, which some of these tests' existing `mini-player.js` mocks didn't provide), so each affected test added a direct `mock.module` for `src/library/view.js` (providing just the names its own chain needed) rather than trying to patch every downstream module's own mock. `gatefold.test.js` (step 7e) stubs `globalThis.window = { matchMedia(){ return {matches:false}; } }` (for `util/motion.js`'s `REDUCED`, the same stub `shell.test.js` already uses) and `globalThis.document = { documentElement: { style: { setProperty(){} } } }` (for `GATEFOLD_ANIM`'s own CSS-custom-property side effects at module load), then mocks `library/credits.js`, `library/thumbs.js`, `crate/tiers.js`, `player/clock.js`, `player/queue.js`, `player/transport.js`, `shell/start-sequence.js`, `shell/player-overlay.js`, `shell/handoff.js` and `library/sealed.js` directly, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning — `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`'s own `buildSealOverlay` import would otherwise reach the real `library/sealed.js`, which reaches the real `library/view.js` (the step 7d hub, seven real circular pairs) just to satisfy one name neither of this file's two tested functions (`gatefoldSideSplits`, `isSealedSheetOpen`) ever touches.
