It checks `gatefoldSideSplits` (exported for this test, same precedent as `angleDelta` in `scrub.js` — fewer than 4 tracks never splits; `S.albumSides` off never splits regardless of count; a single disc of 4+ splits at the midpoint; multiple discs split by disc, not by midpoint) and `isGatefoldOpen`/`isSealedSheetOpen` (both read `false` with nothing open — the module-private `GF`/`SEALSHEET` state isn't otherwise exercised here, since opening either is a DOM- and `requestAnimationFrame`-driven choreography with no meaningful pure core beyond `gatefoldSideSplits`, the same "not much here is pure" shape `shell.test.js` found for `shell/put-away.js`; the Playwright script covers the open/close/hand-off sequences end to end instead). No *existing* test file needed a mock-chain fix for this step: `GF`/`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`/`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`/`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`/`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` were never mocked as plain `main.js` exports by any test in this suite before this step (the gatefold had no test coverage of its own, and the handful of tests that did mock `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` — `clock.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js` — moved that one key to a new `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` mock, the same "stop the chain at the door" move, since `library/sealed.js`'s import of it is real now instead of circular). `forget-library.test.js` (step 02, F3 spec) is a new file, using the same mocking scaffolding `delete.test.js` already established (plus a `profile/screens.js` mock, for its one `clearListeningHistory` smoke test) — against `fake-indexeddb` and the real `storage/repo.js`, it checks `forgetLibrary()` empties the `tracks` store while `meta`/`sessions`/`achievements`/`collection` and the `overlays`/`artwork` records for an edited track and its custom cover are all untouched; that a stats field forgetLibrary leaves alone in memory persists its *current*, not a stale, value on a later `persistSessionCounts()` call (the write-back bug step 5d flagged, now provably fixed rather than just not-regressed); and, resolving ids through `idxOf` by hand (no real DOM, so no re-render to inspect), that a playlist's ids survive Forget untouched and resolve back to indices once matching tracks reappear in `S.tracks` — fully, and partially with the rest staying recorded rather than dropped. Since step 02, `library-model.test.js`'s two manual-album-order tests, `favorites.test.js`'s two id-vs-index tests, `meta.test.js`'s playlist/album-order round-trip and "entry no longer exists" tests, and `delete.test.js`'s playlist-survives-deletion test were all rewritten for the same id-not-position representation (`S.playlists[].items`/`S.albumOrder[album]`) — `delete.test.js`'s own two old `forgetLibrary` tests moved to the new file outright rather than being patched in place, since the behavior they checked no longer exists. `delete.test.js` and `tags.test.js` also each added `pruneThumbs` to their existing `library/thumbs.js` mock, since `delete.js`'s `forgetLibrary()` (and, transitively, `library/import.js`'s real `deleteTracks` import) needs that export to resolve now. `search.test.js`'s one `is:favorite` test was updated to seed the Favorites playlist with a track id instead of an index, for the same reason. `backup-merge.test.js` (step 03, B2) needs none of the mock-chasing above — `src/sync/merge.js` has no imports at all, and `src/sync/backup-doc.js`'s only import (`record-art/tiers.js`, for `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey`) is already DOM- and database-free — so every row and local-state fixture in this file is a plain object literal. It checks the counter invariants (sum across devices; three devices merged in every order of four permutations give the same total; repeated/duplicate syncs never inflate it; this device's own `localState` is never mutated and never ends up holding another device's counts), overlay merge (a later revert beats an earlier edit and vice versa; an overlay for a track not currently present is skipped, not thrown on), playlist portability (an item for a track that only exists on another device survives the merge as its identity key; `resolveIdentityKey` finds it once a matching track is "imported"), the history-clear gate (a newer clear on one device zeroes another's merged totals and sets `ownReset`; once that device adopts the new `historyClearedAt`, its next play counts from zero again; achievements/Collection/rareUnlocked from a stale row are excluded the same way; sealed records are untouched by any of this), sealed-record merging (broken anywhere stays broken after merging with a device that still has it sealed), last-write-wins field isolation (a later change to one field never clobbers an earlier change to an unrelated field), robustness (`toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`mergeRows` on empty or old-format input never throw), a round trip with no other devices, and that Forget Library on one device (F3) leaves its stats counting in full once merged — see step 03's own changelog entry for the key-inventory table this file's test names track. Since step 07 (B4), 9 more tests cover the image-backup additions, using the same plain-object-literal fixtures (no new mocking needed, since `mergeCovers`/`convertCovers`/`lastWrite.picture` are all as DOM- and database-free as everything else in this file): `covers` merging by last-write-wins per track identity key (a later cover change beats an earlier one regardless of row order; a revert tombstone, `hash: null`, wins over an earlier cover set the same way an overlay tombstone does; a tag-only edit with no `cover` field makes no cover claim at all; an unresolvable local id is skipped, not crashed on); `picture` as a plain last-write-wins field (a later change on another device wins; an unrelated, later change to a different field never clobbers an earlier picture change — the same per-field isolation test already covers for `prefs`/`albumSort`, just for the new field); and two schema-v1-compatibility tests confirming a doc with neither `covers` nor `picture` at all — the exact shape every pre-step-07 device's last upload would have had — still merges without throwing, with `merged.covers` empty and `merged.lastWrite.picture` reading `null` (this device's own fresh, current-schema doc always contributes a claim, even an unset one, so a v1 remote row simply has nothing to compete with — it never leaves `lastWrite.picture` literally `undefined`) rather than any value the v1 row couldn't have supplied. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), the fixtures that feed `baseLocal()`'s own `updatedAt` overrides for the `lastWriteWins` tests were updated to the new `{at: N}` stamp shape (`src/state.js`'s `touchBackupField()` now writes `{seq, at}`, not a bare number, so a plain `updatedAt: {playlists: 500}` fixture would otherwise silently read as `at: 0` once `lw()` reads `.at` off a shape that isn't there) — the pre-existing v1-compatibility fixture at the schema-v1 test above needed no change, since a hand-built `lastWrite: {playlists: {at: 1, value: []}}` entry (no `seq` at all) is exactly the "stamp without seq" case `lastWriteWins`'s own `isNewerStamp` already treats as `seq: 0`. Two new tests: a device with a clock an hour behind still wins after editing something it just pulled (seq beats `at`, in either row order), and three rows with identical `seq`/`at` give exactly one winner regardless of row order (the `deviceId` tiebreak). Since RF17 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "merge per item, not per blob"), `baseLocal()` gained a default `playlistMembershipAt: {}` field (point 2's local add-wins stamps), and every test that previously asserted against the old whole-field `merged.lastWrite.playlists`/`merged.lastWrite.albumSort`/etc. shape was rewritten for the new per-key `merged.itemWrites.*`/`merged.playlistMembership` shape — `profileName`/`picture` are the two fields RF17 leaves untouched, so their own existing `merged.lastWrite.*` tests needed no change at all. New tests: three for per-key last-write-wins itself (an unrelated, later change to one field never clobbers an earlier change to a different field; a later change to one key of a map field never clobbers an earlier change to a different key of the *same* field — two different albums' own `albumSort` entries both survive regardless of which stamp is newer, since neither competes with the other's key; a key present as a stamp but absent from the current value map becomes a tombstone that drops out of the merged map entirely); the spec's own required "two offline hearts both survive" case (two devices each heart a different track into the same playlist while offline — neither ever saw the other's `added` stamp, so both identity keys end up in `merged.playlistMembership`, with no tombstone competing against either); and a v2-doc upgrade test (a hand-built `schemaVersion: 2` doc, the exact old whole-blob `lastWrite` shape with no `itemWrites`/`playlistMembership` at all, merges through `fromDocs()`'s internal `upconvertToV3()` cleanly — every key that field's value once held inherits the whole-blob's own one stamp, and the old `lastWrite.playlists` blob explodes into `playlistDefs`/`playlistOrder`/`playlistMembership` entries the same way). `prefs.test.js` gained one more test for point 4's forward-compatibility rule: `rememberUnknownPrefsKeys()` (`storage/prefs.js`) caches a pref key this build doesn't recognize, and `serializePrefs()` echoes it back verbatim on every subsequent call, while `parsePrefs()` itself still drops it when reading the saved blob back into `S` (echoing on save, not on read, is the whole mechanism — see `storage/prefs.js`'s own entry). `sidebar.test.js` (step D3, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`, new, 4 tests) needs none of the mock-chasing above either — `src/shell/sidebar.js` only touches `S`/`$`/`el`, so this mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny, DOM-free `fakeHost`/`fakeEl` pair (an object literal collecting whatever `appendChild` is called with, so a test can assert on the resulting children directly) rather than pulling in a real `document`. It checks the empty-library fallback row, that a system playlist (Favorites) is pinned first, the doc's own required test case (a playlist name containing `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` renders as a single `textContent` string, never parsed as markup — the fake host has no `innerHTML` parser to misuse in the first place, so a regression here would show up as the payload going missing, not as a real `<img>` appearing), and that a missing `#sidebarPlaylists` host (not yet in the DOM) is a quiet no-op. `day.test.js` (step DA1, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`, new, 14 tests) needs no mocking at all — `src/sundown/day.js` has no `main.js`-adjacent import, only the equally pure `solar.js` — so it imports `dayEvents`/`dayPosition` directly and `sunProgress`/`easedProgress`/`computeRate` from `solar.js`. It checks `dayEvents()` against two published-time fixtures (Columbia, MD and Sydney, Australia, both for real dates with real published sunrise/sunset times, within a 5-minute tolerance — the same "cite a real published source" precedent `solar.test.js` already set) and the ordering/never-null invariants (`dawn` before `rise`, `set` before `dusk`, `noon` never `null`); `dayPosition()`'s 48-hour, per-minute continuity across midnight, dawn, sunrise, sunset and dusk (no jump larger than a tiny epsilon anywhere in the walk); 1am reading full night and noon reading full day for Columbia; the dawn phase's own "percent risen" shape at its midpoint, with the phase's two boundaries (dawn itself, sunrise) checked explicitly; that the twilight phase's progress is byte-identical to what `sunProgress()` already returned before DA1 (the "legacy partial data" fallback, exercised directly); polar day (sun never sets, `rateProgress` 0 across six sampled hours) and polar night (sun never rises, `rateProgress` 1 across the same six) using a real high-Arctic fixture (Svalbard, June and December); the `S.sun.ok === false` default (full day, the same conservative "no data yet" `sunProgress()` already had); the fallback stand-in times (`sundown/location.js`'s own fixed numbers, fed in as a plain events object here rather than through `fallbackSun()` itself, which needs `clock.js`'s/`storage/meta.js`'s heavier import chain just to resolve one name each — deferred to Playwright and code review instead, the same "not much here is pure" escape valve `shell.test.js`'s own entry already used for `shell/put-away.js`'s choreography); and that Pure always returns exactly `1` through `computeRate()` regardless of dawn/pace, with Moment holding back longer than Window during dawn the same way it already does during twilight. Since RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), `solar.test.js` gained 5 more tests for the new `depthKeyFor`/`depthCaptionFor` pure mapping (auto-off always reads Off regardless of target; each of the three auto targets maps back onto its own Gentle/Medium/Deep button; an unrecognized saved target falls back to Medium, not Off; Off's own caption names no rate; each depth's caption names its own rate and semitone count), and `meta.test.js` added a `src/sundown/dial-view.js` mock (`updateToneCurveVisibility(){}`) to its existing "stop the chain at the door" list, since `applyPrefs()`'s own restore-time sync now calls it — the real module's top-level `util/motion.js` import reads `window.matchMedia`, which this file's own fake `document` provides no `window` for. Since RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), `routes.test.js` was rewritten for the sheet-based `showRoute('sundown'/'character')`: its `routeEls`/`tabbarBtns` fixtures dropped `sundown` in favor of `home` (the tab bar's own three-tab shape), and a new `openSundownSheet` mock (`shell/sundown-sheet.js`) replaces the old "redirects to the sundown route and selects Sound" test with one asserting `showRoute('sundown')`/`showRoute('character')` each call `openSundownSheet` with the right view and leave whichever `.route` was already visible untouched — `selectSundownView`/`lastSundownView`'s own tests are unchanged, since RF26 didn't touch either function. `clock.test.js`'s `fakeEl()` helper gained a plain `dataset: {}` property, since `drawSun()` now writes `dataset.phase` onto a few more mirrored elements (`#sunKnob`/`#miniSunBtn`) and the bare stub object had nothing for that assignment to land on. `profile.test.js` added a direct mock for `shell/sundown-sheet.js` (`openSundownSheet(){}`) — the same "stop the chain at the door" move as every other mock-chasing step in this file's own history: `profile/screens.js`'s new real import of `openSundownSheet` would otherwise reach the real, unmocked `shell/routes.js` → `social/screens/home.js` → `friend-store.js` → `profile/rhythm-card.js` chain, which this test's existing `rhythm-card.js` mock doesn't provide every export for. `contrast.test.js` (RF12, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 4 — "a small script computes contrast for every `color:` declaration resolved against both themes' backgrounds and fails below 4.5:1 for text under 18px") needs no mocking at all — it only reads the real `src/styles/*.css` files off disk and does plain regex/arithmetic, no DOM, no `S`. It parses the dark token map straight off `00-base.css`'s bare `:root{}` and the light overrides off `22-theme.css`'s `:root[data-theme="light"]{}`, resolves `var(--token)` chains (including a fallback argument) recursively per theme, then scans every stylesheet for leaf-rule `color:` declarations via a flat `/([^{}]*)\{([^{}]*)\}/g` match (correct regardless of `@media` nesting, since a wrapper's own `{` before any leaf rule is just unmatched text to this pattern) — explicitly excluding `:root`/`@`-rule selectors and anything targeting an `<svg>` (an icon's `color` drives `stroke:currentColor`, not text, and RF12 point 1 is a *text* rule; point 2's `.tabbar-btn svg` staying `--accent` is a deliberate exception, not a gap). **It deliberately scopes itself to the one token family RF12 is about** — `--accent`/`--accent-text`/`--amber`/`--miziki-orange`, plus any hardcoded hex literal equal to one of those tokens' own resolved value (how it caught `03-sleeve-pull.css`'s `.heart-btn` bug — a raw `#FF9E2C` the original `var(--accent)` grep sweep couldn't see) — rather than every arbitrary `color:` declaration in the stylesheets: an early version that checked everything produced dozens of false positives on pre-existing, already-shipped, unrelated colors (`--on-accent` text meant to sit on a solid accent fill, not the sky; star-rating amber, sticker/disc-label/warning colors, each already paired with its own opaque card background) that were never part of RF12's remit and would have made the test noise rather than a gate. For each matching declaration under 18px (or with no explicit `font-size` at all, treated conservatively as small), it computes the WCAG contrast ratio against both `--sky1` and `--sky2` in both themes — reusing `docs/design/refine/README.md`'s own stated "lower of the two sky stops" methodology — and fails if any resolvable, opaque instance comes in under 4.5:1. **RF14 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 5)** added one more test to this file, since the Day view's two label surfaces never actually sit directly on the moving ring/sky and so don't need a per-minute sweep of their own (that's `clock.test.js`'s job, below, for `--muted`/`--muted-2`): it parses `25-day-arc-v2.css`'s `.day-dial-label` rule and checks its `color`/`background` pair (`--on-accent` on `--amber`, the disc's own time readout — fixed, theme-invariant) clears 4.5:1 in both themes, and asserts `.day-dial-hourlabel` still reads `color:var(--paper)` — a regression guard that it stays off the ring (RF12's own fix) rather than reading a color that would need its own sweep. `clock.test.js` (RF14) added the `mutedGradientStops` tests themselves: `skyGradientStops`'s existing golden-anchor test was updated for the cooled dark anchor (point 2 — `rgb(61,42,31)` → `rgb(53,42,36)`), and new tests cover `mutedGradientStops`'s per-anchor contrast floors (≥4.5:1 muted / ≥3:1 muted-2, both themes), its exact warm/cool literal values per phase in dark (golden/twilight warm; night/midday/dusk cool — note `pos('twilight')` at its *start*, continuous with Golden's own warm value, not `pos('twilight', 1)`, which legitimately blends into Dusk's own cool value as twilight ends), light theme's one flat pair regardless of phase, a 10-minute-sampled sweep across a real day, and `drawSun()`'s own inline `--muted`/`--muted-2` writes (both themes, and that a repeated call skips the redundant write, the same assertion the pre-existing `--sky1`/`--sky2` test already made). `arc.test.js` (RF14, point 3) covers the rewritten `skyRingStops()`: empty with no/partial sun data, stops sorted ascending and closing the ring from minute 0 to 1440, and each of the seven named anchors holding flat at its own exact hex across its real span — including a dedicated regression test for the Midday/Afternoon boundary (solar noon must fall strictly *inside* the Midday span, not exactly on its boundary with Afternoon, where a conic-gradient's own same-position stop-ordering would otherwise silently resolve to the second-listed color — see `sundown/arc.js`'s own entry for the bug this test caught and how the fix mirrors `day.js`'s own noon/sunset-midpoint boundary rule). Since RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces"), `controls.test.js`'s own `globalThis.document` stub needed one more mock-chasing fix of the same shape as every prior step's: `setMode()` now calls `document.documentElement.classList.toggle('mode-pure', ...)` alongside its existing `style.setProperty` call (for the platter label's Pure-mode flatten), so the stub gained a bare `classList: { toggle(){} }` to match — no other test file's mocks needed touching for this step. Since RF19 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "verify, and keep a way back"), `backup-merge.test.js` gained 13 new tests for `canonicalStringify` (key-order independence, array-order preservation, a real doc's own checksum round-trip) and `validateDoc` (accepting a real `toDoc()` output and a v1-doc upconverted to v3; rejecting a negative or non-integer counter, a missing/non-string `deviceId`, a non-string sealed entry, a malformed `itemWrites`/`playlistMembership` stamp) plus one `fromDocs` integration test confirming a structurally invalid remote row is dropped and never contributes to the merged totals. `backup-sync.test.js` and `backup-reset.test.js` both now mock `sync/images.js` wholesale (a fast, non-cryptographic `fakeSha256Hex` stand-in, plus no-op `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload`) rather than letting `pushBackup()`'s real checksum computation reach the real `crypto.subtle.digest` — real SHA-256 is a genuinely variable number of event-loop ticks to resolve (confirmed empirically), which doesn't compose reliably with either file's own fixed-iteration `flushMicrotasks()` helper and `backup-reset.test.js`'s own `mock.timers`-based fake timers, and flaked the pre-existing RF18 "changed `updated_at` triggers a fresh full fetch" test once the real async checksum call was added to every push; `tests/unit/images.test.js` already covers that the real `sha256Hex` really is SHA-256, so nothing is lost by mocking it out here. `backup-sync.test.js` also gained 4 new tests of its own (a push's own `p_checksum` matches `canonicalStringify(doc)`; a pull accepts a matching-checksum row; a pull drops a mismatched-checksum row and surfaces it via `getBackupStatus().lastError` without throwing; a pull accepts a no-checksum, pre-RF19 row for backward compatibility), each remote row given its own distinct `device_id`/`updated_at` so RF18's own row cache (module-level state that persists across tests in the same file) can't silently serve a stale cached row left over from an earlier test. `backup-restore.test.js` (new, point 4's "a real restore test") is the one `backup-*.test.js` file that leaves `storage/repo.js`/`storage/idb.js` genuinely real, backed by a fresh `fake-indexeddb` `IDBFactory` per test (`tests/unit/idb.test.js`'s own pattern) rather than the in-memory `Map` stand-in every sibling file uses — since `sync/backup.js`'s own import list is deliberately narrow (never `library/view.js`, `record-art/looks.js` or `storage/meta.js`, per that file's own header), this needed no "stop the chain at the door" mock list of its own beyond `sync/images.js`. It drives the real `pullBackupOnForeground()` against a synthetic "another device's" remote row (its own local track id, sharing only an identity key with this fresh device's one real local track) and confirms playlists/album order/stats/achievements/collection/sealed state/a track overlay all land correctly in `S`, that the same values are independently readable straight back out of the real IndexedDB through `storage/repo.js` (not just held in memory), and that they survive a real `DB.db.close()` + reopen on the same underlying fake IndexedDB — simulating an actual app restart, not just a fresh assertion against the same open connection. A second test confirms a playlist entry whose identity key resolves to no local track at all is held back as pending rather than pushed into `S.playlists` as a bare, meaningless string.
- `src/styles/` holds the CSS, split out of what used to be one inline `<style>` block, one file per section (`00-base.css`, `01-header.css`, …), referenced from `miziki.html` as separate `<link rel="stylesheet">` tags in that same original order. Cascade order matters — that numeric prefix is load order, not importance, and the files must stay in it. `fonts.css` (step 01, no numeric prefix — it's not part of that original cascade, just linked first) holds the self-hosted `@font-face` rules that replaced the Google Fonts CDN link; see `src/fonts/`'s own entry and "Security" below. `21-legal.css` (step 08, P3, part A) is the latest numbered file, appended after `20-friend-store.css` — a small, scoped sheet for the Privacy Policy/Terms of use screens: `#profileBody h2` heading styling (scoped there rather than a generic `h2` rule, since `00-base.css` has no generic element resets anywhere in this app), `.legal-list`/`.legal-list li` for their bullet lists, and `.legal-links`/`.link-btn` for the inline "Privacy Policy · Terms of use" footer row `legal.js`'s `legalLinksRow()` builds. Since step D1 (desktop layout prerequisite — see `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), `00-base.css`'s `:root` also carries nine new tokens (`--sky3`, `--cream`, `--panel`, `--chrome`, `--raised`, `--field`, `--sel`, `--cool`, `--disc-a`/`--disc-b`/`--disc-c`), each given the exact dark-mode value every literal it replaces already had — this PR added no light values and changed no pixel; see that step's own changelog entry below for the full token list and the two literals it consolidated onto an existing token (`--paper`→`--cream` on five sticker/note backgrounds whose color was always the cream swatch, never the page's own paper tone; `.mini-player`/`.tabbar`'s two slightly different chrome alphas onto the one `--chrome` value). Since NV1 (`docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`), the sky gradient moved off `body`'s own `background`/`background-attachment:fixed` onto a dedicated `.sky-bg{position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:-1}` element (first child of `<body>`, `miziki.html`) — `body` is the app's one scroller, and `background-attachment:fixed` on it forced a full-screen gradient repaint on every scroll frame; a real fixed-position layer behind everything costs nothing to scroll over instead. `body`'s own `transition:background 1.2s linear` is gone too (a CSS transition can't interpolate between two `linear-gradient()` values, so it never animated anything). `drawSun()` (`player/clock.js`) still writes `--sky1`/`--sky2` onto `:root`, which `.sky-bg` reads the same way `body`'s background used to. The same step added a global `-webkit-tap-highlight-color:transparent` (on `*`) and a shared pressed-state block (`.tabbar-btn`/`.seg button`/`.cta`/`.tracklist li` get `:active{background:var(--sel)}` and `touch-action:manipulation`; `.crate-card` gets a `:active` brightness dip on its face image instead, since it's a flick-gesture surface under `.crate-stage`'s own `touch-action:none` and a flat tint would sit behind the cover art anyway). Since step D2, `00-base.css`'s `:root` also sets `color-scheme:dark` (so native controls follow the theme too), and `22-theme.css` is the latest numbered file, appended after `21-legal.css` — the light token set and the Auto/pinned-Dark selector logic; see that step's own changelog entry for the exact three selector blocks and why only the thirteen tokens that actually differ between themes are redeclared there (everything else — `--accent`, `--on-accent`, `--cool`, the disc gradient, the fonts — is left to inherit from bare `:root` on purpose, per the doc's own "only chrome changes" rule). `23-desktop.css` (step D3; extended step D4, D5, D6, RF8) is the latest numbered file, appended after `22-theme.css` — the desktop shell itself: the `@media (min-width:1024px)`/`(min-width:1280px)` breakpoint rules that turn the shared tab-bar markup into the sidebar, drop `.wrap`'s 520px cap, and show the sidebar's new playlists-list/Sundown-card content; everything in it sits behind one of those two media queries except `.sidebar{display:contents}`/`.sidebar-extra{display:none}`, which apply unconditionally so the sidebar wrapper is invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint rather than a visible box — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design (including the `order:-1` fix a real flex-ordering bug needed, and why the header's search field/`+` menu needed no new markup at all). Since step D4, the D3-era `.mini-player{display:none !important}` placeholder is gone, replaced by the real 88px player-bar layout (`position:fixed;bottom:0`, full viewport width, `order` on each child for the "now playing / transport+seek / mode+queue" zones) — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design, including why volume is the one mockup zone it leaves out. Since step D5, also carries the crate's own desktop layout: `.crate-layout{display:contents}`/`.crate-detail-panel{display:none}` unconditionally (the same invisible-scaffolding-below-the-breakpoint shape `.sidebar` already established), then, inside the `1024px` block, `--crate-card-size:340px` (overriding `10-crate.css`'s own `278px` default) and the `.crate-layout`/`.crate-detail-panel`/`.crate-detail-art`/`.crate-detail-play` rules for the inline record-detail panel beside the bigger crate — see that step's own changelog entry for why the card's own size lives in a CSS variable rather than a second hardcoded number, and why the detail panel never overlaps the sidebar or the crate stage. Since step D6, also carries Now Playing's own desktop layout (`.player-layout{display:contents}`/`.stage-info{display:contents}`/`.player-side-panel{display:none}` unconditionally, the same scaffolding shape; then, inside the `1024px` block, `--sidebar-width` — a new token read-only outside this file, set to the sidebar's own current width at each of its two breakpoints so `.player-overlay`'s own `left` offset never repeats those numbers a third time — `--platter-size:min(32vw,440px)` overriding `02-stage-platter.css`'s own `278px`-baseline default, the `.player-overlay`/`.player-dismiss`/`.player-overlay .stage`/`.platter-stage`/`.stage-info` rules that confine the overlay to the main area and lay the platter/info/side-panel out in three columns, and the `.player-side-panel`/`.player-side-tabs`/`.player-side-tab`/`.player-side-body` rules for the tabbed panel itself) — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design, including the specificity fight one rule had to be written to actually win, and why `#queueBtn` is hidden there. Since RF8 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "one home per control"), the `.sidebar-appearance` rules are gone outright (the control itself was removed from `miziki.html`) and the mini-player bar's own `order` rules dropped `#miniFavBtn{order:2}` (the button itself is gone); a new unconditional rule inside the `1024px` block, `body:has(#playerOverlay.open) .mini-player #barPrev, ...`, hides the desktop bar's own prev/play/next/seek/mode/queue controls whenever the full player is open — the doc's "on the player route, the desktop player bar shows only the now-playing cell, not a second transport" — chosen over a JS-added body class specifically to avoid touching the several separate call sites that already toggle `#playerOverlay`'s own open state (`openPlayerViaSheet`, `openPlayer`/`runMorphOpen`, `closePlayer`, `interruptMorph`). Since RF26 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`), `.sidebar-sundown` (the desktop sidebar's own Sundown-card button, one of the sheet's four entry points) grew from a bare `padding:0 10px` into a full button-reset block (`background:none;border:none;width:100%;text-align:left;cursor:pointer`, plus hover/focus-visible background) now that the element it styles is a real `<button>` rather than a plain `<div>` (see `shell/sidebar.js`'s own updated entry) — its `1024px`-block content/layout rules are otherwise unchanged. `26-sundown-sheet.css` (RF26) is the latest numbered file, appended after `25-pull-refresh.css` — the Sundown sheet itself: `[data-phase="..."]` selectors on `#sunKnob`/`#miniSunBtn`/`#trackSpecPhase` duplicating three of `profile/rhythm-card.js`'s own `PHASE_COLOR` table values as CSS custom properties (see `player/clock.js`'s updated `drawSun()` entry for why this is a deliberate duplication rather than a shared import), `.sun-knob`/`.sun-knob-ring`/`.sun-knob-glyph`/`.mini-sun-btn` layout, `#trackSpecPhase`'s inline-chip styling, and `.sundown-sheet*` — the scrim, the grabber, the half/full-height transform logic (a plain class toggle, not a physics-based gesture — see `shell/sundown-sheet.js`'s own entry), and a `@media (min-width:1024px)` block that re-anchors the same sheet markup as a right-side panel instead of a bottom sheet, the same "CSS reflow, no JS viewport branch" pattern `23-desktop.css` already established for the sidebar/player-bar/crate-detail-panel. **Fixed post-RF26: `.sundown-sheet-scrim`/`.sundown-sheet`'s own z-index (originally 60/61, below the full player's 100) meant that opening the sheet from its one on-platter entry point, `#sunKnob` — the only one of the sheet's four ways in that sits *inside* the open full player rather than beside it — left the sheet painting invisibly behind the still-fully-visible player: `nav-stack.js` correctly pushed the layer and marked the player `inert`, so the tap wasn't silently dropped, but nothing on screen changed and the (now-inert) player stopped responding to any further touch, exactly matching a real report ("clicking the knob just brings up this page... can't interact with the player any longer. Can scroll back to return to the interactive player interface" — the "scroll back" being a back-swipe, which pops the nav-stack layer and clears `inert` again). Confirmed by screenshot before/after, not just computed-style inspection — a before/after Playwright screenshot is the one test that can't be fooled by a false-positive `elementFromPoint()` read. Fixed by raising both to 160/161, above every other overlay in the app including `.morph-disc`/`.gf-handoff-ghost` (150) — the sheet can be opened over any of them (the mini-player's own sun button, the desktop sidebar, Settings, and the knob) and must always paint on top regardless of which one it was opened from.** Since RF10 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "share cards, one good default"), `20-friend-store.css`'s own `.share-actions-row` changed from a fixed 2-column `grid` to `display:flex` with `flex:1` on each `.cta` child — both share sheets' actions row now holds 2 or 3 buttons (Save image, an optionally-hidden Copy, and a new "More options ›" disclosure toggle) depending on platform capability and the row needs to divide evenly between whichever are actually visible, not leave an empty grid track the way a fixed `1fr 1fr` would once a third child showed up. **RF12 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "light-theme contrast") converted every *text* use of `--accent`/`--amber`/`--miziki-orange` to `--accent-text`** across 14 files (`01-header.css`, `02-stage-platter.css`, `03-sleeve-pull.css`, `05-transport.css`, `08-shell.css`, `09-launch-intro.css`, `10-crate.css`, `11-gatefold.css`, `12-sealed-records.css`, `16-edit-sheet.css`, `17-profile.css`, `18-storefront.css`, `19-signin.css`, `20-friend-store.css`, `23-desktop.css`) — a plain `color:var(--accent)` → `color:var(--accent-text)` swap on each of 42 found declarations, leaving every fill/ring/underline/slider-track and every icon 24px and up (nothing in this app's icon set is that large — see below) on `--accent` untouched, per that point's own exact wording. `03-sleeve-pull.css`'s `.heart-btn[aria-pressed="true"]` is the one swap that isn't `var(--accent)` → `var(--accent-text)`: it was a hardcoded `#FF9E2C` literal (the dark-theme value of `--amber`, never adapting for light theme at all) that the `var(--accent)` grep sweep couldn't catch — found instead by the new contrast test (below) failing it at 1.83:1 in light theme, then fixed the same way as every other RF12 swap. `08-shell.css`'s `.tabbar-btn`/`.tabbar-btn svg`/`.tabbar-btn[aria-selected="true"]` rules were restructured for point 2 ("the active tab never relies on color alone"): `color` moved off the button itself onto its `svg`/`span` children individually (so the two can diverge), the selected tab's icon stays `--accent` (point 1's text-only rule doesn't apply to an `<svg>`, whose `color` drives `stroke:currentColor`, not text), its label switches to `--paper` at weight 500, and a new `::before` pseudo-element draws a 2px accent bar along the cell's own top edge. `23-desktop.css`'s icon-only sidebar width (`@media (min-width:1024px)`) retargeted its own hover/focus overrides from the button's `color` onto the `svg` specifically (now that nothing reads the button's own `color`), and hides the new top-edge bar outright (`::before{display:none}`) — a bar reads oddly on a vertical icon rail, and the icon-color change plus (at `1280px+`) the row's own `--sel` background fill already mark the active tab there without it. `25-day-arc-v2.css`'s four `.day-dial-hourlabel*` rules (point 3) moved from `top/right/bottom/left:2%` (squarely inside the dial's own conic-gradient ring, which reads `--muted` text against an unpredictable pink-or-blue segment) to negative offsets clear of the 300px disc entirely, at `11px`/`--paper` — sidestepping the contrast problem structurally rather than only recoloring text that would still sit on an unreviewable background. **`tests/unit/contrast.test.js` is RF12 point 4's "small script"** — see that file's own entry in the tests paragraph below for what it checks and, as importantly, what it deliberately doesn't (every arbitrary `color:` declaration in these files, many of which sit on their own opaque card/chip background rather than the sky gradient these two tokens are judged against). **RF14 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "a moving palette with fixed rules")**: `00-base.css`'s bare `:root` gained `--muted-2:#7A868C` (tertiary text, new, alongside the existing `--muted`) and changed two existing dark values — `--panel` `.42` → `.55`, `--raised` `.04` → `.07` (point 4, "sky, panel and raised read as three levels") — each with its own inline comment pointing at the RF14 point it satisfies. `22-theme.css` got the matching `--muted-2` in all three selector blocks (`#6E7A80` for light, one flat pair regardless of phase; `#7A868C` for the explicit `:root[data-theme="dark"]` re-assertion, matching bare `:root`) and the same `--panel`/`--raised` value-step change in its own dark block. `--muted`/`--muted-2`'s static values here are only the first-paint fallback — `player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` overrides both at runtime per sky anchor via inline style (see that file's own entry) the same way it already does for `--sky1`/`--sky2`. `25-day-arc-v2.css` itself needed no change for RF14 point 3 (the dial ring's own color source) — `.day-dial-ring`'s CSS already paints whatever `conic-gradient()` stops `sundown/arc.js`'s `skyRingStops()` hands it via JS, so swapping that function's own palette needed no change to the rule that consumes it; see `sundown/arc.js`'s own entry for what changed there. **RF15 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "the record store in the surfaces")** touched four files: `00-base.css` gained a `.sky-bg::after{...}` rule (point 2, the grain texture) — a single inline `data:image/svg+xml` `<feTurbulence>` filter tiled at low opacity (`.05`) over `.sky-bg` only, never over cover art or panels, so no binary asset ships and no CSP change is needed (`img-src`'s existing `data:` already covers it) — plus a `@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce)` block that hides it outright, the first use of that media feature in this codebase (distinct from `prefers-reduced-motion`/`REDUCED` in `util/motion.js`). `10-crate.css` gained `.crate-card::after{...}` (point 3, sleeve edges) — a 1px lighter top inset-shadow plus a soft darker bottom band, via one pseudo-element layered over the card face rather than touching its existing nested art/overlay markup. `18-storefront.css`'s `.store-pick-empty` rule was rewritten from a dashed-border placeholder box into the cream divider-tab look (point 4) — see `profile/storefront.js`'s own updated entry for the markup (`.store-pick-empty-tab`/`.store-pick-empty-card`) this new CSS styles, matching `docs/design/refine/Profile.dc.html`'s mockup values exactly (`var(--cream)` background, `#1A1410` ink, `5px 5px 0 0` tab radius, `0 7px 7px 7px` card radius, an `inset` hairline plus `var(--sh-2)`). `02-stage-platter.css` (point 1, the platter label) replaced `.label.has-art .rpm,.label.has-art .mode-tag{display:none}` with the equivalent rule for the new label markup (`.label.has-art .label-arc,.label.has-art .label-body{display:none}` — the label's title/arc only ever shows in the no-art state, since cover art already carries identity there), added the `.label-arc`/`.label-arc text`/`.label-body`/`.label-title`/`.label-spindle` rules themselves, and added `.mode-pure .label.has-art{box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 1px rgba(0,0,0,.35)}` — the Pure-mode flatten (no highlight, hairline ring only), keyed off the new `mode-pure` class `src/audio/mode.js`'s `setMode()` now toggles on `document.documentElement`, superseding this file's own prior "this ring is a fixed Miziki orange, not a mode indicator" comment for exactly the no-art state RF15 adds a label to. The old `.label .rpm{...}`/`.label .mode-tag{...}` rules (and their markup, `miziki.html`'s own entry) are gone outright.

**RF13 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "type, spacing and buttons") added the type/spacing/elevation/radii token scale to `00-base.css`'s `:root`** — see CLAUDE.md's own "CSS token scale (RF13)" section above for the full table and the rule it exists to enforce — then mechanically applied it across every one of the 26 style files: all 238 `font-size:Npx` declarations became `font-size:var(--fs-*)` (the one genuinely ambiguous boundary, `10.5px`, classified by hand per occurrence between `--fs-micro`/`--fs-label` rather than guessed from the number alone), `--sp-*` replaced every exact-match `margin`/`padding`/`gap` px value (4/8/12/16/24/32/48/64), and the size-based letter-spacing default (`.10em`/`.08em`/Antonio `.02em`) was *added* only where a rule had none at all, never overriding an existing hand-tuned value. `tests/unit/contrast.test.js` was updated alongside this sweep to resolve `font-size:var(--fs-*)` back to a literal px value (against the same dark-token map every `--fs-*` token is defined in) before applying its under-18px check — without that, every declaration's `fontSize` would read `null` and the test would treat all of them as "small," which happened to still pass but silently lost its ability to exempt large accent text. Elevation tokens (`--sh-2`/`--sh-3`) replaced the box-shadow literals on the sites that clearly matched RF13's own named categories (`09-launch-intro.css`'s card stack, `10-crate.css`'s crate cards/current card, `11-gatefold.css`'s cover, `17-profile.css`'s badge/settings-menu, `18-storefront.css`'s avatar, `01-header.css`'s two popovers, `03-sleeve-pull.css`'s sleeve, `14-morph.css`'s morph disc) — `--sh-1` (mini-player/buttons) was left for later, since neither has an existing shadow to swap and adding one where none exists isn't a mechanical replacement. `16-edit-sheet.css`'s `.tracklist li` lost its per-row `border-bottom` (the app-wide song/artist row hairline, shared by the library, gatefold and edit sheet alike), and `08-shell.css`'s `.mini-player` lost its own `border-top` — it and `.tabbar` sit flush against each other as one piece of bottom chrome, so having both independently draw a top edge read as a doubled hairline at their shared seam; `.tabbar`'s own top border is the one that remains.

  Since RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Profile is your crate"): `17-profile.css`'s single `.profile-settings-btn` rule became `.profile-settings-wrap`/`.profile-settings-btn` (now a `⚙` icon button, not "Settings ›" text) plus `.profile-settings-btn[aria-expanded="true"]` and a new `.profile-settings-menu`/`.profile-settings-menu[hidden]`/`.profile-settings-menu-item` (+hover/focus) block for the Refresh/Settings popover (point 4's fix for the Refresh/Settings overlap — see `shell/pull-refresh.js`/`profile/storefront.js`'s own updated entries). `18-storefront.css`'s `.store-awning > div` shrank from `height:22px` to `height:12px;border-radius:0 0 12px 12px` (point 3, "the awning shrinks to a 12px trim") and `.store-handle-input::placeholder` changed from `color:var(--muted)` to `color:var(--paper);opacity:.5` (point 3, "the username in `--paper` even as a placeholder" — reads as an empty field waiting for a name rather than a disabled one); a new `.store-listening-row`/`.store-listening-row .g-chev` block styles the one row every storefront state now links into the routed Listening screen (point 1). `25-pull-refresh.css`'s `.pull-refresh-btn` (absolute-positioned, `top:6px;right:10px` — the rule that used to sit directly over the Profile Settings gear, point 4's own overlap bug) is gone, replaced by `.route-refresh-btn` (in-flow, `margin:10px 16px 0 auto`, opacity:0 until focus/hover) for the Home route's own keyboard Refresh button; Profile no longer has a CSS-positioned refresh button of its own at all, since its Refresh trigger moved into the Settings menu (see `wiring/pull-refresh.js`'s own updated entry). Since RF20 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "fresh from pins and notes; the rating is decoration"), `18-storefront.css` also gained `.store-rule-caption{margin:var(--sp-1) 0 0;font-size:var(--fs-caption);color:var(--muted)}` — the one-line explainer `social/screens/friend-store.js`'s new `storeRuleCaption()` renders under each algorithmically-chosen section's heading (the new "You both have" section, and "Fresh for you"'s own new caption).

  `10-crate.css` (step D5) gained its own new token, `--crate-card-size` (default `278px`, the literal every `.crate-card` rule already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), read live by both this file's own `.crate-card` sizing rules (`width`/`height`/`margin-left`, the last now `calc(var(--crate-card-size) / -2)` in place of the old fixed `-139px`) and by `crate/view.js`'s own `currentCardSize()` — see that module's updated entry below for how the two meet in the middle.

  `02-stage-platter.css` (step D6) gained the same treatment for the full player's own platter: a new `:root{--platter-size:min(72vw,278px)}` (the exact literal `.platter-stage{width:...}` already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), with `.platter-stage`'s own `width` now reading it. Nothing else needed to change for the var alone to work — every place in `src/shell/` that measures the platter already does so live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (the morph geometry, the hand-off/put-away/start-sequence ceremonies), never by reading this value directly.
- `src/fonts/` (step 01) holds the self-hosted font files `src/styles/fonts.css`'s `@font-face` rules point at: `antonio-variable.woff2`, `archivo-variable.woff2` (each a single Google-served variable-weight file that already covers every weight this app asks for — 400/600/700 and 400/500/600 respectively — confirmed by diffing Google's own CSS2 API response, which points every static weight at the identical URL per family), and `ibm-plex-mono-400.woff2`/`ibm-plex-mono-500.woff2` (not variable, so two separate files). Only the `latin` Unicode-range subset of each is shipped, not the `latin-ext`/`cyrillic`/`cyrillic-ext`/`vietnamese` subsets Google's own CSS2 API also serves as separate files — a deliberate scope call for this app's own UI text; a song tag in one of those scripts still displays, just in the body font's fallback rather than the display face, the same graceful fallback a missing glyph in a real font always gets. `OFL-antonio.txt`/`OFL-archivo.txt`/`OFL-ibmplexmono.txt` are each family's required license file, fetched from the `google/fonts` GitHub repo unchanged.
- `src/miziki-social.js` is the social client (Supabase). It exposes the `MizikiSocial` global. **This is the source of truth and the only copy** — `miziki.html` loads it directly (as a plain classic `<script src="src/miziki-social.js">`, not `type="module"`, so it keeps executing synchronously in place the same as the inline copy it used to be; see the comment above that tag for why a relative path, not an absolute one — it's also what makes this reference work unchanged under both bases below). Since step 06 (B3), its public API also exposes `getClient()` — a small accessor returning the real supabase-js client instance (or `null` if the social layer is inert), added specifically so `src/sync/backup.js` could reach the one client the social layer already creates instead of constructing a second one of its own, per the step 06 doc's own instruction. Also added: `signOutEverywhere` (`auth.signOut({scope:'global'})`, as opposed to the existing `signOut`'s now-explicit `{scope:'local'}` — previously implicit/default, made explicit so the Account screen's two buttons are provably different, not just differently labeled), `deleteAccount` (calls the `delete_my_account()` RPC from migration 005, then resets local social state and signs out), and `listBlocked` (the Privacy screen's blocked-accounts list). Since step 07 (B4), `deleteAccount` also removes the caller's own files from both Storage buckets *before* calling the RPC, via a new module-private `removeStorageFolder(bucket, uid)` (lists `bucket/<uid>/`, removes every file found, best-effort — wrapped in its own try/catch so a Storage hiccup can never block account deletion itself) called once for `'covers'` and once for `'avatars'`. This has to happen client-side, and before the RPC, because Storage objects have no foreign key to `auth.users` — `delete_my_account()`'s existing cascades (migration 005) can't reach either bucket, and this device's own owner-only RLS policies (migration 006) are what let it list/remove its own files right up until the RPC actually deletes the account out from under it. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `freshState().settings` gained `shareListeningRhythm: false` (default off, the same posture `shareNowSpinning`/`shareMilestones` already have), `updateProfile(patch)`'s own field validation gained a `listeningRhythm` case (either `null` — a clear — or a plain `{verdict, shares}` object, never raw plays), and two new exports: `setListeningRhythm(payload)` (a thin wrapper over `updateProfile({listeningRhythm: payload})`, so the shared snapshot gets the exact same single-column validated write every other profile field already uses, not a second write path of its own) and `getListeningRhythm(userId)` (reads someone else's shared value through the new `get_listening_rhythm()` RPC, migration `007_rhythm.sql` — never a direct `.from('profiles').select('listening_rhythm')`, since `profiles_select` is deliberately blanket and the RPC is where this one column's own stricter visibility check actually lives; returns `rhythm: null` for an owner who hasn't shared one, isn't visible to this viewer, or isn't signed in at all, the same "absent, not denied" shape `can_view()`'s own callers already use, never a surfaced error for the ordinary "nothing to show" case). `loadProfile(handle)`'s existing `Promise.all([...])` batch gained one more call to that same RPC, setting the returned profile's own `listeningRhythm` field for `social/screens/friend-store.js`'s own storefront render to read. Since step DA6 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), `nowSpinning(albumId, trackTitle, phase)` and its own `publishSpin(albumId, trackTitle, phase)` gained a third parameter — the spinner's own local day-arc phase, sent through to `set_now_spinning`'s new `p_phase` (migration `008_spin_phase.sql`) and stored alongside `albumId`/`trackTitle` in `X.spin.last` so the existing heartbeat republish (`setInterval`, every `X.timing.spinHeartbeat`) resends the same phase it was given at play time rather than recomputing it — this file stays import-free by design (see its own entry above), so it has no way to ask `sundown/solar.js` for a fresh one every four minutes; the caller (`player/transport.js`'s `play()`) is the one place that actually computes it, once per real play. `loadProfile()`'s own `now_spinning` select widened to also read `phase`, and `subscribeNowSpinning(cb)`'s realtime payload now passes `phase: row.phase` through to its callback — both feed the same new `src/sundown/spin-wording.js` wording table that `social/screens/home.js`/`social/screens/friend-store.js` render from. Since RF20 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "fresh from pins and notes; the rating is decoration"), `loadProfile(handle)`'s `Promise.all([...])` batch gained one more call, `isMe ? Promise.resolve([]) : rpc('records_in_common', { p_other: profile.id })` — the existing `records_in_common` RPC (`002_rpc.sql`), unused by any client code before this step — setting the returned profile's own new `inCommon` field (`out.inCommon = inCommonRows || [];`) for `social/screens/friend-store.js`'s new "You both have" section to read.
- `supabase/migrations/` holds the schema, RPCs, digging list, follow counts, (step 05, B1) the account-backup table, reserved handles, account deletion and a security hardening pass, (step 07, B4) the two image Storage buckets, (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) the shared listening-rhythm column and its own gated read RPC, and (step DA6, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) now_spinning's own day-arc phase column. Apply them in numeric order. Never edit a migration that has already been applied. Add a new numbered file instead. `005_backup.sql` added `public.library_backup` (one row per device, owner-only RLS — see "Backup: what gets synced" and the step 05 changelog entry below), a `profiles_handle_reserved` trigger, `public.delete_my_account()`, and moved four RLS-helper functions (`is_blocked_between`, `item_visibility`, `effective_visibility`, `can_view`) into a new `private` schema that Supabase's Data API never exposes — every function body that called one of those four by name was re-declared to call the moved name; the RLS policies that reference them didn't need touching, since a policy binds to a function's resolved OID at creation time, not its schema-qualified name. `006_images.sql` added two Storage buckets, `covers` (private, 1 MB limit, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only read/write/delete) and `avatars` (public, 300 KB limit, path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, publicly readable but owner-only write) on Supabase's own `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects` tables, plus the four owner-folder RLS policies per bucket (`storage.foldername(name)` splits the path on `/` and drops the last segment, so `(storage.foldername(name))[1]` is exactly the `{user_id}` prefix both paths are keyed by) — no table of its own, since `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects` already exist in every Supabase project. Its own closing comment notes that account-deletion's Storage cleanup is handled client-side (`src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`), not here, since Storage objects have no FK to `auth.users` for `delete_my_account()`'s cascades to reach. `007_rhythm.sql` (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) added one nullable column, `public.profiles.listening_rhythm jsonb` (the opt-in shared `{verdict, shares}` snapshot — see "What's stored server-side" below), a column-level grant (`grant update (listening_rhythm) on public.profiles to authenticated`, additive on top of 001's existing table-level revoke+grant, the standard way to widen one column's write access without reopening every other column), and a new `public.get_listening_rhythm(p_user uuid)` RPC — **`security definer`**, not `security invoker` like `003_digging.sql`'s own `digging_page()`/`digging_count()` precedent this otherwise follows, a deliberate divergence found by testing, not assumed upfront: an `invoker`-mode call from a signed-out (`anon`) caller failed with "permission denied for schema private," since `private` schema usage is granted only to `authenticated` and `anon` has no way to even reach the `can_view()` check the function body needs to run. `security definer` lets the function itself run with the owning role's privileges regardless of caller, while still gating the *result* through an explicit `can_view()` call inside the function body — so the one column this RPC exists to protect stays gated by the same visibility rules as the rest of the crate (private/friends/public, blocks honored) without ever needing to loosen `profiles_select`'s own intentionally blanket, handle-findability policy. Returns `null`, not an error, for every case nothing should be shown (not shared, not visible to this caller, signed out, or the owner never met the 20-play gate client-side). `008_spin_phase.sql` (step DA6, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) added `public.now_spinning.phase text` (nullable — an older client that calls `set_now_spinning` without a phase at all leaves it `null` forever, never an error) with a check constraint listing the seven day-arc phase names `dayPosition()` can return, and gave `set_now_spinning` a sixth parameter, `p_phase text default null`. Adding a parameter changes the function's own signature, so a bare `create or replace` would have left the old 5-argument version in place as a second overload rather than actually replacing it (Postgres resolves `create or replace function` by exact argument-type match) — the migration drops the old signature explicitly first (`drop function if exists public.set_now_spinning(text, text, text, text, integer)`), so there is exactly one `set_now_spinning` after this migration, not two. The function body is otherwise byte-for-byte the same as 005's own version (still `private.effective_visibility`/`private.item_visibility`, still `security invoker`, still the same private-record early return) — only the new parameter and the one new column in the insert/upsert are added. `clear_now_spinning()` (002) needed no change, since it only ever deletes the caller's own row by `user_id`, regardless of which columns that row had. No realtime publication change — `now_spinning` was already in it (001), and this is just one more column on an existing live-presence row. `012_migrations_log.sql` (step RF5, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 1 — "guardrails for a one-person team") adds `private.schema_migrations(version int primary key, applied_at timestamptz default now())` and backfills versions 1-11 (every migration that already existed by the time this one was written — there's nothing to assert for them, since this migration is where the ledger starts being real, not a record of history rewritten) plus its own version 12. **Every migration from 013 on must open with an assertion that the previous version is present in this table and close by inserting its own** (that file's own header comment has the exact `do $$ ... raise exception ...`/`insert into private.schema_migrations (version) values (n)` shape to copy) — a plain `insert` with no `on conflict`, so a migration mistakenly re-applied out of order fails loudly (a primary-key violation) instead of silently double-applying. `private.schema_migrations` itself has no grant to `anon`/`authenticated` — bookkeeping for migrations, not something a client ever reads or writes. `013_backup_seq.sql` (step RF18, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, "pushes that can't go backwards") adds `public.library_backup.doc_seq bigint not null default 0` and a `security invoker` RPC, `public.put_library_backup(p_device uuid, p_seq bigint, p_doc jsonb, p_device_label text, p_schema_version smallint)`, that replaces `src/sync/backup.js`'s own old plain `.upsert()` — its `on conflict (user_id, device_id) do update ... where public.library_backup.doc_seq < excluded.doc_seq` means a push whose `p_seq` isn't strictly higher than what's already stored is a quiet no-op (the conflict is still "handled," not an error), closing the real race a slow retry (`BACKOFF`, same file) or two overlapping push attempts could otherwise win against a push that already landed more recently. `security invoker`, not `definer` — the existing RLS policies on `library_backup` already require `user_id = auth.uid()` on every operation, and this RPC always inserts/updates with exactly that value, so (unlike `007_rhythm.sql`'s `get_listening_rhythm()`) it needs no elevated privilege at all. Opens with the now-standard "migration 012 must be applied first" assertion and closes by inserting its own row into `private.schema_migrations`, per `012_migrations_log.sql`'s own convention. `014_backup_history.sql` (step RF19, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, "verify, and keep a way back", points 1 and 3) adds `public.library_backup.checksum text` (a server-opaque value — Postgres never parses or validates it; `src/sync/backup.js`'s own push/pull is what computes and checks it, per that file's updated entry above) and bumps `put_library_backup()`'s own signature to a sixth parameter, `p_checksum text default null` — the same "drop the old signature, then create the new one" pattern `008_spin_phase.sql` already established for adding a parameter to an existing RPC, since a bare `create or replace` would otherwise leave the old 5-arg version as a second overload rather than replacing it. The bulk of the file is point 3's own new table, `public.library_backup_history` (one row per past version of a device's `library_backup` row — `doc`, `doc_seq`, `checksum`, `schema_version`, `device_label`, `updated_at`, plus its own `recorded_at`), populated by a `before update` trigger (`private.capture_library_backup_history()`, `security definer` so it can write regardless of which RLS role is doing the update) that copies `OLD` into history and then deletes all but the 10 most recent rows for that `(user_id, device_id)` pair — nothing in the app ever reads this table; it exists purely for a moderator (or Ken himself) to inspect or restore from in the Supabase dashboard if a bad push ever leaves a device's row worse off than it was (owner-only `select` RLS, no insert/update/delete grant to any client role — only the trigger function can write it). See `docs/MODERATION.md`'s own new "Restoring a device's backup from history" section for the dashboard queries. `015_fresh_from_pins_notes.sql` (step RF20, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, "fresh from pins and notes; the rating is decoration") replaces `001_schema.sql`'s own `public.fresh_for_you(p_other uuid, p_min_rating smallint default null, p_limit int default 20, p_offset int default 0)` with a `create or replace function` of the same name, same signature (so `p_min_rating` is still accepted for backward compatibility — a caller that still passes it gets no error — but the function body never reads it any more), same `security invoker`/`language sql stable`, same return shape. Only the `where`/`order by` changed: the filter is now `(c.pin_rank is not null or c.sleeve_note is not null)` in place of the old `c.rating >= p_min_rating` (a record with neither a pin nor a sleeve note was never "fresh" regardless of its rating, and a 1-star record with either is), and the ordering is `(c.pin_rank is null), c.pin_rank asc, c.updated_at desc` in place of `c.rating desc, c.added_at desc` — every pinned record outranks every unpinned one, ties among pinned records break by pin rank (lower number first, the same "pin rank is the order" convention `crate_items`' own `unique (user_id, pin_rank)` constraint already implies), and unpinned-but-noted records sort by most-recently-touched. `records_in_common` (`002_rpc.sql`) is untouched by this migration — it already existed, unused by any client code before this step. Opens with the now-standard "migration 014 must be applied first" assertion and closes by inserting its own row into `private.schema_migrations`, per `012_migrations_log.sql`'s own convention.
- `tests/client.test.js` holds Node tests for the social client against a real local Postgres (see `tests/supabase_stub.sql`, extended in step 07, B4 with a minimal `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects`/`storage.foldername()` stand-in so `006_images.sql`'s Storage RLS policies can be exercised with real `auth.uid()` semantics the same way every other table's policies already are). The player itself has no automated tests yet.
- `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md` (step 08, P3, part A) are the canonical, reviewable source for the in-app Privacy Policy and Terms of use screens (`src/profile/legal.js`'s own entry above has the full in-app content breakdown) — any PR that changes either document's wording must change both the markdown and `legal.js` together, called out at the top of all three files. Both carry `sckhitokiri@gmail.com` as a temporary contact address, noted inline in all four files as a placeholder to be replaced with an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased (see "Deploy previews"/the Part D roadmap item), and both are written from the facts already established elsewhere in this file (the "Backup: what gets synced" and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" tables, the reserved-handles/account-deletion rules, SEALED/CREDITS spec behavior where it's user-facing) rather than generic boilerplate. Per the task doc's own framing, this is draft text for Ken (not a lawyer) to have reviewed before the App Store, not a final legal document — the PR that adds them is opened "do not merge" for exactly that reason. The minimum age started at 13 per the task doc's own instruction, then was raised to 16 at Ken's own request, to avoid GDPR's under-16 parental-consent rules for EU users. Since step DA5 (`docs/DAY_ARC.md`), both docs' "What's collected"/"Who can see what" sections gained the same listening-rhythm-sharing bullet `legal.js`'s own in-app mirror carries — reviewed together, per this file's own two-source-review rule.
- `docs/MODERATION.md` (step 08, P3, part B; extended step RF19, `docs/12_REFINE.md`) — how to review reports against accounts in the Supabase dashboard (weekly is fine at this project's scale, sooner if something looks urgent), and the actions available once one warrants it: clear an avatar/bio, remove a sleeve note, delete an account, and (since RF19) restore a device's backup from history. Each has the exact SQL (or the equivalent Table Editor click-path) against `public.reports`/`profiles`/`crate_items`/`auth.users`/`library_backup`/`library_backup_history`, plus the matching Storage cleanup for account deletion, since none of these run through the app's own client-side RLS-gated paths — a moderator acts as the dashboard's own elevated role, which is also why `delete_my_account()` can't just be called on someone else's behalf and a direct `auth.users` delete is used instead. The new "Restoring a device's backup from history" section points at `public.library_backup_history` (migration `014_backup_history.sql`) — nothing in the app itself ever reads this table, so this doc's own queries (list a device's history, newest first; copy the most recent row's `doc`/`doc_seq`/`checksum` back onto the live `library_backup` row) are the only way it's ever used, a deliberate last-resort override of `put_library_backup()`'s own `doc_seq` guard. Docs only — no app code, except the migration itself, nothing to build or preview.
- `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md` (step D, desktop layout + Auto/Light/Dark appearance — Oct 13, 2026 handoff doc, authored by Ken) is the verbatim source for the seven-PR desktop-layout plan (D1-D7): a 1024px+ breakpoint responsive mode (left sidebar from the existing tab bar, main area, persistent player bar, a side panel on Now Playing), an Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting driven by CSS custom-property tokens (Auto follows the OS via `prefers-color-scheme`), and the three mockup screens (Library, Now Playing, Crate Profile). Explicitly out of scope for all seven PRs: the native iOS app, Supabase/schema changes, new social features, and tablet widths 768-1023px (which keep the phone layout). Each PR stops and waits for Ken's go-ahead before the next one starts — this isn't a self-driving sequence like steps 01-08 above. All seven are complete (see "After step D7" at the end of this changelog); DA1-DA6 below is the next plan in the same one-PR-at-a-time, Ken-gated style.
- `docs/DAY_ARC.md` (step DA, the day arc — Oct 13, 2026 handoff doc, authored by Ken, same format and gating as `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) is the verbatim source for the six-PR day-arc plan (DA1-DA6): Sundown stops being a sunset-only effect and becomes one point on a day-long curve computed from real solar events at the user's location — the platter rises back to full speed across Sunrise (DA1), Vinyl's character drifts warmer at night and brighter in the morning around the user's own settings (DA2, Vinyl only — Pure stays untouched, F1), Sundown and Character merge into one four-tab-bar "Sundown" tab with a Day/Sound switch and the sky follows the whole day in both themes (DA3), a new "Nychthemeron" theme option follows sunrise/sunset directly (DA4), the profile learns and can share a per-phase listening-rhythm stat (DA5, migration `007_rhythm.sql`), and the phase a record was played in travels with "now spinning" and share cards (DA6, migration `008_spin_phase.sql`). Six phases bound the day (Night/Dawn/Morning/Midday/Afternoon/Golden hour/Twilight, by solar altitude — dawn/dusk at −6°, sunrise/sunset at −0.833°), fixing three gaps in today's Sundown: night currently ends at midnight rather than at dawn, sun times go stale if the app stays open past midnight, and there's no morning behavior at all. Each PR stops and waits for Ken's go-ahead before the next one starts, same as the D-series. `docs/design/day-arc/` (added alongside this doc, in the same docs-only PR, per the doc's own first instruction) holds the design handoff: nine `.dc.html` design-canvas source files (`Main`, `MorningLight`, `NightPreview`, `Character`, `ProfileSettings`, `SkyPalette`, `DesktopSidebar`, `Rhythm`, `SpunAt` — text, not renderable by opening directly in a browser, per that folder's own `README.md`) plus the README itself, which also carries the full, already-contrast-checked (≥4.5:1) light/dark sky-token table for all eight day-phase anchors and the phase-colour table DA5's rhythm ring uses — every later DA PR's own prompt points back at this README rather than re-deriving either table. **RF14 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`) added a new "Moving palette rules" section to this README**, per that step's own instruction ("the day arc changes the sky; this PR adds rules the moving colors must obey... `docs/design/day-arc/README.md` gains a section for them") — the four RF14 rules themselves (muted follows the sky, accent stays warmest, the dial ring borrows this file's own anchor colors, three readable dark levels) plus the dial-ring color table and the muted/muted-2 hex pairs, cross-referenced against `docs/design/refine/README.md`'s own pre-existing "Colour (RF12, RF14)" section (the literal values both this section and the implementation were taken from).
- **Tabs design unification (branch `tabs-design-unify`)** supersedes parts of the DA3/Settings descriptions above: the Day view's `#preview` slider and Back-to-live button are gone (the dial plus `.sd-now-btn` is the only preview control); Scratch, Sleeve Pull, Launch intro, Background playback and the wireless note live in static `#playbackSettings` markup parked in `#playbackSettingsStash` (in `#route-profile`), which `renderAppSettingsScreen()` borrows under "Playback & behavior" and `renderProfile()` returns before wiping `#profileBody` (same reason `#libTools` is static: listeners and ids must survive re-renders). Settings no longer has a Library tools row. Home's section heads and feed card use the shared mono label style (`#homeBody` rules in `25-day-arc-v2.css`), with no inline styles in `social/screens/home.js`. Sundown › Sound's three `[data-preset]` buttons are still the fast way to set character, now styled as a `.sd-preset-seg` segmented control matching the Vinyl-drift seg right above it (same view, same "pick one of three" shape) rather than the old stacked `.cta.ghost` rectangles, and `wireCharacter()` marks whichever was clicked `aria-pressed` (session-only, not persisted or restored on reload). A later follow-up re-added per-value fine-tuning, but tucked behind each `.sd-charcard` instead of restored as an always-visible block: each card is now a `<button data-char-tune>`, and tapping it reveals a single `<input type=range>` in a sibling `.sd-charcard-fine` panel (`id="sdChar{Wow,Sat,Soft,Comp}Fine"`, `hidden` by default, `grid-column:1/-1` so it drops into its own row inside the 2×2 grid) — only one open at a time, seeded from `S.char[key]` at the moment it's revealed rather than kept in sync at boot, so there's no `storage/meta.js` write-back loop to maintain the way the pre-removal sliders needed; moving it un-presses whichever preset was last clicked, since it's now a deliberate deviation from that starting point. The Profile tab's Top Album/Top Song tiles (`.profile-top-tile`), the Achievements section (`div.profile-section`) and its empty states (`.profile-empty`) were also brought onto the same card family the storefront redesign (PR #109/#110) and this branch's own Sound-tab cards already use — 14px-radius `var(--panel)`/`var(--raised)` fill with a `var(--line)` border, 8px dashed for an empty/not-yet-filled state — replacing an older flatter 4px-radius/translucent-white look that had been left behind; `button.profile-section` (Collection) already had this look and is unchanged, and the div/button split `src/styles/23-desktop.css`'s desktop grid relies on for placement is untouched. **RF9 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "Sundown, set once") supersedes the Day-view half of this bullet's own opening clause**: the `#preview` slider/Back-to-live button were already gone per that clause, and RF9 additionally removed the Day view's three stat cards (`.sd-cards`) and the old four-row rate/manual/target/pace block, replacing both with the single `.seg.sd-depth-seg` described in `miziki.html`'s own entry above; `25-day-arc-v2.css` dropped the now-dead `.sd-cards`/`.sd-card*` and `.sound-dial*`/`.sd-sky-bg-sound` rules and added `.sd-advanced-toggle` (the new Sound-view disclosure button) and `.sd-info` (a native `<details>`/`<summary>`-based "(i)" disclosure, `summary::-webkit-details-marker` hidden, used for the Character-starting-point explainer). **RF11 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 5, "every helper is one line of caption text; anything longer sits behind an (i)") reused this exact `.sd-info` shape for the four `#playbackSettings` rows** (Scratch, Sleeve Pull, Launch intro, Background playback): each row's `<em>` caption was shortened to one sentence, with the rest moved into a sibling `<details class="sd-info">` — except `#bgAudioNote`, left as a plain, JS-owned `<em>` sibling (not nested inside the `<details>`) since `src/player/background.js`'s `bgUpdateUI()` overwrites its `.textContent` directly on every render and a nested `<details>` child would be silently destroyed by that overwrite.
- `docs/CEREMONY.md` (Oct 14, 2026 — design frames: https://claude.ai/artifact/D9AhvQ7oYjDBLWhtkxX1pY) is the approved spec for the record ceremonies and the custom slipmat: the Vinyl/Pure toggle, the end-of-queue run-out groove and empty slipmat, the side flip, the overlapped record change (new sleeve arrives at 700 ms), the plain-record launch intro that replaces the real-art one, and the on-device custom slipmat. It ends with a five-PR plan (spec, toggle, empty platter/groove/change/flip, launch, slipmat), one feature per PR. Its shared rules restate the iOS `bgPrime()`-before-any-`await` rule (rule 9) and the Pure-skips-every-ceremony rule for every PR that follows.
- `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md` (Oct 14, 2026) is the approved six-PR navigation-performance plan (NV1-NV6): a measurement baseline plus quick wins, a single history-based navigation stack (Back closes the top sheet/overlay everywhere), stable scroll position across tabs and re-renders, instant Home/Profile with pull-to-refresh, gentle motion and swipe-to-close, and large-library scrolling cost. Same one-PR-at-a-time, gated-on-Ken's-go-ahead shape as `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`/`docs/DAY_ARC.md`.
- `tests/perf/` (NV1; extended RF5, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 4) — `nav.perf.js`, the navigation-performance measurement harness (`npm run perf`, not run in CI — see its own header comment and the Commands section below), plus `lib/synthetic-library.js` (a 2,000-track/180-album fixture, no real audio, shaped like `src/library/import.js`'s `storeTrack()` records so `restoreLibrary()` loads it unmodified — since RF5, also exports `buildSyntheticLocalState()`, a steady-state `toDoc()` input built from that same fixture) and `lib/static-server.js` (serves the built `dist/` under `/Miziki/` for the headless browser to load). Writes `docs/perf/BASELINE.md`. Since RF5, every timed measurement runs **15 repetitions**, reported as median/p95 rather than one sample (one run is noisy enough — a GC pause, another process on the machine — that a single sample can read as a regression or an improvement that isn't real); and two new rows: `restoreLibrary()`'s own wall-clock time, reload to the library actually restored, at 2,000 tracks (also median/p95 over 15 reloads), and `library_backup.doc`'s own byte size at the same library size (`JSON.stringify(toDoc(...))`, UTF-8 — a single deterministic computation, not a timing sample, so no median/p95 for this one row).
- `vite.config.js` builds `miziki.html` (not `index.html`) as the one entry point, output to `dist/`. `base` reads from the `MIZIKI_BASE` env var, defaulting to `/Miziki/` when unset — the GitHub Actions workflow never sets it, so the real GitHub Pages deploy is unaffected; see "Deploy previews" below for the other value it takes. It also copies `src/miziki-social.js` into `dist/src/miziki-social.js` verbatim — Vite only bundles `type="module"` scripts referenced from HTML, so the plain classic script above needs that explicit copy step or it would 404 in the built app. Since step D2, a second plugin (`copy-theme-boot`) does the exact same thing for `src/theme-boot.js` — the anti-flash head script, also a classic script Vite would otherwise leave untouched.
- `netlify.toml` configures Netlify's PR deploy previews. Production builds (pushes to `main`) are deliberately skipped there — `miziki.netlify.app` no longer updates. See "Deploy previews" below.

## How the app is put together

- **Global state** lives in one object, `S` (audio context, tracks, queue, mode, character settings, sessions, etc.), plus ~89 other top-level variables and ~536 top-level functions that share scope. Expect hidden coupling through `S` and these globals.
- **Audio:** Web Audio graph (`AudioContext`, `decodeAudioData`). There are two paths: **Pure** (reference — the character chain fully disconnected, not just turned down, **and** the playback rate always exactly 1.0; "Pure means untouched") and **Vinyl** (wow & flutter → saturation → top-end softening → bus compression, in that order, **and** Sundown's rate change). Sundown (auto and manual) only ever reaches the recording in Vinyl — `computeRate()` in `src/sundown/solar.js` returns `1` outright when `S.mode === 'pure'`, the one place that rule lives (see F1). The sleep-timer fade and the user's own volume still apply in Pure: those are level changes downstream of the recording, not changes to it, so "untouched" doesn't cover them.
- **Scratch (F2):** in Vinyl, with the Character menu's Scratch toggle on (default), spinning the disc by hand plays the real track forward/backward at the hand's speed via an `AudioWorkletNode` (`src/audio/scratch-processor.js`), joining the Vinyl chain at the same point the normal source does. Scratch off, or Pure mode, keeps spin-to-scrub as an invented, silent fast-navigation gesture (as it always was). Scratch is saved in `S.char.scratch` alongside the sliders; a saved-prefs object from before F2 has no such field, and a missing value means On.
- **Background playback** is optional and routes through a media element plus `navigator.mediaSession` for lock-screen controls. It works on iPhone today, so don't regress it.
- **Persistence:** IndexedDB database `miziki`, **version 4**, with stores `tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays`, `artwork`, `profile`, `achievements` and `collection`.
