It holds the original files plus tags, so the library survives restarts. If storage is unavailable, every call no-ops and the app runs session-only.
  - **Never rename the database or drop or rename a store.** Users' libraries live there. Schema changes go through a version bump with a non-destructive `onupgradeneeded`.
  - **Any future version bump must come with a new upgrade test in `tests/unit/idb.test.js`** that starts from the previous version's real store layout, with data in it, and confirms every old record survives the upgrade and only the new stores/fields are added. This is the test in the "upgrade keeps data" case that would fail if anyone ever wrote a destructive upgrade — see step 4a.
  - **`onversionchange`/`onblocked` (fixed RF5, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 3 — was a known, deliberately-deferred gap before this step).** `DB.open()` (`src/storage/idb.js`) now closes this tab's own connection and sets `DB.staleVersion`/`DB.ok = false` the moment another tab starts a real version bump (`onversionchange`) — every `DB.*` method already treats `ok:false` as a quiet no-op, the same safe degradation storage-unavailable already had, so nothing forks; `DB.onNotice(fn)` is how `main.js` turns that into the user-visible `#dbStaleBanner`, "Miziki updated in another tab. Reload." Separately, `DB.blocked` is set when *this* tab's own `open()` is held up by another tab already holding an older-version connection (`onblocked`) — `restoreLibrary()` (`main.js`) reads it to show "Miziki is open in another tab. Close it there, then reload this one." instead of the old, same-for-both-cases "lasts only for the session... it persists" message, which used to read as an invitation to add songs that would fork this device's library once the blocking tab closed.
  - **Every new saved setting goes into both `serializePrefs` and `parsePrefs`** (`src/storage/prefs.js`, step 4b), **gets a default in `S`** (`src/state.js`), **and gets a test in `tests/unit/prefs.test.js`** that an old saved object without it still loads, with that default in place.
  - **`src/history/` is the only place that writes listening counters** (play counts, session counts, the metal-eligible shadow counters, rare unlocks, last-played timestamps — step 5c). Anything elsewhere that needs to touch one of these should go through `played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js`'s exported functions rather than writing `S.trackPlayCounts`/`S.sessionCounts`/etc. directly.
  - **"Forget library" (`forgetLibrary()`, `src/library/delete.js`) removes your songs and keeps your listening stats** (step 02, F3 spec) — it clears only the `tracks` store, crate-art tiers, and thumbnails nothing else still needs; `meta`/`sessions`/`overlays`/`achievements`/`collection` are never touched, so playlists (incl. Favorites), track edits, custom covers, manual order/sort and sealed state all wait and reconnect automatically once a file with the same id is re-added. "Clear listening history" (above) is the one place that resets stats — the two mean clearly different things now, and should stay that way. **Note for account backup (steps 03+):** because Forget library never touches stats or settings, turning it on needs no account prompt and the same `device_id` carries through — there's nothing backup-relevant for it to disturb.
  - IndexedDB is tied to the origin. Moving the app to a new domain (or a native shell) starts users with an empty library unless there is a migration or export path. The same goes for saved prefs in the `meta` store, including toggles like **Background playback** — a fresh origin starts with `S.bgAudio = false` (the default in `state.js`), even if it's on at the usual URL. This is why background/lock-screen playback can look broken on a Netlify preview the first time: the fix is to flip the toggle on there too, not a code change.
- **Backup: what gets synced** (step 03, B2 — merge logic; step 05, B1 — the server-side table; step 06, B3 — the app actually wires it in; step 07, B4 — custom covers and the profile picture, via Supabase **Storage**, not the `library_backup` doc). The client-side merge logic (`src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`), the `public.library_backup` table (`supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql`) and the sync engine that calls both (`src/sync/backup.js`) all exist and are live: signed in, a device debounce-uploads changed fields (~30s, piggybacking `storage/meta.js`'s existing `queueSave` timing), flushes immediately on backgrounding (`src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` handlers), and pulls-and-merges on launch/foreground. Signed out, nothing is ever uploaded — `scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync` both check for a real Supabase client first and quietly no-op without one. Audio, embedded art, generated crate tiers/thumbnails, and anything that already syncs through the social layer are never backed up — the music stays on the device, even signed in; **a custom cover and the profile picture are the one exception, backed up since step 07, but as real image bytes in Storage, never inside the `doc` blob itself** — see the table below. **Rule: every new saved key goes into this table** (as backed up, device-only, or excluded) when it's added, the same way every new saved *setting* already goes into `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` above. See the step 06 and step 07 changelog entries for exactly how each field below gets its data into (and out of) `S`, and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" further below for the privacy-policy-facing version of this same table.

  **RF1 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`): a clear on one device stays cleared everywhere.** `fromDocs()`'s `ownReset` flag (true when another device's `historyClearedAt` is newer than this one's own) means this device must zero *and persist* every per-device history counter, not just hold the newer clear timestamp in memory — `applyMerged()` (`src/sync/backup.js`) does this by awaiting a `'reset'` event (`emitAwait`, not the fire-and-forget `emit()` every other event uses) that `main.js` answers by calling `history/clear.js`'s exported `resetLocalHistory()`, the same zero-and-persist routine a local "Clear listening history" tap uses for its own full-scope branch. One gap this can't close: plays made **offline on this device, after another device's clear but before this device next syncs,** are reset along with everything else once that sync happens — a play has no timestamp of its own in any of the per-device counters it lands in (`trackPlayCounts`, `sessionCounts`, etc. are plain `{key: count}` maps), so there's nothing to compare against the remote clear's own stamp and nothing to separate out as "after the clear, keep these." This is accepted, not a bug: the alternative (timestamping every single play) is a much bigger change for a rare, low-stakes edge case.

  **`library_backup`'s own privacy rule**: one row per `(user_id, device_id)`, owner-only RLS (select/insert/update/delete all require `user_id = auth.uid()`) — no view, feed, RPC or policy anywhere in the schema ever reads another user's row, and `revoke all ... from anon` means a signed-out request can't even try. The server never looks inside `doc` (a `jsonb` blob, capped at 2MB) — every merge rule in the table below is enforced entirely client-side, in `src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`, not by anything in Postgres. This is also why two devices can never corrupt each other's data at the row level: each device owns and writes only its own `(user_id, device_id)` row, and reads every row for that `user_id` (its own and every other device's) to merge locally.

  | Data | Class | Merge rule |
  |---|---|---|
  | `trackPlayCounts`, `sessionCounts`, `metalEligibleSessions`, `trackMetalEligiblePlays` | backed up | per-device counters: the total is the sum over devices |
  | `phasePlayCounts`, `albumPhaseCounts` (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md` — the day-arc phase, counted by the real clock only, `sundown/solar.js`'s `realDayPosition()`, never `S.previewMin` — see `history/played.js`'s own entry) | backed up | per-device counters, same shape and rule as `trackPlayCounts`/`sessionCounts` just above — `albumPhaseCounts` is the one two-level exception, summed per album *and* per phase by `sync/merge.js`'s own `sumNestedCounters()` rather than the flat `sumCounters()` the others use |
  | `trackLastPlayed`, `albumLastPlayed` | backed up | max |
  | `rareUnlocked`, `achievements`, `collection` | backed up | union; on overlap, the earliest earned date wins |
  | `S.sealed` (sealed records) | backed up | a seal broken on any device stays broken everywhere, independent of any history clear (SEALED spec §2 holds across devices too) |
  | `sessions` store (per-album in-progress play-through) | **excluded, device-only** | this is resume state for one physical play-through within a 4-hour gap on *one* device — merging two devices' in-progress sessions could corrupt the 95%/in-order completion rule itself. Nothing is lost: a session only ever affects anything durable once it *completes*, and that effect (`sessionCounts`) is already a backed-up, per-device-summed counter |
  | `overlays` (track edits, text only) | backed up | last edit wins by timestamp; a revert is a tombstone that also wins by timestamp |
  | `playlists` (incl. Favorites), `albumOrder`, `albumSort`, `albumDisplay`, `trackDisplay`, `albumEdge`, `albumLookPref` | backed up | last write wins, **per field** (not per whole doc — a later change to one of these never clobbers an earlier change to another) |
  | `prefs`, profile display name | backed up | last write wins |
  | a custom cover's own image bytes (step 07, B4 — Supabase **Storage**, `covers` bucket, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, private/owner-only; the *claim* — `{hash, updatedAt}` — travels in the `library_backup` doc as `covers[identityKey]`, but the bytes themselves never do) | backed up | last write wins per track identity key, same shape/timestamp as the overlay it rides alongside — a revert is a tombstone (`hash: null`) that wins the same way |
  | the profile picture's own image bytes, which doubles as the public account avatar once signed in (step 07, B4 — Supabase **Storage**, `avatars` bucket, path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, **publicly readable**, owner-only write; the claim, `picture: {hash, updatedAt}`, travels in the doc the same way `profileName` does) | backed up | last write wins, **per field**, same shape as `profileName`/`prefs` above |
  | listening rhythm's own shared snapshot — `{verdict, shares}` only, never `phasePlayCounts`/`albumPhaseCounts` themselves (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md` — a *separate* Supabase column, `profiles.listening_rhythm jsonb`, migration `007_rhythm.sql`, not inside the `library_backup` doc at all, the same "real data elsewhere, not the doc blob" shape the two Storage-bucket rows above already have) | **opt-in** (default off), published only while the Privacy screen's own "Share my listening rhythm" switch is on and at least `RHYTHM_MIN_PLAYS` (20) plays have been counted | not a merge at all — there's exactly one writer (this device, whenever it's the one that's shared), so "last write wins" is trivially true; read by anyone `private.can_view(owner, null)` allows, via the new `get_listening_rhythm()` RPC — see "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" below |
  | `tracks` (audio, embedded art), `artwork` store's crate-tier/thumbnail blobs, `thumb:*` blobs | **excluded** | embedded art returns with the file; crate tiers and thumbnails are rebuilt locally from whatever art — embedded, custom, or a freshly-downloaded cover — is on the device |
  | social meta (Supabase-side ratings/notes/pins/follows) | **excluded** | already syncs through the social layer |
  | `deviceId` (random UUID, created once per install) | **device-only** | not uploaded as backed-up data; it's the row key (B1) |
  | `theme` (step D2, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md` — Auto/Light/Dark/Nychthemeron, the last added step DA4) | **device-only** | part of `prefs` locally (`storage/prefs.js`), but stripped out of the copy `sync/backup.js`'s `buildLocalState()` uploads — an appearance choice follows the device, not the account, per that doc's own default |
  | the custom slipmat (CEREMONY §6): the `slipmatCustom`/`slipmatWordmark`/`slipmatDim` flags in `prefs`, and the 1024px image in the `artwork` store under `slipmat:custom` (`src/ui/slipmat.js`) | **device-only** | the flags are stripped from the uploaded `prefs` like `theme`; the image is never uploaded, never in the backup doc, never in Download my data |
  | the `miziki-sun-times` cache (step DA4, `docs/DAY_ARC.md` — `localStorage`, `{date, rise, set}`, no coordinates) | **device-only** | never in `prefs`, never in the backup doc, never in Download my data, never sent anywhere — written by `sundown/solar.js`'s `persistSunTimesCache()` (called from `computeSun()`/`location.js`'s `fallbackSun()`) purely so `src/theme-boot.js`'s pre-paint script can resolve a pinned Nychthemeron choice to light/dark before `S.sun`/any module exists, without ever storing a location |
  | `S.rhythmPushedAt` (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`) | **device-only** | the last time *this device* actually pushed the shared rhythm snapshot (`sync/backup.js`'s `maybePushListeningRhythm()`/`pushOrClearListeningRhythm()`) — pure sync-cadence bookkeeping, the same class of local-only timestamp as `uploadedImageHashes` in the same file; never backed up, never shown to the user, never in Download my data |
  | the `miziki-home-feed-cache` cache (step NV4, `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md` — `localStorage`, the Home feed's own already-fetched event rows only, never a token) | **device-only** | never in `prefs`, never in the backup doc, never in Download my data — written by `src/social/screens/home.js`'s `refreshHome()` purely so a return visit to Home shows the feed immediately instead of a blank "Loading…"; cleared by `clearHomeFeedCache()`, called from every sign-out/account-deletion path (`src/profile/settings.js`) |

  `S.charDrift` (step DA2, `docs/DAY_ARC.md` — the day-arc character-drift intensity, `'off'`/`'subtle'`/`'full'`) needed **no new row** here, per the rule just above — the decision this table's own instruction calls for, made explicitly rather than skipped: unlike `theme`, it rides inside `prefs` with no carve-out, since a day-arc intensity choice has no reason to stay device-only — it's an ordinary character preference, the same class as `S.char`'s four sliders or `S.char.scratch`, neither of which needed a row of its own either.

  Local state the merge logic above depends on — all four now exist, added in step 06 (B3), each in `src/state.js` unless noted:
  - `S.historyClearedAt`: a timestamp, bumped on a *full*-scope "Clear listening history" only (not the orphaned-only scope, which just prunes entries for identities no longer in the library on *this* device — it isn't "I want my stats reset," and forcing it onto other devices could wipe stats they still have every reason to keep). Stamped in `src/history/clear.js`'s full-scope branch, persisted to `meta`.
  - `S.sealedBroken`, a `Set` of album ids ever sealed-then-broken on this device — the durable "ever broken" record `src/library/sealed.js`'s `breakSeal()` was missing before this step (it only ever removed an id from `S.sealed`, with nothing recording it was *ever* sealed) — which `src/sync/merge.js`'s "broken anywhere stays broken" rule needs to tell "broken" apart from "this device never had the album at all." Persisted via a new `persistBrokenSeals()` alongside the existing `persistSealedAlbums()`.
  - `S.backupUpdatedAt`, one stamp per backed-up per-field-last-write-wins key (`playlists`, `albumOrder`, `albumSort`, `albumDisplay`, `trackDisplay`, `albumEdge`, `albumLookPref`, `prefs`, `profileName`, and, since step 07, `picture`) — stamped by `touchBackupField(name)` (`src/state.js`) at each field's own real mutation site: `src/library/view.js` (playlist/album-order edits), `src/library/favorites.js` (`toggleFavorite`), `src/library/delete.js` (`deleteTracks`' playlist/order filtering), `src/library/thumbs.js` (`persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`/`persistAlbumEdge`), `src/history/sessions.js` (`persistAlbumLookPref`), `src/profile/screens.js` (`persistProfile`), `src/sync/backup.js`'s own `queueAvatarUpload` (step 07, for `picture`), and `src/storage/meta.js`'s own `saveMeta()` for `prefs`. Since RF16 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`, "edits that know what they've seen"), each stamp is `{seq, at}`, not a bare `Date.now()` — `seq` from the new `S.backupSeq` Lamport counter (device-only, persisted/restored the same way as `deviceId`, via `src/storage/meta.js`'s `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()`), bumped by `touchBackupField()` on every call and, separately, bumped to the max of itself and every `seq` seen across a pull's remote rows by `src/sync/backup.js`'s `pullAndMergeBackup()` (via `src/sync/merge.js`'s new `maxSeqAcross()`) — so an edit made after observing another device's stamp always out-ranks it, regardless of clock skew. `src/sync/backup-doc.js`'s `lw()` helper attaches the doc-level `deviceId` to every stamp it builds (one `toDoc()` call is always exactly one device's contribution, so there's no need to store a device id redundantly inside the stamp itself at touch time — `S.deviceId` can still be `null` long after a field was first touched, before the very first sign-in). `src/sync/merge.js`'s `lastWriteWins()` compares `seq`, then `at`, then `deviceId` (a last, deterministic tiebreak two edits can otherwise tie on) — a stamp with no `seq` at all (a v2-or-earlier doc, from before this field existed) reads as `0`, so it always loses to a real edit from an RF16-aware device.
  - `S.profile.pictureHash` (step 07, B4, `src/state.js`) — the profile picture's own re-encoded SHA-256, `null` until a picture has ever been uploaded or downloaded on this device; set by `src/sync/backup.js`'s `queueAvatarUpload`/`queueAvatarDownload`, persisted alongside the rest of the profile record (`storage/repo.js`'s `profile` store).
  - a timestamp on each overlay write, and on each revert: `src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()` now stores `editedAt: Date.now()` alongside the edited fields, and `revertTrack()`/`undoLastEdit()`'s "no prior overlay" branch now writes a tombstone (`{id, fields: null, reverted: true, revertedAt: Date.now()}`, and since step 07 also `cover: null` in the same tombstone — a revert clears the backed-up cover claim the same way it clears the text fields) instead of deleting the overlay row outright — `loadOverlayFor()`/`applyEdit()` already treat a falsy `fields` as "nothing to apply," so this is a pure addition to the row's persisted shape, not a behavior change for local-only use, and it's exactly the `{id, fields, editedAt, reverted, revertedAt}` shape `backup-doc.js`'s overlay conversion already expected.

  **RF4 (`docs/12_REFINE.md`): one number for sessions.** `trackPlayCounts`/`sessionCounts`/`metalEligibleSessions`/`trackMetalEligiblePlays` each stay per-device counters, summed on every pull (`sync/merge.js`'s `mergeRows()`) exactly as the table above already says — nothing about the merge rule itself changed. What changed is that the sum is no longer thrown away the moment the restore summary that briefly used it is built: `applyMerged()` (`src/sync/backup.js`) now keeps it as its own value, `S.mergedStats` (`{trackPlayCounts, sessionCounts, metalEligibleSessions, trackMetalEligiblePlays, phasePlayCounts, albumPhaseCounts}`, `src/state.js`), in memory and persisted to `meta` so it survives a reload — **never** written back into the per-device counters themselves (the same rule `rareUnlocked`/`achievements`/`collection` already follow, for the same double-counting reason). `history/achievements.js`'s `topAlbums()`/`topTracks()`, `record-art/metals.js`'s `albumMetalFor()`/`trackMetalFor()`, and `miziki-social.js`'s `mizikiAdapter`'s own `sessions:` field all read `S.mergedStats` in place of the matching local counter whenever it's non-null, so a session logged on one device and a session logged on another both count toward the one number every signed-in surface shows, including whatever a friend's view would push to `verified_stats` — before this step, those three surfaces (a device's own display, the cross-device sum, and `verified_stats.full_album_sessions` merged by `greatest()`) could each show a different count for the same album ("What the audit found," item 4). `null` signed out or before the first pull, so every reader's fallback to its own local counter is unchanged from before this step. `supabase/migrations/011_authoritative_stats.sql` changes `sync_stats`'s own `full_album_sessions` column from a `greatest()` to a plain overwrite, since the client now always sends this same authoritative merged total rather than one device's own, possibly-lower count; `unlocked_variant` is untouched, still a `coalesce`. No RPC anywhere in `supabase/migrations/` actually returns `verified_stats` back to any caller today, so there is no existing friend-facing "verified"/"spins" UI copy to rename yet — that migration's own header comment records **spins**, never **verified**, as the word for whichever future PR builds that surface.

  `src/sync/merge.js` and `src/sync/backup-doc.js` are still pure functions over a `localState` snapshot shaped like the above; `src/sync/backup.js` (step 06) is what builds that snapshot from the real `S`/IndexedDB, calls `toDoc`/`fromDocs`, and reads/writes the real `library_backup` row — see that file's own repo-layout entry and the Step 06 changelog entry below for exactly how.
- **Social config:** since step 04, `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (`src/state.js`) point at the real Supabase project (`https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co`, the publishable key) — the social layer is live, not inert. `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` holds Supabase's newer *publishable* key (`sb_publishable_…`), not the legacy JWT-shaped anon key Supabase retires at the end of 2026; the **secret key** (`sb_secret_…`) must never go in this repo. supabase-js is bundled (step 01 — see "Security" below), not CDN-loaded; the publishable key is safe in client code because RLS is the real protection.
- **Reserved handles** (step 05, B1): `admin`, `administrator`, `support`, `help`, `miziki`, `official`, `security`, `moderator`, `mod`, `staff`, `team`, `root`, `system`, `null`, `undefined` can never be claimed — enforced in two places that must stay in sync: a client-side pre-check (`RESERVED_HANDLES` in `src/miziki-social.js`, checked by `checkHandle`/`createProfile`/`updateProfile` before any request goes out) and the real enforcement, a `before insert or update of handle` trigger on `public.profiles` in `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` (`profiles_handle_reserved`, raising `'That handle is reserved'`). It's a trigger, not a validated `check` constraint, specifically so it only ever fires on a *future* write — no handle already claimed before this migration is retroactively checked or broken.
- **Account deletion** (step 05, B1 — the RPC; step 06, B3 — the app calls it): `public.delete_my_account()` (a `security definer` RPC, fixed `search_path`) deletes the caller's own row from `auth.users` — never anyone else's, since it always deletes `where id = auth.uid()` and raises if the caller isn't signed in. Every downstream row (the `profiles` row, and everything that cascades from it: `follows`, `blocks`, `crate_items`, `verified_stats`, `now_spinning`, `digging_list`, `feed_events`, `reports`, plus `library_backup`) is removed automatically by the existing `on delete cascade` foreign keys — this function doesn't delete from any of those tables itself. Irreversible. Since step 06, the Account screen's "Delete account" row (type-your-handle-to-confirm) calls `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()`, which calls this RPC, resets local social state, and signs out — see the step 06 changelog entry.
- `?social=demo` turns on a dev-only demo mode for the social screens. It must not run in production.
- Fonts are self-hosted (step 01 — `src/fonts/`, `src/styles/fonts.css`), not loaded from Google Fonts. Antonio, Archivo, IBM Plex Mono.

## What's stored server-side, and who can see it

(Step 06, B3; the two Storage rows below added step 07, B4.) The flip side of "Backup: what gets synced" above — written for the privacy policy and the App Store privacy label, not for a developer extending the sync engine. Every table below lives in the user's own Supabase project; Miziki makes no other third-party requests (see "Security" below). **Audio files, embedded art, and location (used only for Sundown's sun times) never leave the device, signed in or not** — nothing in this repo ever uploads any of the three, in any table or bucket, under any column. **A custom cover and the profile picture are the one exception**: since step 07 (B4), a re-encoded, EXIF/GPS-stripped copy of each is uploaded to Supabase Storage (never the original file bytes, and never as part of the `library_backup.doc` blob) — see the two Storage rows below for exactly who can see each.

| Server-side table/column | What it holds | Who can see it |
|---|---|---|
| `public.library_backup.doc` (one row per device, `jsonb`, ≤2MB) | Listening stats (play/session counts, last-played timestamps), achievements/Collection/rare-unlock state, sealed/broken-seal state, track edits (title/artist/album text, never audio or art), playlists and manual ordering/sort/display prefs, app settings (`prefs`), the profile display name, and (step 07) a *claim* about each custom cover/the profile picture (`{hash, updatedAt}` — a pointer into the Storage buckets below, never the image bytes themselves) — everything in the "Backup: what gets synced" table above marked "backed up." | **Only the account that owns the row.** Owner-only RLS (`user_id = auth.uid()`) on every operation; `anon` has no grant at all; no view, feed, RPC or policy anywhere in the schema reads another user's `library_backup` row. The server itself never parses `doc` — it's an opaque blob to Postgres. |
| Storage bucket `covers` (step 07, B4; one file per unique re-encoded image, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`) | A re-encoded (EXIF/GPS stripped), capped-at-1200px copy of a custom cover set in the edit sheet. | **Only the account that owns the folder.** Private bucket, owner-only read/upload/delete RLS on `storage.objects` (`(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text`) — the same shape as `library_backup`'s own row-ownership check, just on a Storage path instead of a column. |
| Storage bucket `avatars` (step 07, B4; path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`) | A re-encoded (EXIF/GPS stripped), 512px-square copy of the profile picture — the one image in this whole pipeline that's **publicly readable**, since it doubles as the account's public avatar once signed in. | **Anyone** (including signed out) can read it — that's the point, the same way `profiles`' own handle/display name/avatar can be. Only the owning account can upload or delete its own file. |
| `public.profiles` (handle, display name, avatar URL, bio) | The social-layer profile a user chooses to show. | The handle/display name/avatar are visible to anyone the user's visibility setting (private/friends/public, Privacy screen) allows — friends by default. Blocked accounts are excluded via `is_blocked_between`/`can_view` (now in the API-invisible `private` schema, step 05). |
| `public.profiles.listening_rhythm` (step DA5, `docs/DAY_ARC.md`, migration `007_rhythm.sql` — `jsonb`, ≤2000 bytes, `{verdict, shares}` only, **never** individual plays or the times they happened) | An opt-in snapshot of which part of the day a user listens in most — default **off**, and only ever written once at least 20 plays have been counted. | **Only readable where `profiles_select`'s own blanket row-select rule would otherwise still be too broad to trust with this one column** — unlike the rest of this row's own data (handle/display name/avatar, findable by anyone non-blocked), this column is gated by its own `get_listening_rhythm()` RPC, which re-checks `private.can_view(owner, null)` (the same visibility rule as the rest of the crate) before ever returning a value — not by `profiles_select` itself, which stays unchanged on purpose (see that migration's own comment for why loosening it instead would have regressed handle-findability). The owner always sees their own; everyone else sees it only if shared and visible; a blocked account never does. |
| `public.now_spinning`, `crate_items`, `digging_list`, `verified_stats` | "Now spinning" presence, crate picks, the digging list, verified play-count stats — the social layer's own opt-in sharing surface. | Same visibility rule as `profiles` — gated by the Privacy screen's share-now-spinning/share-milestones toggles (both default **off**) and the overall visibility setting, enforced by RLS using the same `private`-schema helper functions. |
| `public.follows`, `blocks` | Who follows whom; who's blocked whom. | A user's own follow/block lists are visible only to themselves via RLS; follow *counts* are the only thing exposed more broadly, through an existing RPC. |
| `public.feed_events` | The social home feed's own event log (a friend started spinning something, hit a milestone, etc.) | Visible only to accounts that could already see the underlying event per the rules above — generated from, not a bypass of, the tables it summarizes. |
| `public.reports` | User-filed reports against other accounts/content. | Not visible to the reported account or to other users — moderation-only by RLS. |
| `auth.users` (Supabase Auth, not a Miziki-defined table) | Email address, used only for the emailed one-time sign-in code. | Supabase's own auth infrastructure; Miziki never stores a password (there is none — sign-in is a 6-digit emailed code). |

**Nothing above was new as of step 06** except that `library_backup` actually held real data for the first time (migration 005/step 05 created the empty table; step 06 was the first PR that wrote real rows to it) — every other row in that version of this table described tables that predate step 06's own work. **The two Storage rows are new as of step 07** (migration 006 created the empty buckets; step 07 is the first PR that writes real files to them). **The `listening_rhythm` row is new as of step DA5** (migration `007_rhythm.sql`; this step is the first PR that writes real values to it) — the one row in this whole table that's opt-in and default-off rather than always-on-once-signed-in, and the one gated by its own RPC rather than by `profiles_select` itself. The one-sentence version, shown verbatim on the Privacy screen, is unchanged by step DA5 — it was never a claim about social sharing, only about music, location and the private backup: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."*

**Download my data** (Privacy screen, step 06): a `miziki-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json` export a user can pull straight from the app — local stats/settings/playlists/track-edit text always (no audio, no images, signed in or not), plus the account/social/backup section's own current snapshot when signed in. This is the same data inventoried above, just handed directly to the user who owns it, in the one format (JSON, not a account support ticket) that actually answers "what do you have on me."

## CSS token scale (RF13)

`src/styles/00-base.css`'s `:root` defines the app's whole type/spacing/elevation scale (RF13, `docs/12_REFINE.md`). **No font-size or spacing value outside these tokens without a comment saying why** — the rule RF13 itself asked to be written down here.

**Type** (`--fs-*` font-size, `--lh-*` its own paired line-height — a separate custom property, not bundled into one value, since the same size is reused by both a plain line and a capitalized label that each want their own line-height):

| Token | Size/line-height | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `--fs-micro`/`--lh-micro` | 10px / 1.3 | annotations: times, kHz, stat labels |
| `--fs-label`/`--lh-label` | 11px / 1.3 | tabs, tab bar, segments, eyebrows |
| `--fs-caption`/`--lh-caption` | 12px / 1.45 | helper text, sub-lines |
| `--fs-body`/`--lh-body` | 14px / 1.5 | secondary lines, paragraphs |
| `--fs-row`/`--lh-row` | 16px / 1.35 | list titles, setting names, inputs |
| `--fs-title`/`--lh-title` | 20px / 1.2 | card values, sheet titles |
| `--fs-head`/`--lh-head` | 24px / 1.15 | section heads, crate album name |
| `--fs-hero`/`--lh-hero` | 28px / 1.1 | player title, dial word |
| `--fs-display`/`--lh-display` | 36px / 1 | phase name, big stats |
| `--fs-giant`/`--lh-giant` | 44px / 1 | profile name, dial time |

Letter-spacing follows size, not a token of its own: `.10em` at `--fs-micro`, `.08em` at `--fs-label`, `.02em` on Antonio (`var(--display)`) at `--fs-head` and up, `0` otherwise. RF13's own mechanical pass only *added* this default where a rule had no letter-spacing of its own at all — a rule that already carried a different, hand-tuned value (an eyebrow at `.14em`/`.2em`, a crumb at `.1em`) was left exactly as authored; that pre-existing value *is* the "comment saying why" this rule asks for, in the sense that it's a deliberate, reviewed choice already — not something to silently flatten to the default on a later CSS change. Antonio stays to no more than two sizes per screen.

**Spacing:** `--sp-1` 4px · `--sp-2` 8px · `--sp-3` 12px · `--sp-4` 16px · `--sp-5` 24px · `--sp-6` 32px · `--sp-7` 48px · `--sp-8` 64px. A heading sits closer to what it heads than to what's above it (`--sp-6` above a heading, `--sp-2` below it). **Radii:** `--radius-control` 4px (controls), `--radius-card` 12px (cards); circles stay plain `50%`, no token.

**Elevation:** `--sh-1`/`--sh-2`/`--sh-3`, each a tight contact shadow plus a soft cast shadow in one `box-shadow` value, routed through the existing `--shadow` token so light theme (which redefines `--shadow` to a lighter value in `22-theme.css`) lifts every one of these at once. `--sh-1` is for the mini-player and buttons (not applied to either in RF13 itself — see below), `--sh-2` for cards/crate-neighbour cards/the dial, `--sh-3` for the current crate card, the platter/sleeve, and sheets/popovers.

**Buttons:** solid accent for the one primary action per screen; accent outline only for the selected option in a segmented control; real switches for on/off settings; borderless mono text for everything else; slider tracks fill at 45% accent.

**Borders:** no per-row hairline in a song/artist list (`.tracklist li`, `16-edit-sheet.css` — `:active`'s existing `--sel` tint, `00-base.css`, marks a row instead); no border-top on both `.mini-player` and `.tabbar` where they meet (`08-shell.css`) — they read as one piece of bottom chrome, so only `.tabbar`'s own top edge remains; exactly one hairline stays under a sticky header.

**RF13's own scope, for later PRs that touch these files:** the font-size mechanical sweep (238 declarations) and the spacing-scale sweep (exact px matches in `margin`/`padding`/`gap` only) cover every style file. The elevation tokens were applied only to the box-shadow declarations that clearly matched one of the three named categories above (crate cards, the launch-intro card stack, the gatefold cover, profile/storefront cards, the sleeve/morph disc, the two header popovers, the profile settings menu) — `--sh-1` (mini-player/buttons) was left for a later pass since neither currently has a shadow to replace, and adding one where none existed is a visible addition, not a mechanical swap. The button-hierarchy and 45%-accent-slider rules are a design review across the whole app rather than a bounded find-and-replace; RF13 states them here as the standing rule for new CSS, but didn't re-audit every existing `.cta`/`.seg`/range input against them.

## Security

- **Miziki makes no third-party requests at runtime, other than the user's own Supabase project, when one is configured.** Everything else the app needs — fonts, the social client library, the app's own code — is bundled or self-hosted; nothing is fetched from a CDN. **Rule: a future change that adds a new external host (a CDN, an API, an embed) needs both a Content Security Policy update (below) and a privacy note here saying what that host receives and when.**
- **Content Security Policy** (`miziki.html`'s `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` — GitHub Pages serves static files with no way to set a real HTTP response header, so a meta tag is the only option, and it's a second line of defense, not the only one): `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' blob: data: https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co; media-src 'self' blob:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' blob: https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co wss://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co; worker-src 'self' blob:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; object-src 'none'`. `style-src` needs `'unsafe-inline'` because the app writes many inline `style="..."` attributes directly (crate/gatefold pose math, share-canvas layout, disc backgrounds, …) — there is no inline `<script>` anywhere, so `script-src` has no matching exception. `worker-src 'self' blob:` is what lets the AudioWorklet scratch processor (`src/audio/scratch-processor.js`, loaded same-origin via `new URL(...)`, F2) register at all; `img-src`/`media-src`'s `blob:` covers locally-decoded artwork and audio, never a network fetch. **`connect-src`'s `blob:` was missing from step 01's original policy and added in step 02** once Playwright verification for that step turned up that `src/crate/tiers.js`'s `buildCrateTier()` and `src/library/thumbs.js`'s `makeThumb()` both `fetch()` a track's own blob: object URL to re-read its embedded cover art before resizing it — without `blob:` here, every one of those fetches was silently blocked by the browser and swallowed by its own `catch(_e){return false}`, so crate art and thumbnails never actually built for any file with embedded artwork (or a custom cover) once the CSP shipped. Caught and fixed before step 02 merged, not a separate incident.
  - **Step 04 added the real Supabase project's own origins**, once `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` stopped being empty: `https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co` in both `connect-src` and `img-src`, and `wss://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co` in `connect-src` — the realtime "now spinning" subscription needs the websocket origin, REST/auth calls and any Storage-hosted avatar need the https one. Without these, sign-in fails closed with a CSP violation rather than a clear error.
- **Supabase client is bundled, not CDN-loaded** (`@supabase/supabase-js`, an exact-pinned dependency in `package.json`). `main.js`'s `restoreLibrary()` only `await import()`s it when `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` are both non-empty, passed to `MizikiSocial.init()` as `opts.supabaseLib` — the same dynamic-import-for-code-splitting shape as the `?social=demo` chunk. Before step 04, with both keys empty, Rollup proved the import's branch unreachable and eliminated it entirely — confirmed by grepping the built main chunk for any trace of supabase-js (none) — so it added zero bytes to what every user downloaded. **Since step 04, real keys mean this is now a genuinely separate, lazily-fetched chunk** (confirmed in both built targets: a ~231 kB/~60 kB-gzip chunk, fetched only once sign-in is actually opened, main chunk size unchanged). `src/miziki-social.js`'s `init()` has no CDN fallback — passing `supabaseLib` (or a real global at `root.supabase`, e.g. for a test harness that loads it another way) is the only way to give it a client library; if neither is available, `init()` fails closed (inert, same as missing keys) rather than ever fetching one.
- **Tag text (song title/artist/album — untrusted, since it comes from a file's own embedded metadata) must never reach `innerHTML`/`outerHTML`/`insertAdjacentHTML` with anything but a plain string literal.** Build the DOM with `textContent`/`createElement`/`createTextNode` instead. Enforced by an ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` rule (`eslint.config.js`, step 01) that errors on exactly that shape; the small number of genuinely static markup literals already in the codebase (`'Mi<span>zi</span>ki'`, clearing something with `= ''`) are unaffected, since the rule only objects to a *non-literal* right-hand side/argument. Two real instances of this bug were found and fixed in step 01 — see that step's own changelog entry.
- **Server-side RLS-helper functions never belong in a schema the Data API exposes** (step 05, fixing four findings a Supabase Security Advisor scan surfaced on Oct 12): `is_blocked_between`, `item_visibility`, `effective_visibility` and `can_view` are `security definer` functions that decide what's visible to whom — before step 05 they lived in `public`, reachable by anyone (even signed out) directly via `/rest/v1/rpc/<name>`, which could leak whether two users had blocked each other or whether a private crate item existed, independent of any RLS policy. `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` moved all four into a new `private` schema (never added to Supabase's exposed Data API schema list) and revoked `public`/`anon` execute on them, keeping `authenticated` (which the RLS policies that call them still need). A function body that calls one of the four by name has to reference the new `private.`-qualified name — a policy itself doesn't, since it resolves to a function's OID at the moment the policy is created, not by looking up the name again later. The same migration fixed two smaller Advisor findings: `releases_insert`'s policy was `with check (true)` (anyone could pre-register a popular album's `release_key` with a fake title/artist) — fixed by adding a `created_by uuid default auth.uid()` column and changing the check to `created_by = auth.uid()` **(a traceability fix, not a correctness one — `created_by` just lets a bad row be found and cleaned; it doesn't stop a determined user from winning the race on a given `release_key`, since `sync_crate`/`set_now_spinning` are still `security invoker` inserting as whoever got there first. Before a public launch, replace this with real canonical release identity — the planned MusicBrainz pass — or server-verified keys, so `release_key` itself can't be squatted on at all)**; and `touch_updated_at()` had a mutable `search_path` — fixed with `set search_path = ''` (harmless, since `now()` lives in `pg_catalog`, which is always implicitly searched regardless of `search_path`). "Leaked password protection," the Advisor's other standing suggestion, doesn't apply here — Miziki signs in with emailed one-time codes, never passwords.

## Refactor rules (modularization in progress)

1. **No behavior changes in refactor PRs.** A move is a move. Fixes and features go in separate PRs.
2. **One extraction per branch and PR**, small enough to review as a diff.
3. **Deployed URL must not change:** it stays `https://esang-mao.github.io/Miziki/miziki.html`, because the home-screen app is saved to that URL. The build must keep base path `/Miziki/` and output a file named `miziki.html`.
4. Prefer ES modules with explicit imports and exports over new globals. When a module needs `S`, import a shared `state` module rather than reaching for `window`.
5. Keep the section comments and spec references (e.g. "CREDITS spec §2") when moving code. They are the project's design history.
6. After every extraction, run the build, then the device checklist below.
7. **An importing module cannot reassign an exported `let`.** Live bindings are read-only on the import side — only the module that declared the `let` can assign to it. `const` exports are fine to mutate *properties* of (e.g. `S.tracks = …` on the exported `S` object), since that isn't reassigning the binding itself; the rule only bites for a top-level `let` whose value gets replaced wholesale. If code in one module needs to reassign a top-level `let` defined in another, either fold that value into `S` (so it's a property write, not a rebinding) or export a small setter function from the module that owns it. Never work around this with `window`. There are roughly two dozen such top-level `let`s still in `main.js` (`socialAuth`, `GF`, `SEALSHEET`, `saveTimer`, and others) — deal with one only once an extraction actually moves code across the boundary it crosses, not preemptively.
8. **An extracted module may import from `main.js` only as a temporary step**, and only use those imports inside functions, never at module top level — the same shape as the circular imports from step 2c. Each later extraction should move those targets out of `main.js` too, so the number of imports from `main.js` keeps shrinking rather than growing. **As of step 7e, zero modules import from `main.js` anywhere in `src/`** (`grep -rn "from '.*main.js'" src/` outside `main.js` itself returns nothing) — the last five (`player/transport.js`'s `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`, `library/sealed.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, `crate/view.js`'s `openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`, `shell/routes.js`'s `GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`, and `shell/handoff.js`'s `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) all moved to real imports from the new `src/gatefold/` modules in that step, once the gatefold and sealed-record sheet existed for them to import from directly instead of circularly. This doesn't retire the rule itself — a future extraction could still need to reach back into whatever's left in `main.js` (the persistence/launch-intro-gate glue and the `wiring` section, step 7f's own territory) — but there is currently nothing on this list.

   `src/player/sleep-timer.js`, `src/player/scrub.js`, `src/ui/path-note.js`, `src/library/duplicate-scan.js`, `src/history/sessions.js`, `src/history/played.js`, `src/history/clear.js`, `src/history/achievements.js`, `src/record-art/metals.js`, `src/record-art/looks.js`, `src/audio/engine.js`, `src/player/controls.js`, `src/audio/mode.js`, `src/share/crate-card.js`, `src/profile/badges.js`, `src/profile/storefront.js`, `src/profile/settings.js`, `src/profile/screens.js`, `src/social/screens/friend-store.js`, `src/social/screens/home.js`, `src/social/demo.js`, `src/sundown/location.js`, `src/player/background.js`, `src/player/clock.js`, `src/player/queue.js`, `src/util/motion.js`, `src/shell/player-overlay.js`/`src/shell/start-sequence.js`/`src/shell/put-away.js`, `src/library/thumbs.js`, `src/shell/mini-player.js`, `src/shell/header.js`, `src/storage/meta.js`, `src/crate/geometry.js`/`src/crate/tiers.js`/`src/crate/model.js`/`src/launch/intro.js`, `src/library/import.js`, `src/player/transport.js`, `src/library/sealed.js`, `src/crate/view.js`, `src/shell/routes.js`, `src/shell/handoff.js` and `src/gatefold/view.js`/`src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` import nothing from `main.js` — step 3c moved `sleep-timer.js`/`scrub.js`/`path-note.js`'s last remaining `main.js`-sourced names (`drawTime`/`setPathNote`) into modules of their own, `duplicate-scan.js` never needed any (step 5d) — it reaches `deleteTracks` as a real import from `delete.js` instead — and `sessions.js` lost its last two (`applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs`) in step 6b, once `record-art/looks.js`/`library/thumbs.js` existed for it to import them from directly instead; `achievements.js`, `metals.js` and `looks.js` never needed any (step 6b) — `looks.js` does have a real circular import with `sessions.js` itself (for `persistAlbumLookPref`), the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, which is a peer import, not a `main.js` one; `storefront.js` and `settings.js` do have real circular imports with `profile/screens.js` (a 3-way cluster, the same peer-import shape, just three modules), and `friend-store.js`/`home.js` have a real circular pair of their own (for `feedEventText`/`openFriendStore`) — none of this is a `main.js` import. Since step 7a, `location.js` and `background.js` lost their last `main.js` name (`queueSave`, now a real import from `storage/meta.js`) — both do have real circular pairs with `meta.js` itself (`meta.js` imports `applyVolume` back from `location.js`, and `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` back from `background.js`, both for `applyPrefs()`), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, not a `main.js` import. Since step 7d, `library/import.js` lost its last `main.js` name (`renderTracks`, now a real import from `library/view.js`) — a real, one-directional import (`view.js` doesn't import anything back from `import.js`), so not a circular pair; `transport.js`'s own `renderTracks` name moved the same way, shrinking its list to just `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`. `library/view.js` itself never needed any `main.js` import — it does have real circular pairs with six sibling modules (`library/credits.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`) and `crate/view.js`, the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, just repeated several times (see `view.js`'s own repo-layout entry above for the full list). Since step 7e, the last five modules on rule 8's list all moved to real imports from the new `src/gatefold/view.js`/`src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`: `player/transport.js` (`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`) and `library/sealed.js` (`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`) each pick up a real circular pair with `gatefold/` as a result (`gatefold/view.js` imports `play` back from `transport.js`; `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` imports `buildSealOverlay` back from `library/sealed.js`) — the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. `crate/view.js` (`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`), `shell/routes.js` (`GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) and `shell/handoff.js` (`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) each become real, one-directional imports instead — none of the `gatefold/` modules imports anything back from any of these three, so none of them are circular pairs. `gatefold/view.js` and `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` themselves never needed any `main.js` import — `gatefold/view.js` has the two real circular pairs just described plus a third, with `shell/handoff.js` (for `runGatefoldHandoff`; `handoff.js` imports `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back, per the above); `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` has the pair with `library/sealed.js` above plus a real, one-directional import from `gatefold/view.js` itself (`GF`/`buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices` — `view.js` doesn't import anything back from `sealed-sheet.js`, so not a circular pair). **As of this step, zero modules anywhere in `src/` import from `main.js`** — see refactor rule 8 above.

   A future cleanup, noted here rather than done now (step 3b): transport's calls back into `main.js` are direct calls, not events or callbacks, so the order things happen in is exactly what it was before the move. Replacing them with an event/callback layer would change that order and risk breaking the iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9 below), so it's left as direct, temporary circular imports until the functions on the other end are themselves extracted. The same applies to `clock.js`'s circular import.
9. **The iOS audio-gesture rules are load-bearing and must survive every move untouched.** `play()`'s first call is `bgPrime()`, before any `await` — iOS only allows audio to start inside the original tap, so anything that delays past the first microtask boundary breaks it. When moving or refactoring playback code: never add, remove or reorder an `await`; never make a synchronous function `async` or the reverse; keep the `S.gen` generation counter and `onended` handler logic exactly as they are (they're how a stale source is kept from advancing the queue); keep the encoder delay/padding handling in `startSource` (PLAYBACK spec §1) unchanged.
10. **Every extraction PR must pass `npm run lint` with zero warnings** (step 6a introduced the check at zero *errors*; step 7g tightened it to zero *warnings* too — see that step's own changelog entry for why and how). The rule set is small on purpose (`no-undef` and a handful of other core-ESLint safety checks, no style rules — see the Step 6a changelog entry) — it exists to catch the `idOf` class of bug (step 5d: an import dropped from `main.js` while a function still used it; a missing import inside an ES module doesn't fail the build, it only throws once that line runs) before merge, not to enforce a coding style. `npm run lint` is `eslint . --max-warnings=0`; both CI pipelines run it ahead of `npm run test:unit`, so any lint finding — error or warning — blocks a deploy exactly like a failing test. An unused catch-clause binding, var, or argument that's deliberately unused (the "bad input — leave whatever was already there" pattern, by far the most common shape) is named with a leading underscore (`_e`, `_err`, `_mode`, …) rather than left bare — `no-unused-vars`'s `argsIgnorePattern`/`varsIgnorePattern`/`caughtErrorsIgnorePattern` all match `^_`, so this silences the warning without actually needing the variable. A genuinely dead import or declaration (nothing left that reads it, deliberate or not) gets removed outright instead of prefixed — prefixing would just be hiding dead code, not explaining it.

## Device checklist (run on iPhone, from the home-screen app, after each change)

- [ ] App opens; the launch intro plays (and is skipped when turned off)
- [ ] Existing library is still there after the update (IndexedDB intact)
- [ ] Add songs and add a folder; FLAC, WAV, ALAC and MP3 play
- [ ] Pure and Vinyl switch works; character sliders audibly change Vinyl
- [ ] Sundown auto rate and the preview slider; manual rate
- [ ] Sleeve pull, mini-player to full-player morph, spin-to-scrub, scratching with Scratch on/off
- [ ] Crate view, gatefold, sealed records
- [ ] Queue: play next, add, reorder, shuffle and repeat
- [ ] Edit track info and revert; remove from library
- [ ] Background playback plus lock-screen controls with the screen locked
- [ ] Sleep timer
- [ ] Share image: each style and format saves
- [ ] With social config empty, social screens show the calm "not set up" state

## Commands

- `npm install` — install Vite, `fake-indexeddb` (step 4a), `eslint`/`globals` (step 6a) and `@playwright/test` (NV1) — all dev dependencies, test/lint/perf-only, not part of the built app.
- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server at `http://localhost:5173/Miziki/` (note the `/Miziki/` — `base` is mounted in dev too, so `http://localhost:5173/` alone 404s).
- `npm run build` — builds to `dist/miziki.html` plus `dist/assets/` (bundled, hashed CSS and, since step 2a, the bundled `main.js`) and `dist/src/miziki-social.js` (copied verbatim, see `vite.config.js`). `dist/` is gitignored.
- `npm run preview` — serves the built `dist/` at `http://localhost:4173/Miziki/`, closest to what GitHub Pages actually serves.
- `npm run lint` — runs ESLint (`eslint.config.js`, step 6a) over the whole repo. A small, focused rule set — `no-undef` plus a handful of other core-ESLint safety checks (see refactor rule 10 above) — not a style linter; no formatting rules. Zero errors required; `no-unused-vars` warnings don't block. Both CI pipelines run this before `npm run test:unit`, so a lint error blocks a deploy exactly like a failing test.
- `npm test` — runs `tests/client.test.js` (needs a local Postgres; see below).
- `npm run test:unit` — runs `tests/unit/*.test.js` with Node's built-in `node:test`, under `--experimental-test-module-mocks` (needed by `engine.test.js`'s `mock.module()`; requires Node 22, see "Node version" below). No Postgres, no extra dependency. Both CI pipelines (`deploy.yml` and the Netlify build) run this before building, so a failing unit test blocks both deploys.
- `npm run perf` — runs `tests/perf/nav.perf.js` (NV1, `docs/11_SMOOTH_NAVIGATION.md`'s own navigation-performance plan; extended RF5, `docs/12_REFINE.md`, point 4): builds `dist/`, serves it locally, seeds a synthetic 2,000-track/180-album library straight into IndexedDB (no real audio — `tests/perf/lib/synthetic-library.js`), then drives a real Chromium under 4x CPU throttling (Chrome DevTools' own "4x slowdown" preset) to measure, each over **15 repetitions reported as median/p95**: tab-switch paint time, long tasks during a library scroll, sheet/gatefold open-close time, dropped frames on the Sundown tab, and `restoreLibrary()`'s own reload-to-restored wall-clock time at 2,000 tracks — plus one non-timing row, the backup doc's own byte size at the same library size. Writes `docs/perf/BASELINE.md`. **Not run in CI** — timings vary by machine; run it by hand, and pass `--out <path>` to avoid overwriting the checked-in baseline when a later NV PR re-runs it for its own before/after table.

Social client tests need a local Postgres. See the header of `tests/client.test.js` for the connection defaults (`PGHOST=/var/tmp/pgmz`, `PGPORT=5544`, database `mz`).

## Deploy previews (Netlify)

Every PR gets a Netlify deploy preview, separate from the real GitHub Pages
deploy — **GitHub Pages stays on `/Miziki/` and is untouched by this.**

- `netlify.toml` runs `npm run build` with `MIZIKI_BASE=/` (Netlify previews
  live at their own root domain, not under a `/Miziki/` subpath the way the
  GitHub Pages project site does) and `NODE_VERSION=20`, publishes `dist`,
  and rewrites `/` to `/miziki.html` (status 200, not a redirect — the URL
  bar stays at the preview root) so the preview link opens the app
  directly instead of 404ing on an index page that doesn't exist.
- To test the same build locally: `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, then
  `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run preview` (the preview server also reads `base`
  from the build, so it has to be set the same way or it'll try to serve
  from `/Miziki/` again) — then open `http://localhost:4173/miziki.html`.
- `MIZIKI_BASE` only changes `base` in `vite.config.js`; nothing else
  about the build differs between the two targets.
- A Netlify preview is a different origin from GitHub Pages, so its
  IndexedDB starts empty: an existing library doesn't show up, and prefs
  (including **Background playback**) default off. If lock-screen
  playback stops on a fresh preview, that's this, not a regression —
  toggle Background playback on for that origin too.
- **`miziki.netlify.app` (the production domain) no longer updates, on
  purpose.** Netlify builds both a production deploy (whatever's pushed to
  the site's production branch, `main`) and a deploy preview for every PR,
  as two separate billed builds — but nothing ever links to or uses
  `miziki.netlify.app`; the real app is GitHub Pages. Paying for a
  production rebuild on every push to `main`, on top of the PR previews
  that are the only thing this Netlify site is actually for, is what ran
  the account's build credits out before. `netlify.toml`'s `[build]` table
  now has `ignore = "[ \"$BRANCH\" = \"main\" ]"`: Netlify's `ignore` setting
  runs that command before starting a build and, per Netlify's own docs,
  "skips the build, returning early from the build process" if it exits
  `0` — which happens, per that same bash test, only when the branch being
  built is `main`. Per Netlify's billing FAQ, build-minute counting starts
  when dependencies begin loading, which is after this check, so a skipped
  build shouldn't be billed — this is also the documented, standard way
  (e.g. in monorepo setups) to avoid paying for builds Netlify would
  otherwise run unnecessarily, not a workaround. A PR's deploy preview
  builds its own head branch, never `main`,
  so `$BRANCH` there is the PR branch name, the check is false, and the
  preview builds exactly as before. If `miziki.netlify.app` itself is ever
  needed again (e.g. to compare against a GitHub Pages regression), it's
  one `enabled = false` removal away, or a manual "Trigger deploy" from the
  Netlify dashboard still works regardless of this setting.


### Node version

Node **22**. Set in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (`node-version`), `netlify.toml` (`NODE_VERSION`), and `package.json`'s `engines` field. `npm run test:unit` also needs 22 for `--experimental-test-module-mocks` (see Commands above). See `docs/CHANGELOG.md` for when and why it was bumped from 20.

## Changelog

The full step-by-step history of every refactor and feature PR in this project — what changed, why, and how each step was verified — lives in `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, not here. This file (CLAUDE.md) is kept to durable conventions, current architecture, and commands so it stays small enough to load in full; read the changelog when you need the reasoning behind a past decision or a detailed account of what a specific step actually did. (Within that file, phrases like "the refactor rules above" or "this file's own" rule still refer to this file, CLAUDE.md, unless the sentence is clearly pointing at another changelog entry.)
